The test keeps its inputs and meaning. |

## M1-38 Device approval and pairing

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-38/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-38-identity-device-approval` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Migration version 18 is the next free one on the base. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-38/2 | 2026-10-14 | A wrong verification code answers `200` with the device in state `revoked` (`revoke_reason` `verification_failed`), audited as allow `device.approve` with `result: rejected_wrong_code`. See [08 section 5.2](spec/08-identity.md). | The request had a committed effect (the device and its sessions are revoked); an error status would claim nothing changed, and the approving person must see that the device is gone. |
| M1-38/3 | 2026-10-14 | The verification code reaches the pending device through the `TokenResponse` of the login that leaves it pending and through `getDevice` of its own id. `listMyDevices` from a pending device stays `403 device_pending`, because authz narrows `device.list` of a pending device to the resource of the device itself (M1-28, 05 section 2.5); the use case still sets the code on the own entry, so the description of `Device.verification_code` holds once a pending device may list. Other devices and owners never get it. | The authz rule is normative and older; the device needs only one way to read its code. |
| M1-38/4 | 2026-10-14 | Pairing codes: 8 Crockford characters from one `crypto/rand` byte each (low five bits); the insert uses `ON CONFLICT (pairing_code_hash) WHERE status = 'requested' DO NOTHING`, and a collision draws a new code, at most 5 times (`pairingCodeAttempts`), then `500`. | 40 bits against few open pairings make a collision rare; several in a row mean a broken random source, which must not loop. The partial unique index only covers requested pairings, so a closed pairing frees its code. |
| M1-38/5 | 2026-10-14 | `completePairing` with a proof of another key rejects a `requested` pairing (committed, then `401 invalid_dpop_proof`); an `approved` pairing answers the same `401` and stays approved. | The state machine of the brief has no `approved -> rejected`; the device exists for the approving person, only its own key can collect it, and the collection window ends it otherwise (`pairing_expired`). |
| M1-38/6 | 2026-10-14 | `approveDevice` and `approvePairing` write in a unit of work declared as the rights change `device.approve` of the device's person (the caller for a pairing), the expiries as `device.expire`; like `addOwner`, a first read and `authorize` run before that unit, and the unit locks and checks the row again. An unknown pairing code ends the step-up and writes its deny entry in a committed unit without rights change. | 05 marks both actions as rights changes; the rights hook must lock `permission_version` before any domain row, and a refused or unknown request must not raise the rights version. The deny entry belongs to the committed effect (the ended step-up), so it is recorded in the unit, not aggregated after a rollback. |
| M1-38/7 | 2026-10-14 | An expiry found on access (an expired pending device at `approveDevice`, an expired pairing at `completePairing`) is written in its own unit of work with the anonymous principal, so it is audited and evented like the job: the system action `device.expire` without actor, `revoked_by` NULL. The request still answers `409 invalid_state_transition` or `410 gone`. | The brief applies the expiry "lazily on access and by the cleanup job" and audits it as a system action with actor NULL; the caller did not decide the revocation. |
| M1-38/8 | 2026-10-14 | `devices.created_at` (and `approved_at` of an active device) takes the clock of the login or pairing request; `Devices.Register` keeps the database time. A pending device past `auth.device_pending_ttl` counts as revoked in the Authenticator (`401 session_revoked`, `device_inactive`), in `refreshToken` and as a known device of a later login (`409`). | The pending window counts from the request clock the use cases compare with; correctness must not wait for the job (brief "Correctness never depends on the job"). |
| M1-38/9 | 2026-10-14 | Cleanup job case: `identity.auth_cleanup` existed (M1-35); `AuthCleanup.Run` now also calls `ExpirePendingDevices` and `ExpirePairings` of the login service, each device or pairing in its own unit of work, read in batches of 200. Expired pairings are marked `expired`, not deleted. | One job per 02 section 12.3; separate units keep one failing row from blocking the rest, and deleting old transient rows belongs to the retention job, which grants its delete. |
| M1-38/10 | 2026-10-14 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`), fixed with tests: the units that must commit although the request answers an error run on `txn.Detach` (`committed` in `app/approval.go`): the end of the step-up and its deny entry after an unknown pairing code, the expiry of a pending device found by `approveDevice`, and the unit of `completePairing` with its rejection and expiry. The approval tests send an `Idempotency-Key` like a client (`approveDevice` and `approvePairing` are keyed), and the wrong-code case runs once with and once without the key. | With the key the Idempotency-Key stage runs the use case inside its own unit of work and rolls back every error answer, so a wrong pairing code ended neither the step-up nor left a deny entry, and a guess cost no new biometric confirmation (reproduced by the red team, `TestPairingWrongCodeEndsStepUp` red before the fix). |
| M1-38/11 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: the migration keeps the number 00018 of the integration branch (INT-W14/2), its comments name the English chapter files (new checksum recorded with `make migrations-lock`), and the code comments, the configuration test and these rulings name the English chapter files. None of the other integration fixes of `int/m1-wave14` touches this ticket alone: they join it with M1-40, M1-41, M1-43, M1-45, M1-47, M1-48 and M1-51, and `api/rest/rest.gen.go` stays below `repo.max_file_kb` with this ticket (the split of INT-W14/4 comes with M1-41). | `main` already holds M1-37 (`00016`) and M1-46 (`00017`), as the integration branch did, so the number follows without a gap; the specification is English on main. No behaviour changes. |

## M1-40 Passkeys

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-40/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-40-identity-passkeys` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-36/1 and M1-37/1). |
| M1-40/2 | 2026-10-14 | The RP display name is the RP ID. | The product name lives in the instance settings (M1-46); the display name is only a label, and a fixed value keeps the options independent of a setting that changes. |
| M1-40/3 | 2026-10-14 | Sign counter: when the stored and the received counter are both above zero and the received one is not greater, the ceremony continues, the higher value is kept and one audit entry is written with the action of the ceremony (`auth.login.complete` or `auth.step_up`), decision `allow`, resource `passkey` and details `{passkey_id, sign_count_regression: true, stored, received}`. Counters that stay zero (synced passkeys) are no regression. The passkey row is locked before the rule is applied, so two parallel uses keep the higher value. | [03 `passkey_credentials`](spec/03-data-model.md), [08 section 2.5](spec/08-identity.md) and [R-DM-31](spec/03-data-model.md): a regression is reported, not refused, because synced and cloned authenticators cannot be told apart. |
| M1-40/4 | 2026-10-14 | The decoy credential ID for a login name without passkeys is the first 32 bytes of `HMAC-SHA-256(token pepper, "passkey-decoy/v1" \|\| 0x00 \|\| lower case name)`. Residual risk: for a known name the number of real passkeys is visible, and a decoy and a real list differ in their transports. | [08 section 2.5](spec/08-identity.md): the answer must not reveal accounts; the context string separates this use of the pepper from codes and tokens, and the HMAC keeps the decoy stable for a name. |
| M1-40/5 | 2026-10-14 | When `auth.webauthn_rp_id` is empty and the host of `server.public_url` is an IP address, or when both passkey keys are empty and `server.public_url` is `http` on a host other than `localhost`, or when `auth.webauthn_rp_id` is empty and the host of `server.public_url` is no valid RP ID (a single label such as `bothy`), the instance has no passkeys: the start does not fail, the passkey operations answer `403 feature_disabled` (reason `passkeys_disabled`) and `createStepUpChallenge` has no `passkey_options`. An explicit RP ID or origin that breaks the rules of 08 section 2.5 fails the start (cross-check `webauthn_relying_party`). | An IP address is never a valid RP ID (WebAuthn Level 3, "RP ID"), and an http origin outside localhost is no secure context, so browsers offer no passkeys there; installations reached by IP or plain http (tests, a private network) must keep starting; passkeys serve the web console and instances with a domain (08 section 6). Spec 08 section 2.5 names the rule. |
| M1-40/6 | 2026-10-14 | Every completion that opened an issued ceremony row and then failed (refused response, rejected device, server failure) sets the row to `failed` in an own short unit of work after the rollback. Only a registration whose challenge belongs to another session leaves it untouched. | A row must work once (03 `auth_challenges`, redemption); a rollback alone would leave it `issued`, and the same assertion could be sent again until `auth.login_flow_ttl` ends. |
| M1-40/7 | 2026-10-14 | A refused registration response (unknown, used, expired or foreign challenge, wrong challenge, rejected attestation, algorithm outside ES256, EdDSA and RS256) answers `400 validation_failed` with pointer `/credential` and writes no deny entry; a duplicate credential ID answers `409 conflict` (pointer `/credential`). | The brief fixes `400` for registration; a refused response is a bad request, not a denial of [07 section 4.1](spec/07-permissions.md), whose closed list of reasons has none for it. |
| M1-40/8 | 2026-10-14 | `completeStepUpWithPasskey` counts like a wrong signature (`failed`, toward `auth.step_up_fail_limit`) every response that does not confirm the challenge: another challenge in `clientDataJSON`, a passkey of another person, a revoked or unknown credential, a refused assertion, and a passkey completion of a challenge that was issued without `passkey_options`. An unknown, foreign, finished or expired challenge does not count, as in M1-35. | [08 section 6.2](spec/08-identity.md): a wrong confirmation counts, a challenge the caller could not have used does not. |
| M1-40/9 | 2026-10-14 | `passkey.register` is authorized at begin and again at complete; the conditional step-up is decided in `authz` through `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` and `PrincipalSource.HasActivePasskey`, so a person who registered the first passkey between begin and complete needs a step-up at complete. | [07 section 4.2 step 8](spec/07-permissions.md): rights logic stays in `authz` (B7); the second check closes the window between the two calls. |
| M1-40/10 | 2026-10-14 | An active passkey counts as a remaining sign-in method when an identity is unlinked (`PasskeySource`) only while the person keeps an active identity at the enabled `local` provider other than the one being unlinked; the credential overview lists the active passkeys oldest first. | [08 section 3.3](spec/08-identity.md) names the passkey as a way in, but a passkey logs in only with a local identity (08 section 2.5); counting it without one would let a person unlink the last usable identity (review finding). |
| M1-40/11 | 2026-10-14 | `user.name` of the creation options is the subject of the person's local identity, else the handle; `user.id` is the 16 bytes of the principal ID; a person who is not `active` gets no options (`401` for an enrollment, `400` for a registration). | Brief M1-40 "Enrollment with passkey"; the user handle carries no personal data. |
| M1-40/12 | 2026-10-14 | go-webauthn is pinned at `v0.18.2` (the Go proxy serves the tag); it is the only package that imports the library (`identity/adapters/webauthn`, vendor group `webauthn` in the module register). The adapter rebuilds the session data from the checked challenge, the RP ID of the configuration and required user verification; the allow list of the stored `webauthn_session.v1` and the person are enforced by the use case through the credential lookup. | Brief M1-40 fixes the port with JSON in and out and no ceremony state in the adapter. |
| M1-40/13 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: the migration is `00019_identity_passkey_credentials.sql` as in the integration branch (INT-W14/2; M1-40/1 was written with the number 18 of its base), its comments, the code comments and these rulings name the English chapter files (new checksum recorded with `make migrations-lock`). `auth.login_flow_ttl` and `EnrollmentCodes.MatchID` (over the query `LockEnrollmentCode`) come with this ticket, in the form INT-W14/3 kept; M1-41 reuses them. `go.mod` keeps `kin-openapi` at the newer version of main and adds go-webauthn `v0.18.2`. The split of the REST package (INT-W14/4) comes with this ticket, not with M1-41. | `main` holds M1-38 with `00018`, so `00019` follows without a gap. On main `api/rest/rest.gen.go` was already larger than on the integration branch (English descriptions of the contract), and with the operations of this ticket alone it reached 1030 KiB, above `repo.max_file_kb`; the hook `check-added-large-files` would refuse the commit. No behaviour changes. |

## M1-41 OIDC login with PKCE

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-41/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e50`, branch `feat/M1-41-identity-oidc` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it (the launcher's task overrides the brief's local merge). The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00018`; tickets of the same wave that also take `00018` are renumbered at integration. | Instructions of the launcher; migration numbers are per base (INT-W13/2). |
| M1-41/2 | 2026-10-14 | The callback reads the flow without a lock and talks to the provider outside any transaction; its only writes are conditional on the status (`IssueLoginCode` from `started`, `FailLoginFlow` from `started` or `code_issued`). Of two callbacks with one state at most one issues a code; the other gets `400` like a used state. | 02 section 8.5 forbids outgoing calls inside a transaction; a row lock would have to be held across the token request. |
| M1-41/3 | 2026-10-14 | `flow_expired` is the answer of a found flow whose `expires_at` passes while the callback runs (after the token request); a flow already expired when the callback arrives is `400` without redirect, as 08 section 2.2 step 6 says for an expired state. | 08 names both; this keeps both meaningful. |
| M1-41/4 | 2026-10-14 | The discovery document of an issuer with `{tenantid}` is read from the URL with `organizations` in place of the placeholder; its `issuer` must equal the configured template exactly. The `iss` response parameter of a template issuer must name one of `allowed_tenant_ids`, and the ID token's `iss` must equal it. | Entra publishes the multi-tenant document under the alias whose `issuer` is the template; M1-36/5 refuses the alias as issuer, so the alias appears only in this URL. Written into 08 section 2.6. |
| M1-41/5 | 2026-10-14 | `external_identity.v1` gains `schema` and `preferred_username` (the handle of a provisioned person starts from its local part, which `display_name` alone cannot give); `auth_time` is stored as Unix seconds. | Brief rule "Provisioned handle" needs the claim at complete, and the callback stores only this document. Written into 08 section 2.2 step 6. |
| M1-41/6 | 2026-10-14 | Provider secret references are confined at resolution (`wire.ProviderSecrets`): only files below `<secrets.dir>/idp/` and variables `BOTHY_IDP_*`, never the value of any `secret_ref` key of the server schema; another reference makes the token request fail, so the callback answers `login_failed`. The write-time check of M1-36 stays syntax only. | Closes M1-36/14 with the minimum: the server's own secrets cannot leave as client secret; a write-time refusal would need the real secrets directory in the domain rules. |
| M1-41/7 | 2026-10-14 | `CompleteLogin` gets `AllowPending` (OIDC only): a provisioned person in status `pending` logs in again with a pending device and gets only the self information; `disabled` and `deleted` never log in. The allow entry of a provisioning login carries `provisioned: true`; no `user.updated` event is written for the provisioned person. | 08 R-ID-14 and 3.1 step 3; the other login paths keep refusing pending people. The event belongs to the owner's `enableUser`, which already emits it. |
| M1-41/8 | 2026-10-14 | A subject that is linked at the provider under another issuer (another tenant of a multi-tenant provider) is neither mapped nor linked again: complete answers the neutral `401` and fails the flow. An enrollment code that became unusable between begin and complete is the same `401`. | `(provider_id, subject)` is unique among active identities (03); mapping needs the issuer too (R-ID-02). |
| M1-41/9 | 2026-10-14 | Link mode: an unknown, expired or other person's `login_code` is `404 not_found`, a wrong verifier `401 unauthenticated` (flow failed), a subject linked to anyone `409 conflict` (flow failed); `identity.link` (audit always) writes an allow entry at begin (`step: begin`) and at complete (`step: complete`). | The brief fixes 404 for another actor and 409; the same 404 for an unknown code hides whether a code exists. |
| M1-41/10 | 2026-10-14 | The re-login rule counts from `created_at` of the session, for a child session from its parent; the refusal commits the revocation (`expired`, families revoked, `session.revoked`) like a reuse. A zero `auth.idp_recheck_interval` switches the rule off; the schema refuses values below `1m`, so only tests can set it. | The child inherits the parent's login; without the parent's time an extension session would outlive the interval. |
| M1-41/11 | 2026-10-14 | `kernel/jose`: RSA keys only through the new `ParseVerificationJWK` (2048 to 8192 bits, odd exponent below 2^31); `ParseJWK`, used for device keys and DPoP, still refuses RSA. `PS256` verifies with a salt of the hash length. The header member `x5t#S256` is written when signing a client assertion and ignored when parsing. ADR 0067 notes both. | Keeps the device profile unchanged and bounds the cost of a provider key. |
| M1-41/12 | 2026-10-14 | The adapter tests of client authentication, JWKS reload, cache TTL and private targets run in `adapters/oidc` against the fake provider with the real egress client; the HTTP tests repeat private targets through the chain. The ID token claims are verified by the use case over the parsed claims the adapter returns. | Brief "Scope" splits JWS (adapter) and claim rules (domain); the adapter tests need no database. |
| M1-41/13 | 2026-10-14 | The HTTP tests of M1-41 run as subtests named after the brief in four groups with one world each (`TestOIDC_Login`, `TestOIDC_IssParameter`, `TestOIDC_Entra`, `TestIdentityLink`), plus `TestOIDCEgress_PrivateTargetsRejected` with two worlds; cases that move the clock run last in their group, and the denial aggregator is flushed before each case. | One world per test made `identity/adapters/httpapi` exceed the default `go test` timeout of 10 minutes under `-race` (each world builds the request validator of the contract); the pattern is the one of M1-44 (`runTokenCases`). |
| M1-41/14 | 2026-10-14 | `httpservertest.New` takes the request validator from a process-wide cache per contract and operation list (`SharedValidator`, like `SharedResponseValidator`; on main it came first with PL/3). `cmd/bothy` pins the five new configuration keys in `TestConfigPrintShowsSourcePerKey`. | `identity/adapters/httpapi` exceeded the 10-minute default timeout of `go test` already on the base under load (the same failure stands in the reports of M1-37, M1-45, M1-46 and M2-24); building the validator per world was almost all of its run time. With the cache the package runs in a fraction of that time under `-race`, without a data race; the validator is read only and serves concurrent requests in production. A test helper outside the scope list of the brief, changed because the brief requires `make check` green and the same failure recurred (fix the class once). |
| M1-41/15 | 2026-10-14 | Test code only: `TestOwnerRace` (M1-31) also accepts `[204, 403 forbidden]` for the two crossing revocations, the outcome when the second request authorizes after the first has committed; the invariant "exactly one owner left" is checked unchanged. | The test failed with `[204 403]` under load in the suites of M1-44, M1-50 and this ticket; the serialised order is correct behaviour, so the flake class is fixed in the test, not in the code. |
| M1-41/16 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: the migration is `00020_identity_login_flows.sql` as in the integration branch (INT-W14/2; M1-41/1 was written with the number 18 of its base), its comments, the query comments, the code comments and these rulings name the English chapter files (new checksum recorded with `make migrations-lock`). `auth.login_flow_ttl`, `EnrollmentCodes.MatchID` and the query `LockEnrollmentCode` came with M1-40 (M1-40/13); this ticket reuses them and only widens their comments (INT-W14/3). The split of the REST package (INT-W14/4) is already on main and is regenerated, not merged. The constant `fieldCodeTaken` of INT-W14/10 comes with this ticket, because on main its handle conflict is the third use of the literal `taken` and goconst counts from three. `LoginCompletion.AllowPending` is added once here; M1-43 reuses it (INT-W14/5). The fix of INT-W14/6 to `TestOIDC_Login/TestIDPRecheckForcesRelogin` is part of this commit. | `main` holds M1-38 with `00018` and M1-40 with `00019`, so `00020` follows without a gap; the specification is English on main. No behaviour changes beyond the integration content. |
| M1-41/17 | 2026-10-17 | The two RS256 calls of `internal/kernel/jose` (`rsa.VerifyPKCS1v15` in `verifyRSA`, `rsa.SignPKCS1v15` in `RS256Signer.Sign`) carry `NOSONAR`: the SonarQube Cloud rule for a secure mode and padding scheme is about RSA encryption, and these lines make and check PKCS #1 v1.5 signatures. RS256 stays, because OpenID Connect providers sign ID tokens with it by default and the client assertion offers it next to PS256. | The rule reports a vulnerability and fails the security rating of the quality gate; a signature with PKCS #1 v1.5 padding is no encryption, and RFC 7518 section 3.3 defines RS256 with exactly this padding. |

## M1-43 Trusted proxy header login

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-43/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e50`, branch `feat/M1-43-identity-trusted-header` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-43/2 | 2026-10-14 | W4 is resolved as [PLAN section 7](PLAN.md) and 16 section 4.3 state it: a `trusted_header` provider without a loopback public listener does not stop the start. The driver stays off (`loginWithTrustedHeader` answers `404`), the readiness check `trusted_header_requires_loopback` fails while such a provider is enabled, and `identity_provider.manage` refuses to enable it with `409 conflict`, `reason: trusted_header_requires_loopback` and a hint naming `server.listen_addr`. `platform/config` has no start-time cross-check of the driver, so nothing was removed there. | The driver is a runtime setting of the owner; a mistake in it must fail closed without preventing the start (08 section 2.4 rule 1, ID-T08, SEC-T01). |
| M1-43/3 | 2026-10-14 | The loopback flag is not a field of `app.Config` but state of `app.TrustedHeaderDriver`: `internal/wire` sets it from `trustedheader.IsLoopbackListener` of the bound public listener after the listen and registers the readiness check then; tests set it with `Identity.SetPublicListenerLoopback`. It starts as false (fail closed); the role worker opens no public listener and never registers the check. | `config.Bind` refuses an exported field without a key, and the bound address exists only after the listen, which runs after the identity step. |
| M1-43/4 | 2026-10-14 | `kernel/reqmeta.Transport` (TCP peer from `RemoteAddr`, the request header, the route's action) is set by the authenticate stage of `platform/httpserver`; the header login handler and the per-request binding read it. Two files outside the brief's scope change for this: `kernel/reqmeta` (new file) and `platform/httpserver/stages.go` (three lines). | The generated strict handlers and the `actor.Authenticator` receive no `*http.Request`; the brief requires the raw peer and the configured headers in the use case and the binding check in the shared Authenticator. |
| M1-43/5 | 2026-10-14 | The binding check also runs in `refreshToken` (inside its unit of work, after the family key matched; the revocation commits like a reuse and the answer is `401 session_revoked`). | The brief says the check covers `refreshToken` "because the authenticator is shared", but `refreshToken` is anonymous and never reaches the Authenticator; rule 5 of 08 section 2.4 binds every later request. A proof of another key still never revokes (ID-T17). |
| M1-43/6 | 2026-10-14 | The binding reads the identity and the provider of a `trusted_header` session with two queries after the session lookup instead of joining them into `GetAuthState`. | Only sessions of this driver with the switch on pay the reads; the shared lookup and its sqlc code stay unchanged. |
| M1-43/7 | 2026-10-14 | The refusal of enabling the driver is audited as a deny of `identity_provider.manage` with the new reason `trusted_header_requires_loopback`, added to the closed list of 07 section 4.1 (row "no `authorize` result", like `malware_detected`) and to the reason lists of `kernel/auditing` and `authz/domain`. | The brief asks for an audit deny; a deny needs a reason of the closed list, and none of the existing ones describes the refusal. |
| M1-43/8 | 2026-10-14 | The handle of a provisioned person follows the rule of brief M1-41 (part of the login before `@`, lower case, only `a-z0-9._-`, starting with a letter or digit, cut to `principal.handle_max_chars`, `user` when nothing remains; a taken handle gets `-` and four random Crockford Base32 characters). M1-41 is not merged, so the rule lives in `domain/trustedheader` (`HandleFromLogin`, `HandleWithSuffix`) until both drivers share one helper. | Brief M1-43 Design notes, step 5. |
| M1-43/9 | 2026-10-14 | `CompleteLogin` gains `AllowPending` (a pending person logs in, 08 section 3.1 item 3 and R-ID-14) and `ProviderID` (recorded as `provider_id` in the allow entry). A known identity of a pending person logs in with the header too; disabled and deleted people never do. Several field lines of the login header are absent; a comma inside one value is part of the value. | Brief M1-43 step 6; 08 section 2.4 rule 6 speaks of several values, which HTTP represents as several field lines. |
| M1-43/10 | 2026-10-14 | When the binding fails at the Authenticator, the deny entry names the route's action from `reqmeta.Transport`; a caller without a route (the gateway before M1-64) records `auth.login.complete`. | The audit row needs an action code; M1-64 owns the gateway binding and its action. |
| M1-43/11 | 2026-10-14 | The integration tests of the driver run in five worlds: one each for ID-T08, SEC-T01 and the binding switch off, `TestTrustedHeaderLogin` (binding on, no provisioning) with the cases ID-T09, ID-T10, the missing header, a disabled person, several values, no mapping and the enrollment code as ordered subtests, and `TestTrustedHeaderProvisioning` with provisioning and `TestTrustedHeaderNeverLogged`. The test-first red runs were taken by mutation of the finished code (binding always bound, peer not checked, loopback ignored, missing header accepted), each turning its test red. | With one world per case the package `identity/adapters/httpapi` passed the default timeout of `go test` in `make test` on the loaded host (as M1-44/13); the code and the tests were written in one step, so the mutations show the tests catch the behaviour. |
| M1-43/12 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash, with `LoginCompletion.AllowPending`, `loginAttrs`, `ServiceDeps.Random` and the refresh order as INT-W14/5 states them (`AllowPending` itself came with M1-41). Code comments and the chapter key of `auth.trusted_header_bind_requests` name `08-identity.md`; the regenerated `config-schema.json` carries the key. The language gate (`tools/docs/language_check.py`) allows the non-ASCII test values `Röbin` and `ÄÖÜ` of this ticket and `Ünal` of M1-41 as words of `tools/docs/language-allow.txt`. | The gate reads every tracked file and also decodes escaped umlauts, so these RFC 2047 and handle test vectors failed `make docs`; one allow list entry per value keeps the vectors unchanged and fixes the class in one place. |

## M1-48 Groups and grants over the API

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-48/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave12` at `da08f58`, branch `feat/M1-48-authz-api` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-44/1). |
| M1-48/2 | 2026-10-13 | Non-owners see grants only inside their `manage` scope; filters narrow it and never widen it. The scope (spaces and channels with effective `manage`, a channel also through its space or the instance) comes from the rights snapshot per request (`Authorizer.AdminScope`) and is applied in the query. | Brief ruling 1; 07 section 2.6 item 6. |
| M1-48/3 | 2026-10-13 | A grant or group the caller may not read is `404` for every operation on it, with the same status, body and headers as a random id (AZ-P4). For grants this changes the visibility rule of M1-28: a grant on a space or channel is visible to owners and to holders of `manage` there, no longer to everyone who reads the target. | Brief ruling 2 and the table "Visibility and authorization per operation"; reading a target does not make its administration data readable. |
| M1-48/4 | 2026-10-13 | Revoking an already revoked grant succeeds with the stored grant, without change, event or rights version, and is audited with `no_change` (06 section 2 "No effect"). Every revocation needs a reason that is not blank after trimming; 07 section 9.5 says so. | Brief ruling 3; 03 R-DM-09 and 07 9.5. |
| M1-48/5 | 2026-10-13 | Deleting a group revokes its live grants with the reason `group_deleted` (revoked by the caller) in one rights change and keeps the memberships as history; `Admin.DeleteGroup` takes no reason. | Brief ruling 4; `deleteGroup` has no body. |
| M1-48/6 | 2026-10-13 | `member_count` and the member list show memberships in effect at the request: not removed, not expired (at `expires_at` already over). In `group.changed` of a deletion the count is that of the memberships kept as history. | Brief ruling 5; 07 section 2.5. |
| M1-48/7 | 2026-10-13 | The delegation limits (`CheckCreate`, `CheckRevoke`) run in the requirement step of `domain.Decide` (`decideDelegation`), after the requirement and before "view as" and step-up; the guard step keeps `agent.invoke` and `token.create`. | Brief scope 3 and 07 4.2 "Requirement before step-up": a caller beyond the limits must not be asked for a step-up. |
| M1-48/8 | 2026-10-13 | `getGrant` and the instance filter of `listGrants` check an instance grant as `grant.read` on the caller as resource `principal`, whose requirement is `owner`; for `getGrant` a `forbidden` becomes `404`. A host grant is `404` until M2-39. `listGrants` without a target filter authorizes through `AdminScope` only: a non-owner without `manage` gets an empty page, not `403`. | 05 lists `grant.read` on `space`, `channel`, `host` and `principal` only; the brief makes instance grants owner only and lets a `principal` filter narrow instead of refuse. |
| M1-48/9 | 2026-10-13 | `createGroup` with `source = oidc_claim` checks that `provider_id` names an existing provider through the foreign key `groups_provider_id_fkey` (`400`, `/provider_id`, `invalid`), not its kind. A local group with `provider_id` or `external_ref` is `400` with code `forbidden`; a taken claim is `409 conflict` on `/external_ref`. | authz must not read identity tables and has no port for providers; the claim sync of M1-42 only reads groups of OIDC providers, so a group bound to another kind stays empty and harmless. |
| M1-48/10 | 2026-10-13 | `updateGroup` without `name` and `description` is `400 validation_failed` on `/name` with code `required`; names are trimmed on create and update. | The brief requires at least one of them; the schema `GroupUpdate` cannot say so. |
| M1-48/11 | 2026-10-13 | Field codes of the new checks: `in_past` (an expiry that is not after the request; before `not_after_creation`), `unknown_subject`, `unknown_user`, `duplicate` (a capability listed twice), `required` and `forbidden` (a missing or forbidden id of a pair, in the body or as `#query/<name>` in `listGrants`). The audit details of `grant.create` name a group subject as `subject_id`, like every other subject. | The brief's codes and audit details. |
| M1-48/12 | 2026-10-13 | Files outside the brief's scope that the ticket had to change: `internal/wire` (construction, `Handlers.Access`, the configuration keys `group.name_max_chars` and `group.description_max_chars` with their tests), `api/routes/real_test.go` (the enabled table), `cmd/bothy/config_test.go`, `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml` (the rule `group.` is stale once the keys are registered) and the generated configuration reference. | Enabling an operation needs its handler in the start (api/README "Enable an operation (handler ticket)"); a registered key must leave the planned list. |
| M1-48/13 | 2026-10-13 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`, two Majors), fixed with a test each: (1) `createGroup` and `createGrant` check the shape before `authorize` without the caller's id (`domain.CheckGrantShape`, `CheckClaim`, `groupShape`) and build the row after it, so an anonymous call is `401`, not `500` (`TestAnonymousWritesAre401`); (2) behind the Idempotency-Key stage a raced revocation, deletion or removal records the call without effect inside the late rights change instead of returning `errRaced`, because a failed late rights change fails the whole unit (M1-31/2); that attempt raises `permission_version` without a difference, as M1-31/9 accepts for `enableUser` (`TestConcurrentKeyedRevocations`). Minors fixed: every operation with an id runs `authorize` before it answers a missing resource, the class of M1-31/16 (`TestNoExistenceOracleBeforeVisibility`); the reason of a revocation is checked like the reason of a grant (`domain.CheckReason`); deleting a group revokes only grants in effect, an expired one is reported as expired by the fold of the same change. Not changed: the sentence of 07 9.5 stays English (instruction of the launcher: everything English, M1-48/1); `listGroups` under "view as" and the receive action `grant.read` of `grant.changed` for resource `grant` have no caller before M1-49 and M1-65; until M1-52 and M1-53 wire a structure source, targeted grant reads of spaces and channels answer `404` in production, as every structure lookup does; `AdminScope` reads all spaces and channels per list request (bounded by the size of the instance, like `Scope`). | Findings of the red team in the scope of this ticket. |
| M1-48/14 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash, with the test world of INT-W14/7 without the identity keys of M1-45, which comes after this ticket on main. Code comments, the chapter keys of `group.name_max_chars` and `group.description_max_chars` and the rulings name the English chapter files and headings (`03-data-model.md` "Configuration values of this chapter", 06 "No effect", 07 4.2 "Requirement before step-up", api/README "Enable an operation (handler ticket)"); the sentence of 07 section 9.5 replaces the English sentence at the same place. The module README and the server changelog name the reasons `step_up_not_possible` and `claim_managed_group` after their registered codes (`403 forbidden`, `409 conflict`), as the problem code check of main requires. The main-only case `TestVisibleByResourceKind` of M1-28 expects the visibility rule of M1-48/3: a grant on a space is hidden from a reader without `manage` and shown to a holder of `manage`. Generated files are regenerated, not merged. | `main` is English and does not hold M1-45 yet; the case was added on main after the integration base and still asserted the rule M1-48/3 replaces. No other behaviour changes. |

## M1-51 Agent records, backend policy and credential holder

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-51/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave12` at `da08f58`, branch `feat/M1-51-agents-records` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00016_agents.sql`. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-44/1). |
| M1-51/2 | 2026-10-13 | `adapter_kind` matches `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` (examples `hermes`, `code_runner`, `custom`); the use case checks it (`400 validation_failed` on `/adapter_kind`), the table has no check, because 03 lists none. There is no length key, so the request body limit bounds it. | Ruling the brief asks for: a lower-case identifier like a handle, without dots, so it can be an audit code and a metric label. |
| M1-51/3 | 2026-10-13 | Lock order of `setAllAgentsBillingMode`: `permission_version` first (rights hook, 03 section 6.3), then the `agents` rows in ascending `principal_id`, with `agent_ids` the listed rows (duplicates count once), without the list every personal row, disabled agents included. A first unit without rights change authorizes, checks the body and whether any agent differs from the target; when none does, it records `no_change` and the call ends without a rights version. A given but empty list switches nothing. | One order for every multi-row change prevents deadlocks; the emergency switch must also reach agents that are disabled. |
| M1-51/4 | 2026-10-13 | Disabling a disabled agent and enabling an active one answer `200` with the agent, without version, event or rights version, and audit `no_change: true`. The test line of the brief ("repeat gives `409`") is not followed. | The design notes, the acceptance criteria and 06 section 2 "No effect" say so; the test line contradicts all three. |
| M1-51/5 | 2026-10-13 | A personal token of an owner gets `404` on the mutations of an agent and `403` on the two operations on the instance (`createAgent`, `setAllAgentsBillingMode`), not `403` everywhere as the brief's table says. | SR5: the token has no owner role, so the agent is invisible to it (07 section 4.3 visibility before the requirement); authz decides, and invisible resources answer `404` like an unknown id (02 section 9.2). |
| M1-51/6 | 2026-10-13 | `TestBR01_CredentialHolderChangeIsChecked` changes the holder of a personal agent of the sole owner to another active person; the owner keeps `trigger` everywhere, so I1 fails. A world with a second owner cannot hold a personal agent at all (03 section 6.4), so the brief's setup is not reachable through the API; a holder change succeeds for a disabled agent or to the sole owner. | I1 without an owner exception (Ruling L22) makes the sole owner the only possible holder of an active personal agent. |
| M1-51/7 | 2026-10-13 | `TestCreateAgentHandleConflictCaseInsensitive` creates the agent `alex` next to the person `alex` (`409 conflict` on `/handle`); `Alex` is refused with `400` by the pattern of `AgentCreate.handle` before any database access. The unique index on `lower(handle)` holds the case-insensitive rule, as M1-26 tests it. | The API and the table accept lower-case handles only, so upper case can never reach the index. |
| M1-51/8 | 2026-10-13 | Disabling and enabling bump `agents.version` and append `agent.updated`, like every change of the record or of the display name. | `status` is part of the representation whose ETag is `version`; a client that holds the old version must see the new state. |
| M1-51/9 | 2026-10-13 | `listAgents` first authorizes `agent.read` in filter mode on the descriptor `{kind: agent}` without an id: it answers the liveness of the caller (`401` anonymous or disabled, `403` pending) and allows only an owner; `not_found` lets the list continue. Then candidates are read in id order in batches and each is filtered with `authorize(p, agent.read, agent)` in filter mode until one more than the page size is visible. | 07 section 7.4 rule 3; the descriptor check also proves the check of the route's action when the list is empty (02 "Proof of the check"). Filter mode keeps the per-item refusals out of the audit log. |
| M1-51/10 | 2026-10-13 | `presence.billing_mode_matches` is always present: `false` only when a reported mode exists and differs from `billing_mode`, otherwise `true`. `presence.rate_limit` stays absent until the heartbeat stores it (M2-20). | The brief's rule; no `null` (ADR 0053). |
| M1-51/11 | 2026-10-13 | `authz.AgentPolicySource` stays as declared: the status comes from `PrincipalSource`, the effective tool class is the maximum authz computes from `ToolClass` and `ReportedToolClass`, and `billing_modes_supported` is part of the agent representation (`Agents` port), not of the source, because no rule of authz reads it. `PersonalAgents` filters by the principal status through `identity.Directory`; the agents adapter never reads `principals`. | "Implement the interface as declared"; 03 principle 12. |
| M1-51/12 | 2026-10-13 | A credential holder must be an active person: an unknown id, an agent, an app or a person who is not active is `400 validation_failed` on `/credential_holder_id` (code `not_active_person`), checked again in the writing unit. `setCredentialHolder` on an organization agent is `409 conflict` with reason `billing_mode_not_personal`. | The brief; the holder's own subscription runs the agent (10 section 1). |
| M1-51/13 | 2026-10-13 | The description is a display text under SEC-IN-4 (no control or bidi characters, may be empty); the reason of every policy, holder, tool class, disable and enable call must not be blank (`400` on `/reason`), also for `ToolClassSet`, whose schema has no `minLength`. | One rule for every administrative reason (SEC-AUD-5), as identity checks it. |
| M1-51/14 | 2026-10-13 | `createAgent` generates the agent id before its writing unit and declares it as the affected principal; `identity.PrincipalWriter.CreatePrincipal` accepts the id. `412 version_conflict` carries the full agent as `current`. | The rights hook records the views before the change, so the principal must be named first (M1-29). |
| M1-51/15 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details: `from_billing_mode`, `to_billing_mode`, `from_credential_holder_id`, `to_credential_holder_id` (only where there is one), `from_tool_class`, `to_tool_class`, `changed_fields`, `from_status`, `revoked_tokens`, `adapter_kind` and `reason`; `setAllAgentsBillingMode` writes one entry with `agent_ids` and the parallel `from_billing_modes`, `held_agent_ids` with the parallel `from_credential_holder_ids`, `to_billing_mode`, `to_credential_holder_id` and `reason`. | 10 section 14 with the flat attributes of `audit_details.v1`, whose lists hold one kind; ids and codes only, never names or configuration values. |
| M1-51/16 | 2026-10-13 | `architecture/modules.yaml` grants `agents` the adapter vendor `pgx`. The integration tests use the real API tokens of identity for agents (so a revocation takes effect in `api_tokens`) and fake sessions for people. | The repository and the sqlc code need the driver; step-up and sessions are not under test here. |
| M1-51/17 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash, including the test worlds of INT-W14/8. The migration is `00022_agents.sql` (INT-W14/2), not the `00016_agents.sql` of M1-51/1; its comments, the code comments and the module README name the English chapter files and headings (`03-data-model.md`, `05-actions.md`, `07-permissions.md`, `09-agents.md`, 02 "Source port" and "Proof of the check", 06 "No effect", 03 principle 2, 7, 10 and 12), so its checksum is recorded anew. The main-only case `TestEmptySourcesKnowNothing` of M1-29 no longer checks `emptyPolicies`, which this ticket removes because agents is now the agent policy source. Generated files are regenerated, not merged. | `main` is English; the case was added on main after the integration base. No behaviour changes. |

## M1-45 Disable a person, revoke a device, end a session

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-45/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave12` at `da08f58`, branch `feat/M1-45-identity-revocation` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in the German chapter 08. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-44/1). |
| M1-45/2 | 2026-10-13 | `devices.revoke_reason` holds a machine code with a CHECK: `revoked_by_self` for the device's own person, `revoked_by_owner` for an owner and another person's device, `user_disabled` from `user.disable`, `inactive` from the job; the free text of the request goes only into the audit entry (`details.reason`). See [08 section 10](spec/08-identity.md) and [08 section 5.4](spec/08-identity.md). | The brief's ruling 1; 03 `devices` already constrains the column to codes. |
| M1-45/3 | 2026-10-13 | `session.revoke` stores `logout` when the session's own person ends it and `admin` otherwise; the session and every live child session end with the same reason, their families with `logout` resp. `session_revoked` (the family rule of M1-34). See [08 section 10](spec/08-identity.md). | The brief's ruling 2: 03 has no own code for "ended remotely by the person"; the audit entry names the actor anyway. |
| M1-45/4 | 2026-10-13 | Disabling a disabled person, revoking a revoked device and ending a revoked session succeed without effect: `200` with the object (`204` for a session) and an allow entry with `no_change: true`, decided in a first unit of work without rights change, so no rights version is raised and no event appended. A deleted person is `409 invalid_state_transition`. | The brief's ruling 3 (06 section 2 "No effect", R-API-27); phase 1 of a rights change raises `permission_version` unconditionally and a no-op must not invalidate every rights cache. |
| M1-45/5 | 2026-10-13 | Each use case authorizes twice: in the first unit (so a refused call never raises the rights version) and again inside the unit declared as the rights change, after phase 1 locked `permission_version` and after the resource row is locked. When the only two owners disable each other at once, the second unit sees its caller disabled and answers `401 session_revoked`. | The brief's transaction shape; decision and change then see the same rights version, and `TestDisableUser_ConcurrentOwners` fails without the second check (it answers `409 last_owner` instead). |
| M1-45/6 | 2026-10-13 | SR8 for `user.disable` is the invariant check of the M1-29 rights hook before the commit, no own last-owner query: the last active owner gets `409 last_owner`, the whole unit rolls back and the hook writes the deny entry after the rollback (`TestAZM26_DisableLastOwnerFails` compares every touched table row by row). Because every rights change checks SR8, `device.revoke` and `session.revoke` also fail with `409 last_owner` in an installation without any active owner, which `bootstrap-owner` rules out; the tests add an owner. | The brief's design note; the outcome equals a check first. |
| M1-45/7 | 2026-10-13 | `SessionEnded` of M1-34 is reused with its list `SessionIDs` and gains `At`; every step that ends sessions publishes one event per device, so each ended session is in exactly one event with its own device. `DeviceRevoked {DeviceID, UserID, Reason, At}` is new, one per revoked device. No subscriber exists in M1; the golden subscriptions of `internal/wire` do not change. | The brief asks to reuse the M1-34 event; M3-22 consumes `SessionIDs`. Before, one event named the device of the first session only, which would have been wrong for `user.disable` across devices. |
| M1-45/8 | 2026-10-13 | The use cases live in `app.Lifecycle` (port `identity.Lifecycle`), not in a `Service`; the commands `DisableUserCmd`, `RevokeDeviceCmd` and `RevokeSessionCmd` are declared in the port package with `Actor actor.Actor`, because the handlers depend on the port only. The new store port `app.LifecycleStore` holds what no store had (`SetPrincipalDisabled`, `LockDevice`, `LockLiveDevicesOfUser`, `LockLiveSessionsOfUser`, `LockLiveSessionsOfDevice`, `RevokeDevices`, `RevokeKeysOfDevices`, `InactiveDevices`); `LockUser`, `RevokeSessions`, the family revocation of `Sessions` and `Tokens.RevokeAllForPrincipal` are reused. | The brief names the earlier tickets' names as binding; the identity tickets have one use case type per area (`People`, `Sessions`, `Tokens`). The suggested `List*` names lock, so they are called `Lock*`. |
| M1-45/9 | 2026-10-13 | Lock order inside the rights change: the principal row, then the person's devices, then the sessions (each table in ascending id), then the writes in the order of 08 section 11. | A login that inserts a session holds a key share lock on its device; locking the devices first makes such a session either committed and read, or wait until after the revocation, where the revoked device refuses it. |
| M1-45/10 | 2026-10-13 | `auth.device_inactive_revoke_after` (default `180d`, minimum `1h`) is registered. An active device whose `last_seen_at` (else `approved_at`) is older counts as inactive at once: the Authenticator reports its key as not active (`401 session_revoked`, audit `device_inactive`) and records no activity for it, and a refresh is `401 session_revoked`. The job `identity.auth_cleanup` revokes such devices with the steps of `device.revoke`, one rights change per device (`device.expire`, reason `inactive`, `revoked_by` NULL, sessions `inactive`, audit without actor); selecting reads outside a rights change, so a run without such a device raises no version. `AuthCleanup.Run` returns deleted challenges plus revoked devices. | The brief's design note "Inactive devices" and 08 section 5.4: requests never wait for the job. Recording the refused request as contact would revive the device. |
| M1-45/11 | 2026-10-13 | `access.changed` is appended as the M1-29 path computes it: for the affected principal only when its visible spaces and channels differ. `user.disable` of a person who sees something appends one; `device.revoke` and `session.revoke` change no grant and normally append none, but still raise `permission_version` (05 "Permissions" = `yes`). | Same rule as M1-44/6; the hook is the only writer of `access.changed`. |
| M1-45/12 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details: `user.disable` `{reason, sessions_revoked, devices_revoked, tokens_revoked}`; `device.revoke` `{reason, user_id, revoke_reason, sessions_revoked}`; `session.revoke` `{user_id, revoke_reason, sessions_revoked}`; `device.expire` `{revoke_reason, user_id, sessions_revoked}`; a call without effect `{..., no_change: true}`. | SEC-AUD-5: IDs, codes, counts and the reason text, never token values. |
| M1-45/13 | 2026-10-13 | The integration tests run in the session world of M1-34 (`sessions_env_test.go`, now with the revocation handlers and a `DeviceRevoked` subscriber) as two groups with one world each (`TestLifecycle_Revocation`, and `TestLifecycle_Owners` for the cases that need an exact number of owners), so the package stays as close as possible to its size before; on this machine under a load average above 40 the whole package with `-race` took about ten minutes already (M1-44/13). The use cases were written before the integration tests in this session: the red run of the tests against the domain-only commit fails to build (`identity.DeviceRevoked` undefined), and seven mutations (inactive check off, no second authorize, tokens kept, no first no-change unit, one `SessionEnded` per call, reason always `admin`, keys kept) each turn a named test red. | Test first could only be shown for the domain functions; the mutations show the tests catch each behaviour (as M1-44/13). |
| M1-45/14 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash with the integration fixes INT-W14/9 (one `identity.DeviceRevoked` with the typed reason of M1-38 and `At`, one query `LockDevice` and repository method, the cleanup and Authenticator order, and the test helpers `freshPhone` and `personalOff`) and the part of INT-W14/10 that was still missing on main: `freeHandle` of the header login (M1-43) uses `fieldCodeTaken` instead of the literal. Code comments, query comments, the configuration test and these rulings name the English chapter files and headings (06 section 2 "No effect", 05 column "Permissions"); the texts of 08 sections 10 and 11 are added in English at the same places. No migration. Generated files are regenerated, not merged. | `main` is English and already holds M1-38, M1-41, M1-43 and the split helpers of INT-W14/10. No behaviour changes. |

## M1-42 Groups from OIDC claims

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-42/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave14` at `fd504d9`, branch `feat/M1-42-identity-oidc-groups` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in the German chapters and `docs/operations/oidc-entra.md`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-45/1). |
| M1-42/2 | 2026-10-14 | Preconditions verified: `group.member.sync` is in `actions.yaml` (resource `user`, right `system`, audit, rights change, no rate-limit class), in 05 rule 1.6 and in 02 4.6 for `authz`; M1-41 carries the external identity in `auth_login_flows` and maps in `completeOidcLogin`; M1-29 provides the rights hook with `permission_version`, `access.changed`, `VerifyInvariants` and the deny entry in its own transaction. | Brief "Preconditions". |
| M1-42/3 | 2026-10-14 | The port types use `ids.ID` instead of `uuid.UUID`, and `ReconcileClaimMemberships` takes the provider id as first parameter and `MembershipWithGroup`/`Group` as inputs; `ClaimDiff` carries `ExpiresAt`. `SyncClaimGroups` is declared in the interface `authz.ClaimSync`, which `Admin` embeds, so identity depends on that one method. | Every id of the code base is `ids.ID`; without the provider the pure function cannot keep claim groups of other providers untouched, which its table test asks. |
| M1-42/4 | 2026-10-14 | The unit of work of `completeOidcLogin` runs first without a rights change. When the sync finds a change it returns `authz.ErrClaimSyncNeedsRightsChange` without writing; identity rolls the unit back and runs it once more declared as a rights change of `group.member.sync` (resource kind `user`, the id is filled in by authz). A login with a present claim but nothing to change therefore never raises `permission_version`; a login that changes pays a second attempt. | 03 section 6.3 requires `permission_version` to be locked before any domain row of a rights change, and 08 section 3.4 item 3 forbids raising it without a change; the person is only known after the flow row is locked. `AllowLateRightsChange` is reserved for the Idempotency-Key stage. |
| M1-42/5 | 2026-10-14 | The sync leaves a per-unit state that the rights check reads: `access.changed` and `group.changed` name the person as actor (the unit's request is anonymous), and the deny entry of an I1 violation names the person as actor and resource and adds `provider_id`, `group_ids` (every group of the change) and `agent_ids` to the violations; the error carries the hint "Sign-in blocked by a group mapping. An owner must switch the affected agent to organization first." (`HintI1ClaimSync`). | Brief "Transaction" and "I1 violation": actor = the person; the generic deny entry of M1-29 names the declared resource, which has no id before the person is known. |
| M1-42/6 | 2026-10-14 | Extending a membership clears `expiry_reminded_at` (brief) and also `expiry_reported_at`; 03 now says the report is written once per expiry time. | Every rights change folds due expiries first (M1-30), so an expired claim membership is reported in the same unit and then extended; with the marker kept, its next expiry would never be reported and the client would keep a stale view. |
| M1-42/7 | 2026-10-14 | An I1 violation ends the flow as `failed` (own unit after the rollback) and answers the `InvariantError`; it does not count a failed attempt of the enrollment code and writes no deny entry of `auth.login.complete` (the deny entry of `group.member.sync` is the record). | Brief "I1 violation": the enrollment code stays unused, and one deny entry names what happened. |
| M1-42/8 | 2026-10-14 | `groups_state` is written only for a provider with `groups_claim`; `groups` only with `present` (sorted, without duplicates, empty omitted); `ParseExternalIdentity` refuses other combinations. A JSON `null` claim is a claim of another form (login fails); `_claim_names` that is not an object is ignored. | Fail closed for a claim the provider sends malformed; absent keeps the old memberships until they run out. |
| M1-42/9 | 2026-10-14 | Claim values never reach logs or audit details; `group.changed` carries the `Group` of the contract, whose `external_ref` is the owner's mapping, so `TestGroupClaimSync_ValuesNeverLogged` checks logs and audit for every value and events only for a value without a group. | The `GroupChangedPayload` schema of M1-48 embeds `Group` with `external_ref`, which `group.read` shows anyway; changing the contract is out of scope. |
| M1-42/10 | 2026-10-14 | Removing an expired claim membership whose value left the claim is a change (sets `removed_at`, `member_removed`, rights version raised). | The row is not removed yet (07 section 2.5 keeps expiry and removal apart); leaving it would let a later claim extend it after a removal the claim already asked for. |
| M1-42/11 | 2026-10-14 | Tests: the brief's integration tests run through the login in `identity/adapters/httpapi` (one world per group, as M1-41/13); `TestGroupClaimSync_OtherProviderUntouched`, the concurrency of the store path, `TestGroupClaimSync_ExpiryWithoutJob` and the AZ-P7 case of `group.member.sync` run in the authz PostgreSQL world, which has the Authorizer with session fakes. The red runs: the new domain and parsing tests failed to build before the code; the app and login tests were written with the use case and were shown red by mutation (hook state ignored, callback not reading the claim). | The AZ-P7 table of M1-29 lives in authz; the expiry check needs a session that outlives `auth.group_claim_max_age`, which the identity world's `auth.session_max_age` does not. |
| M1-42/12 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash; no integration fix of int/m1-wave15 belongs to this ticket (INT-W15/1 to /5 concern M1-49, M1-52 and M2-39), and the ticket adds no migration. The code comments of `domain/claimsync.go`, the chapter of `auth.group_claim_max_age` in the schema test and `config-schema.json` name `08-identity.md`; the additions to `03-data-model.md` (`group_memberships`) and `docs/operations/oidc-entra.md` (new section 7, the configuration values become section 8) are written into the English text at the same places, and the module README section stands after "OIDC login (M1-41)". The operations guide names the manual change of a claim group `409 conflict` (reason `claim_managed_group`), as the problem code check of main requires. Generated files are regenerated, not merged. | `main` is English; no behaviour changes. |

## M1-49 Who has access, preview and view as

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-49/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave14` at `fd504d9`, branch `feat/M1-49-authz-explain` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-44/1). |
| M1-49/2 | 2026-10-14 | The preview text takes the primary subtag of the caller's `users.locale`: `de` or `en`, anything else `de`; `text_locale` names it. Dates are the instant `expires_at` in the caller's `users.time_zone` (invalid or empty: UTC), `de` as `02.01.2006`, `en` as `2006-01-02`. The templates live in `authz/domain/previewtext` (`catalog_de.go`, `catalog_en.go`); the golden files hold the example of 07 section 9.3. | Brief "Plain-text rendering"; 01 section 9 (language follows `users.locale`, product default German). |
| M1-49/3 | 2026-10-14 | A delegation limit of 07 section 2.6 (`delegation_exceeds_own_level`, `delegation_exceeds_own_capabilities`, `subject_kind_not_allowed`, `owner_only`) gives `200` with `allowed = false` and `denial_reason`, decided with `CheckCreate` on the caller's rights read like `authorize`. Such a preview runs no dry run: `effects`, `invariant_violations` and `hints` are empty, the text holds the summary, abilities, capabilities and the denial line; the target must still exist (`404` otherwise). | 07 section 9.3: the preview shows why a grant would fail; a refused grant has no effects, and an agent grant with `trigger` cannot even be stored (the check of `grants`). |

A preview inside an ambient unit of work is an internal error, because its rollback would fail the outer unit. | R-RE-10; brief "Dry run in the real transaction". `previewGrant` is exempt from the Idempotency-Key stage, so no ambient unit exists on the HTTP path. |

## M1-52 Spaces and sidebar

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-52/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave14` at `fd504d9`, branch `feat/M1-52-chat-spaces` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migrations take the next free numbers of the base, `00023` and `00024`. Text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-51/1). |
| M1-52/2 | 2026-10-14 | Fractional index scheme (`chat/domain/fracindex`): the widely used base-62 scheme with integer part; digits `0-9A-Za-z` in ASCII order, head letters `a` to `z` for 1 to 26 integer digits and `A` to `Z` for 26 to 1 (negative range), no fraction ending in `0`, `A` with 26 zeros invalid. `KeyBetween("", "")` is `a0`, an open upper end increments the integer part, an open lower end decrements it, otherwise the shared prefix plus the rounded-up digit midpoint. Lists order by `sort_key COLLATE "C"`, then `id`. | The brief's scheme and vectors; byte order equals key order, appending keeps keys short (10000 appends stay at four characters). |
| M1-52/3 | 2026-10-14 | Lock for create and move: first the transaction advisory lock `0x626f746879737063` (a fixed value of its own), then every space that is not archived in ascending id (`FOR UPDATE`), then the neighbours are read in a new statement and the key computed. Archive and update lock only their row. | With no space that is not archived there is no row to lock, and two first creates computed the same key `a0` (`TestConcurrentFirstCreatesKeepDistinctKeys` fails without the advisory lock). Read committed sees the rows a waiting writer committed in the next statement, so later keys stay distinct (`TestConcurrentMovesKeepDistinctKeys`). |
| M1-52/4 | 2026-10-14 | `sidebar.updated` carries no `resource.version`; the payload `SidebarPayload` carries the version of the personal row. The scope is the space. A repeated identical PUT answers the same state and still counts the row version up (last write wins). | 04 section 2.2 rule 3 names `sidebar.updated` among the types without `resource.version`, and the catalogue says the same; the brief's "resource version = state version" contradicts it. |
| M1-52/5 | 2026-10-14 | `Structure.ListSidebarStates(ctx, a actor.Actor)` takes the caller instead of a user id. | Visibility runs through `authorize`, which needs the credentials of the caller (owner role only in a session, SR5). |
| M1-52/6 | 2026-10-14 | authz: `Effective` caps the level at `read` on an archived space path as on an archived channel path (one function, `archivedCap`); `resolveSpace` reads `SpaceArchived`. The rights views carry the level on each space itself (`AccessView.SpaceLevels`), and `DiffVisible` reports a space whose own level changes in `changed` with `level` and `previous_level`, for every rights change, not only for archiving. The new port `authz.RightsQuery` (`EffectiveRights`, implemented by the Authorizer on the same snapshot and resolution as `authorize`) gives the level and capabilities of `SpaceView`; a space visible only through a channel reports `read` without capabilities. | 07 section 4.5 point 3 asks for a `changed` entry at `read` for a space without channels, which the views of M1-29 could not express; spaces in the view only appeared or disappeared before. |
| M1-52/7 | 2026-10-14 | Order of the checks: `updateSpace` authorizes, locks, refuses an archived space with `409`, then checks If-Match and the body; `moveSpace` authorizes `space.reorder` on the instance, refuses both fields (`/before_id`), locks the order, answers an unknown space `404` and an archived one `409`, then checks the target (`/after_id` or `/before_id`: itself, unknown or archived); `putMySidebarSpace` refuses agents and apps with `403 forbidden` (reason `subject_kind_not_allowed`) before any lookup, then authorizes `sidebar.update` (`404` for an invisible space); an archived but readable space keeps its sidebar row writable. | The brief's codes; the kind check reveals nothing about a space. |
| M1-52/8 | 2026-10-14 | `grants_target_space_id_fkey` is added `NOT VALID` in `00023` and validated in `00024`. `bothy_app` may read, insert and update `spaces` (never delete: archiving is final) and read, insert, update and delete `user_space_states` (personal data). Test fixtures of authz and identity that put a space grant into the database now insert the spaces row first. | upgrade.md rule M5; 03 `spaces` retention class `content`. |
| M1-52/9 | 2026-10-14 | `docs/api/openapi.yaml`: the examples of the space operations use icon keys of the design system (`headset`, `flask`) instead of `icon.headset` and `icon.flask`, and `moveSpace` declares `409`. | An example must pass the rule it documents; moving an archived space is `409` (07 section 4.5 point 2). |
| M1-52/10 | 2026-10-14 | Field codes: `/name` `required`, `too_long` or the textcheck rule, `taken` with `409 conflict`; `/color`, `/icon`, `/sort_key`, `/after_space_id`, `/after_id`, `/before_id` `invalid`; `/use_global_order` `not_allowed`; `/reason` `required` or the textcheck rule. Audit details: none for `space.create` and `space.reorder` beyond the resource, `changed_fields` for `space.update`, `reason` for `space.archive`, `no_change: true` for an update without change and for archiving an archived space; `sidebar.update` is not audited (05). | Ids and codes only (SEC-AUD-5). |
| M1-52/11 | 2026-10-14 | Files outside the brief's scope that the ticket had to change: the space fixtures of the authz and identity tests (foreign key), `api/routes/real_test.go` (the enabled table and the stub server), the configuration tests, `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml` (the rule `space.` is stale once the key is registered) and the generated configuration reference, and `wire/empty_sources.go`, whose structure placeholder keeps only the channel and thread part. | Enabling operations and registering a key need these (M1-48/12). |

The `later` entries removed from `api/operations.yaml` use the key `milestone` of main. M1-52/7 and the module README write the refusal of agents and apps as `403 forbidden` (reason `subject_kind_not_allowed`), the form of the problem code check of main, and the two non-ASCII test inputs of `domain_test.go` and `fracindex_test.go` stand between `language-check: off` and `language-check: on` comment lines for the language gate (D-W-94). ## M1-54 Operators with invariant I1

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-54/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave16` at `1e260ad`, branch `feat/M1-54-chat-operators` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. The port methods take the caller as `a actor.Actor` like every structure method (M1-53/0); the commands carry target, assignment, reason and If-Match. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language. |
| M1-54/1 | 2026-10-14 | Order of checks (D2): load, `authorize` (unknown and invisible `404`, non-owner with read `403 forbidden` with `reason: owner_only`, stale step-up `401`), If-Match (`428`, `412` with `current`), the body of D1, then `409 conflict` for an archived target (an archived space freezes its channels). A request without If-Match is answered `428` by the request chain before any handler, as for every operation with a required If-Match; this leaks nothing, because unknown and foreign targets get the same answer. Like `archiveSpace` the use case runs two units: a first one decides and records the same assignment as a call without effect (`200`, no version, no event, no `permission_version`, allow entry with `no_change`); only a real change runs the second unit, declared as the rights change with the target as resource, which locks `permission_version` first (03 6.3), then the target row and, for a space, its inheriting channels in ascending id, and checks If-Match and the archive state again under the lock. A personal API token of an owner acts with the grants of the person without the owner role (SR5): without a grant that reads the target it is `404`, with one `403`. | 06 "No effect" and 03 6.3; a no-op must not raise the rights version; SR5 decides the token case, D2 names its `403` for a token that sees the target. |
| M1-54/2 | 2026-10-14 | Served targets (D5): chat implements `ServedTargets` in `adapters/authzsource` with the query of 10 5.3 (`queries/operators.sql`): channels that are not archived in spaces that are not archived whose effective operator is the agent, channels with a thread of kind `thread` whose fixed operator is the agent (open or closed), and every space that is not archived whose default operator is the agent as an entry with a zero channel (D5 point 4). The I1 query of authz already accepted such space entries (M1-29), so `internal/authz` is unchanged. A channel of an archived space never counts, as in 10 5.3. | Without point 4 a grant of `trigger` on an empty space with a personal default operator would pass, and a later `createChannel` (no I1 action) would break I1; `TestI1_SpaceDefaultOperatorServesSpace` holds it. |

| M1-54/4 | 2026-10-14 | Affected principals (D3): the second unit names the old and the new effective operator of the channel (for a space: the old and the new default) through `authz.RightsChanges.AddAffected` after the row locks and before the write, so the set is exact under the lock of `permission_version`; the rights hook verifies I1 for every personal agent (M1-29) and writes `access.changed`. `chat/app.Deps` takes the rights hook, the principal directory of identity and the agent policy source (an agent_id is valid when the principal is an active agent and has an agents row); `wire.ChatWrites` gains `Rights`, and `wire.NewChat` builds the directory and the policy source over the pool. | 02 8.1 and 10 5.1; chat may use the ports of identity and authz (02 4.4). |

Thread creation stays with M1-55: `TestBR_02_ClosedThreadWithFixedPersonalOperator` inserts its closed thread as a SQL fixture, because the chat repository has no thread insert besides the main thread yet. | D6 of the brief; the brief's "through the chat repository" needs a use case that M1-55 builds. |

The grant G-6 of the app Tracker is left out (apps come with M2). The `via_agent` case of the explanation uses the organization agent Texter (07 10.2, hint H1), not a personal agent as the brief says: a personal agent is triggered only by its holder, an owner who reads every channel, so it can never produce `via_agent`. | The brief asks for the world of 07 with real sources; the via_agent semantics of M1-49 (07 section 6) decide the agent. |
| M1-54/7 | 2026-10-14 | Files outside the scope list of the brief: `internal/wire` (`chat.go`, `empty_sources.go`, `serve.go`) to bind the new collaborators and drop the empty thread and served-target part, and `api/routes/real_test.go` (the stub server and the table of enabled operations), which every newly enabled operation must extend. | Without them the operations and the sources are not wired, and the routes tests do not compile. |

The integration rulings INT-W17/1 to INT-W17/5 resolve the merge with M1-55 (the shared `Thread` method, one `Deps.Policies`, the split of `rest.gen.go`, the `person` test helper) and belong to the port of M1-55, so none of them is folded in here; without M1-55, `rest.gen.go` stays below `repo.max_file_kb`.
