## M1-39 Local accounts with password and TOTP

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-39/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-39-identity-local-accounts` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. If M1-40 enters first, both entries of `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` and both `PrincipalSource` methods stay. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-39/2 | 2026-10-14 | TOTP is enrolled in two steps as the spec states (08 section 2.5, `LocalCredentialSet` with `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`): `totp_enroll` seals a new 20-byte secret, sets `totp_enabled_at` and `totp_last_step` to NULL and returns the otpauth URI once; a valid `totp_code` sets `totp_enabled_at` (if NULL) and the step. The members of one request apply in the order `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`, and any rejected member rolls the whole request back; a re-enrollment of an established path makes it pending again until confirmed. The URI is marked with `idempotency.MarkOneTimeSecret`. | Brief "SetLocalCredential"; the spec counts the path as established only with a confirmed secret, so a new, unconfirmed secret cannot keep it established. |
| M1-39/3 | 2026-10-14 | `authz` learns "credentials exist" from `PrincipalSource.HasEstablishedLocalCredential(ctx, userID)` (a row of an active identity of the person with `totp_enabled_at IS NOT NULL`). The map `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` lives in the new file `authz/app/stepup.go`: step 8 itself is in the pure `authz/domain/decide.go`, which may not call a port, so `gather` asks the source for the person of the resource (its id for a `user` resource, else its owner) and sets `Input.NoCredentialYet`; `decidePrincipalRules` then skips only the freshness check of a `conditional` action, never the check "human with a session". `passkey.register` stays "yes" until M1-40 adds its entry. | 07 section 4.2 step 8 and B7 (the rule stays in `authz`); the brief fixes the map name and signature for a clean merge with M1-40. |
| M1-39/4 | 2026-10-14 | The otpauth issuer is the host of `server.public_url`; label `issuer:login name`, every part URL-escaped. | Brief "TOTP": the display name lives in the instance settings (M1-46), which this ticket must not depend on. |
| M1-39/5 | 2026-10-14 | AEAD scheme of the TOTP secret: AES-256-GCM, key = the 32 bytes behind `auth.totp_key_ref` as hex (the form `bothy secrets init` writes for `totp_key`, generator `key`), 12-byte random nonce, AAD `"totp/v1" \|\| 0x00 \|\| identity_id`, stored `0x01 \|\| nonce \|\| ciphertext and tag`, the sealing ref in `totp_key_ref`. The start resolves the current key once in the identity step (`wire.TOTPBox`); a missing key or another length stops it. `Open` of a row with another ref resolves that ref through the secret provider (bounded by 5 seconds, its port has no context). | Brief "Sealing", 19 secrets table: a new key applies to new rows while old rows stay readable; the identity in the AAD keeps a copied ciphertext from opening for another person. |
| M1-39/6 | 2026-10-14 | Common passwords: SecLists `10k-most-common.txt` at `3a5f62f` (MIT), stored only as the first 16 hex characters of `SHA-256(lowercase(NFC(password)))`, sorted and unique (adapter README). The NFC normalization lives in `adapters/passwordpolicy` (vendor group `xtext` now also allows `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`); `domain.CheckPassword` gets the NFC form, and app gets the extra outbound port `PasswordNormalizer` beside the three ports the brief fixes. | The domain and app layers may not import vendors (02 "Layers within a module"); no word list in the repository (white-label scan, size). |
| M1-39/7 | 2026-10-14 | Failure window: the column `local_credentials.fail_window_started_at` (03) starts with the first failure; a failure at or after its end starts a new window; reaching `auth.login_fail_limit` sets `locked_until`, clears count and window and writes a deny entry on the person with `{lock: true, identity_id}` in the same unit of work as the counter. A wrong TOTP code with the right password, a person that is not active and a path without confirmed TOTP count as failures of an existing row; a locked row counts nothing. Every rejection is one aggregated denial `{cause, identity_id}` (no identity for an unknown name), never the login name, the password or the code. | 08 section 13 (failures per account: password and TOTP), brief step 6; the login name could be a mistyped password. |
| M1-39/8 | 2026-10-14 | Exactly one Argon2id computation per rejected request: `Verify` for an existing row, also while locked, else `VerifyDummy` against a hash computed at start with the current parameters. Only a successful login with changed parameters computes a second hash (the rehash) after the verification. `TestIDT21_UnknownNameTimingEqualsWrongPassword` takes the samples in pairs back to back and compares the median of the pair differences, which cancels the drift of a loaded machine; without the race detector it measures the real Argon2id, under it (as in `make check`) Argon2id is instrumented to hundreds of milliseconds with a spread far above `authz.not_found_timing_tolerance`, so the test swaps in a hasher of constant cost and measures the database work, where an unknown name could still differ. `TestDummyUsesConfiguredParameters`, `TestStaleParametersCostLikeUnknownNames` and the case `TestLockedAccountStillHashes` hold the one computation of equal cost. The integration cases run as subtests of `TestLocalAccounts` on one world, as M1-44/13, and the timing test runs in parallel with the package. | 08 section 13, ID-T21; a rehash only happens after the password was proven, so it reveals nothing about unknown names. |
| M1-39/9 | 2026-10-14 | The Argon2id floors are the minimums of the configuration keys (`auth.argon2id_memory_kib` 19456, `auth.argon2id_iterations` 2, `auth.argon2id_parallelism` 1), so the start and `bothy config check` stop with a problem that names the key (ID-T24); the hasher refuses them too. Upper bounds keep a configuration from exhausting the server: memory 4194304 KiB, 1000 passes, parallelism 255 (the limit of the library), `auth.password_min_length` 1024. | Brief "Argon2id"; the configuration loader already reports every key problem before the start. |
| M1-39/10 | 2026-10-14 | `wire.(*Identity).BindLocal(sealer, hasher)` builds the local accounts after `BindSessions` in the start step `sessions`; `nil` builds the Argon2id hasher of the configuration, tests pass a counting one. `bothy setup` does not build them. The allow entry of `local_credential.set` carries `{identity_id, password_changed, totp_enroll_started, totp_confirmed}`. | The login completion of `BindSessions` is a collaborator; the host commands never log in with a password. |
| M1-39/11 | 2026-10-14 | The login verifies the password on a read of identity, person and row without locks and only then opens the unit of work, which reads them again with the row `FOR UPDATE` and the provider and identity share-locked, and decides there (lock, counter, TOTP step, login). A row whose hash changed in between is rejected without counting (cause `credential_changed`); an unknown name runs the same lookups in its unit of work. This deviates from step 3 of the brief, which locks the row before the hash. | With the lock held during Argon2id, parallel attempts on one known name queued behind each other while those on an unknown name did not, so the time revealed accounts and an attacker could hold the row of a person; the counter stays exact because it is decided under the row lock (code review M1-39). |
| M1-39/12 | 2026-10-14 | Every verification of `passwordhash` lasts at least the running average cost (weight 1/4) of the most expensive parameter set it has seen; the average of the configured parameters is learnt at construction. | Code review M1-39 (Major): after a change of `auth.argon2id_*` the rows keep their old parameters until their next login rehashes them (08 section 2.5), so without padding a wrong password on such a row took another time than an unknown name, which verifies with the configured parameters. |
| M1-39/13 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken byte for byte from the integration squash with the integration fixes INT-W18/1 to INT-W18/7, apart from the points named here. The integration base also holds M2-39 and M2-40 (terminal), which are not on main, so nothing of them is ported: `setHostState` stays out of the enabled list of `api/operations.yaml`, the host route rows and the `Hosts` handler stay out of `api/routes/real_test.go` and `wire/routes.go`, and the host and terminal tables stay out of `migrations/privileges_test.go`. The migration is renumbered to follow `00027_chat_thread_tags.sql` without a gap: `00029_identity_local_credentials.sql` (INT-W18/3) becomes `00028_identity_local_credentials.sql`, with the number in the changelog and the module README, and its checksum is recorded. The comments of the migration, its test, `queries/local_credentials.sql`, `authz/sources.go`, `authz/app/rules_test.go` and the new identity packages use the English chapter files and terms (`03-data-model.md`, `08-identity.md`, 03 principle 9, the condition `has_credentials` of 07 section 4.2, the step-up value `conditional`), so the checksum of the migration differs from the integration file. The multi-byte cases of `TestPasswordRules` use Greek letters instead of German umlauts, because the language gate (D-W-94) rejects umlauts in tracked files; they still count twelve and eleven code points of more than one byte. the new keys of `wire/config_test.go` name the chapter `08-identity.md`. The changelog entry, the module README (status line, port list and the new section on local accounts), the secrets stack README, the key rows and the generator paragraph of `docs/operations/configuration.md`, the new section of `docs/REUSE.md` and these rulings are written into the English text at the same places. Generated files are regenerated with the generators of the ported tree, not merged. | `main` is English, holds M1-38 to M1-55 but not M2-39 or M2-40; the migration numbers of main must follow without a gap. |
| M1-39/14 | 2026-10-19 | The field error code constants `PasswordTooShort` and `PasswordCommon` in `identity/domain/password_rules.go` carry `NOSONAR`. | SonarQube Cloud reports the name `PasswordTooShort` as a possible hard-coded credential (Go rule S2068) and rates new code C for security; the values are the public error codes `too_short` and `common_password` of the API, not secrets. |

| M1-56/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M1-56-messaging-send` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. | M1-56/1 | 2026-10-14 | The foreign key `threads.parent_message_id -> messages(id)` (`DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`) is added in `00032` `NOT VALID` and validated by `00033`, as `00025` and `00026` did for `grants.target_channel_id`. Test code of chat that created threads from invented parent ids now inserts a parent message first, and the schema test of `00025` lists the new key. | upgrade.md rule M5: a new constraint on an existing table starts `NOT VALID`; before M1-56 no thread could name a parent, so the validation finds nothing. |
| M1-56/2 | 2026-10-14 | The mention scanner is code of `messaging/domain`, no Markdown library: `CodeMask` follows the block structure of CommonMark 0.31.2 (block quotes, list items, lazy continuation, headings, thematic breaks, fenced and indented code) and the inline rules of backslash escapes, autolinks and code spans, and its tests hold every code-span and code-block example of the specification (107 to 147, 328 to 349). Raw HTML is not part of content format 1, so an HTML tag is text and its backticks can open a code span; example 344 is the one case that differs from CommonMark. | Brief D5 allows a scanner that passes the examples; no new dependency, license or vendor group. SEC-OUT-1 removes raw HTML from the dialect. |
| M1-56/3 | 2026-10-14 | The port `internal/messaging` depends on no other module (02 section 7.2, `make arch`): `ThreadCreated` names the thread by id, and the handler of `createThread` reads the thread through `chat.Threads.Get` after the unit of work, so the answer shows `last_message_seq`, `message_count` and `version` after the first message. `messaging.Message` has no attachments, reactions, components or pins yet; the REST form and the events send them empty and `pinned: false`. | go-arch-lint allows a module port only kernel ports; the adapters may use the chat port. |
| M1-56/4 | 2026-10-14 | Mentions (07 section 4.6): a person is mentioned when the author may read the person (`user.read`) or the person may read the thread; the second check runs for every author, as 07 states it, and is decisive only for authors without `user.read`. The person is checked as a principal without a session, and authz gives the owner role only in a session, so an active owner (from the directory) counts as a reader of every thread. The checks run in filter mode; no audit entry for a token that stays text. | 07 section 2.1: owners read everything; without the owner rule an agent could not mention an owner who holds no grant. |
| M1-56/5 | 2026-10-14 | Body rules beyond the brief: `content_md` with U+0000 is `400` on `/content_md` with code `invalid`; an empty `attachment_file_ids` and an empty `components` are accepted, only non-empty ones are `not_enabled`; an explicit `kind: "text"` and an absent kind give the same request hash. | PostgreSQL text and jsonb cannot hold U+0000, which would otherwise be a `500`; brief D2 refuses only non-empty lists. |
| M1-56/6 | 2026-10-14 | A lost race on `(author, client_msg_id)` answers from a second unit of work like a repetition. When the request already runs inside an outer unit (only `createThread` with both `Idempotency-Key` and `first_message`), the transaction is lost with the unique violation, so the answer is `409 conflict` and the client repeats. | 04 section 5.1 point 4; a nested unit cannot start a new transaction. |
| M1-56/7 | 2026-10-14 | `message.created` is encoded without HTML escaping, so the payload size follows the content. A payload of at least `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` gets the longest valid UTF-8 prefix of `content_md` that fits (binary search over character boundaries), `content_truncated: true` and the envelope flag `truncated`; the system line of a reopen is a `message.created` without the `client_msg_id` echo. | 04 payload rule 2 (the appender refuses, the producer cuts); brief D7. |
| M1-56/8 | 2026-10-14 | The HTTP world of the tests is `services/server/test/messagingworld` (like `test/terminalworld`), so the tests of `app`, `adapters/httpapi` and `adapters/postgres` each measure their own package. The test subscriber of `MessagePosted` is a method of a type in each test package, because the txn registry derives the module of a handler from its package. | Coverage is per package (`make coverage`, SonarQube); the registry refuses handlers outside `internal/<module>`. |
| M1-56/9 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held for the identity lookup and the owner mention rule (red runs in the report); the domain tests, the migration test and the HTTP tests over PostgreSQL were written before or together with their code and run red on test defects first, not on missing code. | Honest record of the order of work. |
| M1-56/10 | 2026-10-14 | Code review: the scanner kept the container stack in a form where a blank line walked every open list item, a line of bullets rechecked the thematic break over the rest of the line per marker and a block quote searched the innermost item backwards, so a message of `message.max_content_bytes` with thousands of nested containers took seconds of CPU. The parser now keeps the first container a blank line stops at, the last non-break byte of each line and the indexes of the open items, so every step is constant time; a test bounds six such inputs. The `around_seq` window is chosen in Go (`domain.PickAround`, `domain.PageFlags`) from those rows; the flags then follow from whether a side returned more rows than it gave. All values stem from the one statement, so from one snapshot. | Brief notes 3 and 4; the window rule stays a pure, table-tested function instead of SQL arithmetic. |

## M1-58 Editing, deleting and revisions

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-58/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave19` at `b380a4e`, branch `feat/M1-58-messaging-edit-delete` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The brief's contract (the operations, schemas and parameters of `openapi.yaml`, the actions of `actions.yaml`, 02 4.3 and 4.5 naming `eventlog.Redactor`) is the same on the base and on `main`, so no contract commit is brought in. The port reuses `messaging.Message` as the `MessageView` of the brief. `Update`, `Delete` and `Revisions` take the caller from the context (kernel `actor`), as the brief's signatures state; `Create` and `CreateThread` keep their explicit actor. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step; one message DTO per module (M1-57 reuses it too). |
| M1-58/1 | 2026-10-15 | No-op rule: an edit whose resulting content equals the stored content byte for byte and whose resolved mention set equals the stored one (order ignored), an empty body included, answers `200` with the current message and its `ETag` and writes nothing: no revision, no event, no new version. Mentions that resolve differently than before (a person left or joined) make an edit with the same content a change, which stores the unchanged content as a revision. | Brief design note 2 point 5; the resulting state is what the edit compares. |
| M1-58/2 | 2026-10-15 | Error precedence of `updateMessage`: `428` (the precondition stage of the chain, before any lookup) > `404` (missing and invisible share the path: the message is read in the visible channels of `Scope(message.read)`, and `authorize` gets an unknown thread hint when none is found) > `403` (author, `write`, `channel_read_only`) > `410` > `412` > `413` > `400`. An `If-Match` that is not a version (`"x"`, a weak tag) is `412` like a stale one, because version 0 never exists. A system line names a person in its author column but is the server's: editing it is `403 forbidden` (the contract lists no `409` for `updateMessage`, and the content CHECK of a system line would otherwise fail with `500`). | Brief design note 2; 06 "Concurrency" item 2; the response list of `updateMessage` in `openapi.yaml`. |
| M1-58/3 | 2026-10-15 | The visible channels of an edit, a deletion and a revision list come from `Authorizer.Scope` for `message.read`, the list action of reading messages; `message.update` and `message.delete` are not read-level actions, which `Scope` refuses. An app needs `messages:read` for them to find its messages, so an app with only `messages:write` gets `404` on its own message. | 07 section 7.4 rule 1; the predicate is the read scope, the decision is `authorize` with the action itself. |
| M1-58/4 | 2026-10-15 | Lock order (design note 4): the thread row through `chat.Threads.LockForMessageChange`, then the message row (`LockVisibleMessage ... FOR UPDATE`), several message rows in ascending `id`, the counters `event_seq` and `audit_seq` last through the phase hooks. An edit locks only the message row; a deletion re-reads the row under its lock and ends without a write when it was deleted meanwhile, so parallel deletions count the message down once and an edit that waited for a deletion gets `410`. | 03 section 6.3; M1-62 locks the thread first too, so a deletion and a move cannot deadlock. |
| M1-58/5 | 2026-10-15 | Redaction (design note 6): `domain.RedactPayload` is a pure function that returns the `eventlog.RedactFunc` for the event form of the tombstone; `message.created` and `message.updated` become `{"message": T}`, `reaction.added` and `reaction.removed` keep `message_id`, `thread_id` and `principal_id` with `emoji: ""` and `count: 0` and drop every other member, `pin.added` and `pin.removed` stay, any other type or a schema version other than 1 is an internal error that fails the deletion. sync writes the payloads in one statement and sets `redacted_at` only when unset; `bothy_app` gets the column right `UPDATE (payload, redacted_at)` on `event_log` in its own migration `00041` (module sync), `message_revisions` is `00040` (module messaging); numbered `00040` and `00041` on the ticket branch, INT-W22/1. | 04 section 2.2 rule 5, R-DM-18; a column grant makes a change of the envelope impossible for the server role. The migration numbers are the next free ones of the base; a parallel ticket that takes them makes the later one renumber at integration. |
| M1-58/6 | 2026-10-15 | Reactions and pins (design note 8): their tables come with M1-59, so the deletion has a marked step where M1-59 adds the two `DELETE` statements and `TestDeleteMessage_RemovesReactionsAndPin`; the redaction of their events is already defined and tested. `messages.search_vector` does not exist on the base (M3-13), so DP-T01 cannot check it yet; the tombstone's empty content makes it empty by itself when the column comes. | Out of scope of the brief; the tests hold what exists. |
| M1-58/7 | 2026-10-15 | The deletion of another principal's message writes one `allow` entry through `auditing.Recorder` in the unit of work, with resource kind `message`, the message id and only `thread_id`, `channel_id` and `author_principal_id`; an own deletion and an already deleted message write none. A refusal is recorded once by the deny path of authz (R-AZ-04). `wire.NewMessaging` takes the write ports as `MessagingWrites` (appender, redactor, recorder). | 05 `message.delete`, column Audit "yes for other people's"; SEC-LOG-4 and 20 section 4.5: no content in audit details. |
| M1-58/8 | 2026-10-15 | Test first: the tests of the redactor, the thread count, the migration, the domain rules and the HTTP use cases ran red before their code (compile failures on the missing symbols, and the migration schema test on the missing table); `TestEventLogRedactionGrant` and `TestEnvelope_RedactedFlag` were written after the grant and with an envelope mapping that already existed, so they passed on their first run. | Honest record of the order of work. |
| M1-58/9 | 2026-10-16 | Port onto main: code and tests as in the integration squash except: the migrations are `00039` and `00040` (after `00034`); M3-13 is not on main, so `LockVisibleMessage`, `UpdateMessageContent` and `TombstoneMessage` keep `*` (INT-W22/3 is not ported), `wire.NewMessaging` takes no directory and the M3-13 world is not touched; M2-24 and M2-39 parts stay out. From wave 22: the unions of INT-W22/1, INT-W22/4 and the `Redactor` and `Audit` of INT-W22/5 in `filter_test.go` and `mentions_test.go`. | `main` holds M1-56, M1-57 and M1-60 only. |

## M1-61 Read state, drafts and personal settings

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-61/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave21` at `8b981ed`, branch `feat/M1-61-sync-personal-data` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00041`. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base holds every rule the ticket needs (03 `read_states`, `drafts`, `user_settings`, 04 section 14, 05 personal actions and events, R-AZ-21, 01 section 11, 13 section 3.3, the OpenAPI operations and schemas are the same apart from translation), so no contract commit was brought in. The test of the setting registry finds the chapters by their number (`docs/spec/01-*.md`, `13-*.md`) and their section numbers, so it reads the German chapters of the base and the English ones of `main`. `make check` runs with `SONAR_SKIP=1` as the launcher sets it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step; chapter names differ between the base and `main`. |
| M1-61/1 | 2026-10-15 | A setting that was deleted starts again at `version = 1` on its next PUT; the `settings.updated` of the delete carries the last value with the last version plus one and `deleted: true`. | Brief note 16: clients drop the local entry on `deleted: true`, so the new entry with version 1 applies; keeping a tombstone row only for the version would store a default the server must not store (04 section 14, row Settings). |
| M1-61/2 | 2026-10-15 | `draft.deleted` carries no `resource.version`, although the brief's event table names `version: last + 1`. | The catalogue (`docs/spec/actions.yaml`, ADR 0049) records `resource_version: false` for `draft.deleted`, and the frame conformance (G9) checks every delivery against the catalogue; the core contract is not edited by this ticket. Clients apply it in `seq` order (04 section 9 point 3). The empty final state of a PUT still answers version last plus one in its body. |
| M1-61/3 | 2026-10-15 | The six personal actions of this ticket (`read_state.update`, `read_state.mark_unread`, `draft.read`, `draft.write`, `settings.read`, `settings.update`) are refused to agents and apps by `authorize` itself: the requirement table of authz has override rows `All(Human, Self)` and `All(Human, Self, Level(read))` with the unchanged 05 texts. An agent or app without read on the thread or channel still gets `404` (visibility comes first). | Brief note 22: permission logic only in authz; the requirement step of M1-28 let an agent naming itself pass `self`. The table already holds overrides by action id (message.create, thread.tags.set). |

The kind and space of a channel draft come from `Structure.GetChannel` after `draft.write` allowed the call. | Brief note 8 asks for the messaging port, and the base port had no reply check; additive changes only. |

## M1-63 Catch-up over REST and snapshot

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-63/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave25` at `719cfaa`, branch `feat/M1-63-sync-catch-up` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base already holds what the ticket needs (the query parameter `epoch` on `listEvents`, the required `features` on `Snapshot`, `sync -> identity` in 02 and `architecture/modules.yaml`, R-SY-03, R-SY-04, R-SY-16, R-SY-18), so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-63/1 | 2026-10-15 | `head_seq` is the committed value of `event_seq`, read without a lock through the new `instance.Versions.EventSeq`; `min_retained_seq` is `min(seq)`, or `head_seq + 1` for an empty log. | After retention the log can be empty while the counter is not, so `max(seq)` would report `cursor_ahead` wrongly; `instance.Counters` refuses calls outside the phase hooks (M1-15). A commit is visible before its counter lock is released (04 section 3.2). |
| M1-63/2 | 2026-10-15 | The resync check order, first match wins: an `epoch` different from `sync_epoch` gives `epoch_changed`, then `cursor > head_seq` gives `cursor_ahead`, then `cursor < min_retained_seq - 1` gives `retention`, then `head_seq - cursor > sync.catchup_max_events` gives `too_far_behind`. Without `epoch` there is no epoch check. | A new time line can overtake old cursors, so the epoch decides first (R-SY-18); `cursor_ahead` stays the second net for a client without a stored epoch (R-SY-04); a lost range is reported as `retention` before the size of the backlog. |
| M1-63/3 | 2026-10-15 | `active_runs` is empty (`adapters/noruns`, bound by `internal/wire`) until M2-05 binds the runs port, and every agent of `agents` has `state: unknown` without `reported_billing_mode` until heartbeats exist (M2-20). A caller the agent listing refuses as a whole (`403`, an app without the agents scope) gets an empty `agents`. | The table `runs` and the heartbeats do not exist in M1; `AgentRuntimeState` documents `unknown`. A snapshot must not fail for an app because it may not read agents. |
| M1-63/4 | 2026-10-15 | The unread counters are computed through ports, never by a join across module tables: `chat.Threads.Counters` (new, threads of the listed channels with `last_message_seq > unread_floor_seq`), the person's `read_states` of those channels (new query `ListReadStatesInChannels`) and `messaging.Mentions.CountUnread` with the position `GREATEST(last_read_seq, unread_floor_seq)` per thread; unread is `last_message_seq` minus that position, never below 0. A thread is listed when unread or its mentions are above 0 and `thread.read` allows it in filter mode. Threads without a message above their floor are not listed, and their mentions do not count. The counters cover the channels the snapshot lists (visible, not archived). | 02 "Rules" point 1 and 07 section 7.4 rule 6; every message of such a thread is at or below the floor, so nothing there is unread for anybody; every `channel_id` of `unread_threads` then names an entry of `channels`. |
| M1-63/5 | 2026-10-15 | `trace_context` is in a catch-up envelope only when the caller is an agent; no delivery-only member is set in M1. | 04 "Envelope": the traceparent goes only to agent adapters; `invocation_token` and `redelivered` come with M2. |
| M1-63/6 | 2026-10-15 | The port is named `Snapshot` (02) and its DTO `SnapshotView`; the DTO and its parts (`SnapshotSpace`, `SnapshotChannel`, `UnreadThread`, `RunSummary`, `AgentPresence`, `DraftRef`, `EvaluatedFlag`, `SnapshotPrincipal`) are types of the sync port that mirror the OpenAPI schemas. | Go cannot give an interface and a struct the same name in one package; the port package may import only kernel packages (M1-16/4), so it cannot carry the views of chat. |
| M1-63/7 | 2026-10-15 | `is_owner` comes from `identity.Directory.User` (an active owners row) for people and is false for agents and apps; `permission_version` comes from `Authorizer.Scope`. | `authorize.Scope` has no owner flag: `InstanceWide` is also true for a grant on the instance, which is no owner role. |
| M1-63/8 | 2026-10-15 | `CatchUp.Check` authorizes `events.catch_up` on the instance; `CatchUp.Page` does not and checks each event with its receive action. The visible sets of the page query come from `Authorizer.Scope` with `channel.read`. | The gateway reuses `Check` (M1-64, M1-65), so REST and WebSocket apply the same rule, including `events:subscribe` for apps; `channel.read` is the list action whose scope is the set of readable channels and spaces. |
| M1-63/9 | 2026-10-15 | `authorize.ResourceFromEvent(kind, id, space, channel, thread)` (new in `kernel/authorize`, tests in `internal/authz`) names a thread, channel or space by its id without hints and gives any other kind only its most specific scope (thread, else channel, else space). | The scope of a stored event is a copy from the time of the event; a thread moved since then would contradict a channel hint, which authz treats as an internal error. With the most specific hint authz reads the current path, so the rights at delivery decide (04 section 8.1). |
| M1-63/10 | 2026-10-15 | `channels` lists the channels of `ListSpaceChannels` per visible space; a space that turns invisible or unknown between the two reads is skipped. DM channels without a space are not listed, because chat has no listing for them before direct messages (V3, `dm.enabled`). | The brief asks for the listings of `listSpaces` and `listSpaceChannels`; M1 creates no DM channel. |
| M1-63/11 | 2026-10-15 | Test code: `test/messagingworld.NewSync` builds the world of `NewPersonal` with catch-up and snapshot as `internal/wire` builds them (`wire.NewSyncReads`), with the identity and agents modules, the instance row with its `sync_epoch` (pgtest databases have none) and a configurable partition size; the retention case ages the first partition with `UPDATE event_log SET occurred_at` and runs the partition maintenance of M1-16 with the world's clock. AZ-P5 for the snapshot builds three random worlds through the public ports with a logged seed. | The world of 07 section 11.1 in `internal/authz` is a fake world without HTTP; the shared HTTP world of chat and messaging has every module the snapshot reads. |
| M1-63/12 | 2026-10-15 | Review finding (Blocker): `pin.added` and `pin.removed` (a message, received with `thread.read`), `command.changed` (a command, `command.read` on the channel) and `grant.changed` (a grant, `grant.read` on space, channel, host or principal) made every catch-up page that met them fail with `500`. Fixed for the whole class in one place: `authorize.ResourceFromEvent` sets `Resource.Event` with the full scope, and authz (`eventTarget`, for `Authorize` and for `webhook_out.deliver`) decides such an event on the first object the receive action takes: the target of a grant (the subject principal of an instance grant), then the thread, channel and space of the scope; an event with no fitting object, or whose receive action is still deferred (`file.read` until M3), is filtered as `not_found`, never an error. The webhook delivery of apps builds its descriptor with `ResourceFromEvent` too. `TestResourceFromEventCatalogue` decides every durable event type of the catalogue without an error. | A stored event that no rule can decide must not stop every reader at its `seq`; the fix in authz covers REST catch-up, the gateway (M1-65) and the webhooks, which had the same class. |
| M1-63/13 | 2026-10-15 | Red team round 1 (NEIN, CI red): the test package `api/routes` lacked the two new operations in its stub server and route table, and golangci-lint and semgrep reported five findings in the new lines. Fixed without a behaviour change, apart from one: `Page` takes the receive action through `authorize.ParseAction`, and an event whose receive action is outside the catalogue of the build is filtered instead of failing the page. | The semgrep rule allows catalogue ids only through constants or Parse functions; failing closed matches the webhook delivery. |
| M1-63/14 | 2026-10-18 | Port onto main: code and tests as in the integration squash (with the M1-63 side of INT-W28/1) except the points named here. M2-26 is not on main, so `authz` has no `authorizeDelivery` and `eventTarget` runs only in `Authorize`, `authorize.Resource` gains only `Event`, and `apps/app/deliver.go` is not touched; the webhook parts of M1-63/12 apply when M2-26 is ported. The `Identity` of `internal/wire` on main has no `Directory` (it came with M2-24 and M3-13), so `buildSyncReads` and the messaging test world pass `identityapp.NewDirectory` over the identity repository, as chat and messaging do on main, and `buildSyncReads` needs only the agents and settings steps. The messaging test world on main builds no instance settings (M3-13), so `NewSync` builds them in `syncReads` for the identity writes, which have no `Search`. M3-18 and M1-62 are not on main: the chat step builds only the sync reads, `threads.sql` gains only `ThreadCounters`, and `txn_registrations.golden` is unchanged because the ticket registers no subscriber. | The ticket keeps its behaviour; only collaborators that main does not have yet are left out or built the way main builds them. |

## M1-62 Moving threads

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-62/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave25` at `719cfaa`, branch `feat/M1-62-chat-thread-move` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base holds every rule the ticket needs (03 `threads`, `thread_tags`, "I1 check", "Lock order", 05 `thread.move` and the events `thread.moved` and `thread.moved_out`, 04 "Basic rule", 07 4.2, the OpenAPI operation and schemas are the same apart from translation), so no contract commit was brought in. `make check` runs with `SONAR_SKIP=1` as the launcher sets it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-62/1 | 2026-10-15 | `thread.moved_out` is appended before `thread.moved`, both in the transaction of the move with consecutive `seq`. | A client that receives both removes the thread from the source first and then shows it in the target, so it ends with the thread in the target (brief note 1g). |
| M1-62/2 | 2026-10-15 | A thread without operator moves only into a channel without effective operator; a thread with operator only into a channel whose effective operator is that agent (`IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`). | 03 `threads`: the target's effective operator must equal `operator_agent_id`, and `NULL` equals only `NULL`; otherwise the move would give an operated thread to a channel run by nobody or another agent, against R-DM-12 and I1. |
| M1-62/3 | 2026-10-15 | The source as target is `400 validation_failed` on `/target_channel_id` with code `same_channel`; an archived target (or a target in an archived space) is `409 conflict`. The domain checks run after `authorize` in the order main thread, same channel, archived target, dm channel, operator; the reason check (`CheckReason`) follows them. | The same channel is a malformed request, not a state conflict; an archived channel accepts no management change (07 4.5). The order is brief note 1b. |
| M1-62/4 | 2026-10-15 | The domain rule is `CanMove(th Thread, source, target MovePlace, targetOperator ids.ID) error` with the zero ID for "no operator", not the signature `CanMove(ThreadState, ChannelState, ChannelState, *uuid)` of the brief. | `domain.ThreadState` already names the thread state (`open`, `closed`, M1-55); the existing types `Thread` and the zero `ids.ID` express the same facts without a second thread type or a pointer. |
| M1-62/5 | 2026-10-15 | After the row lock the use case compares the locked thread's channel with the one it authorized; a concurrent move that committed in between makes it authorize `thread.move` again on the new place before any check or write. | 07 4.2 and brief note 4: no decision may use the old location. The read before the lock is needed so an unknown and an invisible thread give the same `404` before any lock. |
| M1-62/6 | 2026-10-15 | sync keeps one subscriber object: `syncapp.NewSubscribers(drafts, moves)` takes the new port `ThreadMoveRows` next to `DraftRows`; messaging gains `messagingapp.Subscribers` with `ThreadMoveRows`, and `wire.Modules.MessagingSubscribers` is required. The registrations are `chat.ThreadMoved -> messaging` and then `-> sync`. | Brief scope; the subscriber of `MessagePosted` keeps its draft-only port (M1-61 note 5). |
| M1-62/7 | 2026-10-15 | Test code only: the cross-module world of `test/integration` registers the three subscriptions of internal/wire, binds the subscriber of sync, serves the personal handlers of sync and inserts a `devices` row for every session, because read states and drafts name the writing device (foreign key). | The move tests need read states and drafts through the real HTTP chain in the world with Support, Allgemein, Eskalation and Marketing of 07 sections 10 and 11.1. |
| M1-62/8 | 2026-10-15 | authz treats a zero `TargetChannelID` of `thread.move` as an unknown, invisible target (`not_found`), no longer as an internal error. | Red team: the nil UUID passes the request validator as `target_channel_id`; the internal error answered `500` for an existing thread and `404` for an unknown one, an existence oracle for invisible threads against R-AZ-01 and brief note 1a. |
| M1-62/9 | 2026-10-18 | Port onto main: code and tests as in the ticket range (`719cfaa..014e5ac`) with the M1-62 side of INT-W28/1, except the points named here. M3-18 is not on main, so the ticket keeps its own `ShareChannel` and `ShareSpace` (query, `app.Store` method, postgres method and fake) and INT-W28/2 is not ported. M3-13 is not on main and `threads` has no search columns, so `MoveThread` returns the whole `threads` row and the adapter converts it with `toThread` directly instead of through `threadRow`. The cross-module world of `test/integration` on main built neither messaging nor the thread and message handlers (they came with M3-13), so it now builds the messaging module as `internal/wire` does and serves the thread, message and personal handlers; the authorizer has no `Hosts` (M2-39 is not on main). The txn registry subscribes `messaging.MessagePosted -> sync` and then `chat.ThreadMoved -> messaging -> sync`; the notify subscribers of M3-18 are not on main, so `txn_registrations.golden` gains only the two `ThreadMoved` lines. Catch-up (M1-63) delivers `thread.moved` and `thread.moved_out` through `authorize.ResourceFromEvent` like every other thread event and needs no change. Generated files are regenerated, not merged. | `main` holds M1-55 to M1-61 and M1-63 but not M2-39, M3-13 or M3-18; the ticket keeps its behaviour. |
| M1-62/10 | 2026-10-20 | Port onto main after M1-63 and M3-18: M3-18 brought the queries `ShareChannel` (channels.sql) and `ShareSpace` (spaces.sql) with the same SQL, the same `app.Store` methods and the same Postgres methods that this ticket had added for `thread.move`, so the copies of this ticket are removed and the move uses those of M3-18, as INT-W28/2 did on the integration line; the fake keeps one `ShareChannel` and `ShareSpace` (with the fault hooks of the move tests). The txn registry subscribes `chat.ThreadMoved` to messaging, then to sync, after `messaging.MessagePosted -> sync` and before the two archive subscriptions of notify, the order of the integration line. Unit tests over the fakes cover the failure paths of the move and the sync subscriber of `ThreadMoved`. M1-62/9 describes the earlier base; where it differs, this row wins. | The PR lane ports only the ticket and the integration fixes it needs; no behaviour of either ticket changes. |

## M2-39 Host records and the target host

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-39-1 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-39-terminal-hosts` from `int/m1-wave14` (`fd504d9`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it (task of the workflow over the brief's local merge). The base copy of chapter 24, the terminal part of chapter 03 (`hosts.reported_session_users`, `host_session_users`, the composite key of `terminal_sessions`, R-DM-39 to R-DM-41, `host.os_user_max_chars`) and the host schemas of `openapi.yaml` lag `plan/spezifikation`; this branch carries the plan text of these parts, merged three-way so the base edits of M1-03, M1-15, M1-50 and M2-38 stay, and regenerates the REST models and the SDK clients. | Ruling T12, the heart of the ticket, exists only in the plan text; carrying it verbatim is no deviation from the core contract, and the plan port then brings identical content. |
| D-M2-39-2 | 2026-10-14 | Every transition into a state other than `active` ends the open sessions of the host in the same transaction; where the table names no close reason (into `pairing` or `confirming`) the reason is `host_rekeyed`. A request for the state a host already has is no transition and answers without effect. `pairing` and `confirming` cannot be retired (24 3.1 draws no such edge; spec gap in the report). | When leaving `disabled` there are normally no open sessions; ending them anyway costs one indexed query and closes any row a crash left behind. |
| D-M2-39-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TerminalOn` is true for an effective owner on every host and for a grant `terminal` in effect; the exception of I6 for owners on a `personal` host (07 2.12 point 3) is decided by the guard SR11 of M2-40 with `i6_holder_only`, after the requirement. | Before M2-40 no host can be `personal` (only `confirmHost` and `setHostLlmCredentials` set it), so the atom does not repeat the guard. |
| D-M2-39-4 | 2026-10-14 | `host.read` is `All(HumanSession, Any(Owner, Assignee, Terminal))`, `host.manage` `All(HumanSession, Owner)`. A requirement whose first part is `HumanSession` is a gate: `authorize` and `Scope` evaluate it right after the liveness and pending checks, before snapshot, resolution and visibility, and answer `forbidden` with `subject_kind_not_allowed`; 07 4.2 says so in one paragraph. | 05 4.14 refuses agents, apps and personal tokens with `subject_kind_not_allowed` (TM-T17, AZ-M59), which visibility first would turn into `404`; the answer depends only on the principal, so it reveals nothing (AZ-P4). |
| D-M2-39-5 | 2026-10-14 | `access.changed` lists the hosts with `TerminalOn` per principal: over every host for an effective owner and over the host grants otherwise; a host is `added` with `capabilities: ["terminal"]` and no level, `removed` with kind and id only, never `changed`. State and holder do not enter the set, so `setHostState` and a holder change write no `access.changed`; `owner.add`, `owner.remove` and `createHost` report hosts like every other target. A rights change on a host target affects every active owner, the current holder and the subjects of the grants on the host (groups expanded). | M2-46 can end sessions from `removed` entries alone; the set follows 07 2.12 point 3, which names neither state nor holder. |
| D-M2-39-6 | 2026-10-14 | A grant on a host needs an existing host (`404` otherwise) and refuses a retired one with `409 conflict` without a reason; `grant.changed` of a host grant has an empty scope. `getGrant` and `listGrants` with `target_kind=host` read host grants like instance grants (owner only); a non-owner gets `404` for a host grant and for the host filter, the answer of an unknown id. | A retired host is final (24 3.1), a grant on it could never work; the holder sees the host, not the grants on it. |
| D-M2-39-7 | 2026-10-14 | `listHosts` asks `Scope(host.read)` (new members `HostIDs`, ascending, and `AllHosts`, memoised per request only) instead of authorizing `host.read` on the instance, then filters each item with `authorize` in filter mode; it lists hosts in every state, retired ones included, ordered by `lower(name)` then `id`, with a keyset cursor bound to the caller. | The catalogue gives `host.read` only the resource `host`, so the brief's `ByKind{instance}` cannot be reached; `Scope` runs the same liveness checks and the gate. Retired hosts stay readable records (retention `security` after `retired_at`), and the contract has no filter parameter. |
| D-M2-39-8 | 2026-10-14 | A name taken by a live host (without regard to case, also when the unique index fires in a race) is `409 conflict` without a reason; `updateHost` on a retired host is `409 conflict`; `disabled_at` describes the current disabled state and is cleared on enable and retire; every `host.manage` write with an effect is a rights change, also a pure rename; a write without effect raises nothing and audits `no_change`. | Like group names in M1-29; the audit keeps the history; 05 rule 1.7 (Rechte ja) and 06 section 2 "Ohne Wirkung". |
| D-M2-39-9 | 2026-10-14 | A session user name must equal a reported name exactly, including case (else `400 validation_failed` with `errors[].code` `session_user_not_on_host`); uniqueness per host stays without regard to case (03). Two new `errors[].code` values of `validation_failed`: `unknown_user` (a person id that is unknown, not a human or deleted) and `mutually_exclusive` (`assigned_user_id` with `clear_assigned_user = true`); 24 section 13 names them. | The host service compares `osu` exactly with `host_service.session_users` (24 5.3), so a case variant would fail there with `4104`. |
| D-M2-39-10 | 2026-10-14 | `terminal_session.changed` comes with M2-44; `EndSessions` is the one path of every server end and writes `terminal.terminate` as the system (no actor) with `session_id`, `close_reason`, `bytes_in`, `bytes_out` and `duration_ms`. | No session can be opened before M2-44, so no client can miss the event. |
| D-M2-39-11 | 2026-10-14 | `reported_session_users` is shown only once a host has been paired (`paired_at` set); `connected` comes from `Presence`, bound to `NoConnections` until M2-43 replaces it with the registry of the control connections. | The schema says "Absent before pairing"; without the route `/hosts/connect` no host is connected, so `false` is the truth. |
| D-M2-39-12 | 2026-10-14 | `host.updated` carries the host without `noise_public_key` (the fingerprint stays, it is a digest); `key_changed` stays absent until M2-41. `host.manage` audits one `update` entry when name or holder change and one `session_user` entry per assignment and per end with the name in the new reserved member `os_user` (`auditing.OSUserName`, pattern of `host_session_users.os_user`); never the host name. | "never keys" (05 6) means no key member; one entry per assignment matches 24 10.1 "per assignment or end" and keeps the details flat. |
| D-M2-39-13 | 2026-10-14 | The pairing code is the token kind `hpc` of ADR 0061, issued by `identity.Tokens.NewHostPairingCode` (redacting secret and HMAC with the token pepper, no database); the authenticator refuses it before any lookup. `createHost` keeps the `Idempotency-Key` of the base contract (M1-03/6) instead of `auth_flow` of the brief: the handler marks the one-time secret, a replay answers `409 conflict` with `reason: one_time_secret`. | `createHost` must return a code (openapi `HostPairingCode`, the check `(state = 'pairing') = (pairing_code_hash IS NOT NULL)`), and identity owns the token format and the pepper; the merged contract wins over the brief. M2-41 adds the redemption and reuses `PairingCodes`. |
| D-M2-39-14 | 2026-10-14 | The signing-key purpose `terminal` gets an active key at every start; like `webhook_out` it rotates only on owner request, its retiring window is `terminal.session_token_ttl` plus `auth.clock_leeway`, and `getJwks` does not publish it. `rotateSigningKey` accepts `terminal` now; the clause of M1-32/2 that refused it until M2-39 is superseded. | 19 10 names no interval for it, and the host service fetches the keys over its control connection (M2-43). |
| D-M2-39-15 | 2026-10-14 | AZ-M60 of 07 11.1 names a host R3 without holder on which Alex holds no grant, instead of R1. | R1's holder is Alex (row AZ-M54), and a holder reads the host (07 2.12 point 5), so the old row contradicted the visibility rule. |
| D-M2-39-16 | 2026-10-14 | Names that differ from the brief: the fixtures live in `services/server/test/terminaltest` and the HTTP world in `services/server/test/terminalworld` (test code, outside the coverage measurement), the tests are split over `terminal/app`, `adapters/postgres` and `adapters/httpapi`; `Precondition` replaces `IfMatch int64` in the commands; `People.User` returns a found flag; the checksum vectors of `apitoken` carry no kind, so `hpc` adds no vector (the format test covers it); the test world authenticates with the test authenticator of `httpservertest` (sessions, step-up times and tokens through the fake credential source), like the worlds of M1-48 and M1-51. `audit.reason_max_chars` enters the schema here (M2-24 adds the same key). | `make coverage` measures each package by its own tests (D-M2-24-23); `go-arch-lint` admits test support only under `test/`. |
| D-M2-39-17 | 2026-10-14 | `httpserver.DecodeCursor` refuses a cursor key with a NUL character in any string with `400 cursor_invalid`, for every list. | The cursor has no signature (M1-18), so a client can forge a key whose string PostgreSQL text cannot hold (SQLSTATE 22021, a `500`); the red team of M2-39 found it in `listHosts` after M1-31/16 had the same class, so the codec fixes it once for all lists. |
| D-M2-39-18 | 2026-10-18 | Port onto main from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave15` with the integration fixes INT-W15/3 to /5: the migration is `00028_terminal_hosts.sql` (00023 to 00027 are M1-52, M1-53 and M1-55 on main; content and checksum unchanged, `terminalVersion` 28); `authz.AccessViewOf` takes spaces as paths with the archived flag and the space levels of M1-52 and the hosts of M2-39 (`spaces []Path, hosts []ids.ID`, all four maps); the start steps register chat and then terminal, and authorizer, rights hook and admin get both the structure and the host source. The contract of the session users (03 4.14, 24, `openapi.yaml`) is already on main, so its port commit is not taken; the spec additions of this ticket (07 3 and 4.2, 08 12, 19 secrets and SEC-AUD-5, 24 2.2, 10.1 and 11) are written into the English chapters, the module README is English, and generated files are regenerated, not merged. | Tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each as one pull request with the integration fixes it needs, and `main` is English. |

## M2-40 Terminal rights, fresh step-up and the guards SR10 to SR12

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-40-1 | 2026-10-14 | `terminal.read_own` is `All(HumanSession, ByKind{terminal_session: Any(Self, Owner), host: Auth})`: on a host, "self or owner" means "own sessions on a host you can see, owners all"; the list query of M2-44 restricts the rows to the caller's `user_id` for people who are not owners, so on the host only visibility counts. A `terminal_session` is visible to its person and to effective owners whatever the requirement (else `404` with `not_self`), so the Owner gets `forbidden` `not_self` on `terminal.attach` of a foreign session. | A host has no owner of its own sessions; the requirement can only gate the list, the rows are the use case's filter. |
| D-M2-40-2 | 2026-10-14 | Fresh step-up evaluates only its own branch in step 8: the quick reattach first (only `terminal.attach` of a `detached` session from the same device and login session within `terminal.quick_reattach_window`, equal allowed), else a fresh step-up (`terminal.step_up_max_age`, equal allowed, `passkey` or `device_key`, not consumed). A console session fails closed until M6-18 adds the passkey rule with `userVerification = required`. `identity.StepUp.ConsumeFresh` checks the freshness again on the locked session row (the first domain row lock of the action's transaction) with the rule of `authorize` (`authz.FreshStepUpCheck`), never on the actor of the request. | Two requests with one confirmation both pass `authorize`; only the lock orders them, so the second opening sees the consumption (08 6.3 point 4, 03 6.3). Console sessions do not exist before M6-01. |
| D-M2-40-3 | 2026-10-14 | The guards of `terminal.open` and `terminal.attach` run after step 8 in the order SR10 server host, SR10 assignee, SR11, SR12; the first failure wins. | The order of 07 section 5 and 24 6.1 step 2; a server host is closed to every person who is not an owner, assigned or not, so its reason tells the person what would help; a person without a fresh step-up learns `step_up_required` first. |
| D-M2-40-4 | 2026-10-14 | The difference of `access.changed` and the preview effects use `TerminalEffective` (07 2.12 point 3 with the I6 clause): a switch to `personal` gives `removed: [{kind: host, id}]` for every other owner, a switch back `added` with `capabilities: [terminal]`. SR10 and SR12 are guards, not part of effective `terminal`, and never appear in the difference; a state or assignee change still writes no `access.changed` (D-M2-39-5). | The declaration raises the rights version (03), so the I6 clause belongs to the view; 04 8.2 reports the additional right itself, and SR10 depends on `reported_is_server_host`, which raises no rights version. |
| D-M2-40-5 | 2026-10-14 | I6 is verified in phase 3.1 of every rights change, like I1 (M1-29): for every host with `llm_credentials = personal` the grants valid at `t` (not revoked, `expires_at` after `t`, active subject, live group); a group grant there is always a violation, a grant to a user other than the holder too; owners do not count. | `user.enable` of a person whose grant became dormant before the switch to `personal` would make a grant to another person valid again; 03 6.8 names only the actions where a violation starts from the request. |
| D-M2-40-6 | 2026-10-14 | With several violations in one change the problem code is the first of `last_owner`, `i1_violation`, `i6_violation`, and `conflicts` lists the entries of that code; the deny entry carries the violations of that code (`details.violations` with `host_id` for I6). The brief's order (`i1`, `i6`, `last_owner`) is not taken. | M1-29 already ruled `last_owner` before `i1_violation` (without an owner no personal agent can be in order either); keeping that and putting I6 last changes no existing answer. |
| D-M2-40-7 | 2026-10-14 | `setHostLlmCredentials` refuses `credential_holder_id` with `none` or `organization` with the new `errors[].code` `not_allowed` (documented in `HostLlmCredentialsSet` and 24 13); a `retired` host answers `409 conflict`; every other state accepts a declaration, and `confirmHost` (M2-41) sets it again at the confirmation. `ApplyLlmDeclaration` writes the declaration on a locked host and ends the foreign sessions; the caller audits and appends `host.updated`, so `confirmHost` can reuse it with its own audit. | `retired` is final (24 3.1); a silently ignored holder would hide a client error. |
| D-M2-40-8 | 2026-10-14 | `RecordSessionUserReport` compares a report with the stored one by the set of names and each name's ordered `workdirs`; an equal report writes nothing and raises no rights version. A different report locks the host, compares again, writes the report with the next host version, ends with `access_revoked` the sessions of every person whose assigned name vanished and appends `host.updated`; it writes no `host.manage` audit entry (`host.pair` and `host.connect` audit their own calls). | A host repeats its report after every reconnect, and a version bump per reconnect would empty every rights cache. |
| D-M2-40-9 | 2026-10-14 | The rights hook accepts `host.pair` and `host.connect` as the action of a declared rights change, although 05 marks their column "Permissions" `no`. | 03 (`grants`, paragraph on `permission_version`) counts `reported_session_users` as rights state, which only these actions write; 05 rule 1.7 says that actions with "Permissions" `yes` raise the version, not that no other may. |
| D-M2-40-10 | 2026-10-14 | `updateHost` that changes or clears the assignee ends, in its unit of work, the sessions of the old assignee on the host with `access_revoked` unless the old assignee is an active owner (`People.IsActiveOwner`, bound to `identity.Directory.User` without `authorize`); sessions of owners and of the new assignee stay. A change of the owner status or of `reported_is_server_host` is M2-46's. | Only the old assignee can lose SR10 by this change; every other person who is not an owner was already refused by SR10. |
| D-M2-40-11 | 2026-10-14 | The preview of a grant on a host renders `summary.host`, then denial, I6 violation, `terminal_not_effective`, group reach, missing expiry; no abilities and no capabilities line. The cause of `terminal_not_effective` for a person is the first failing guard (server host, assignee, session user); a group gets one hint with the number of its active members in effect whom SR10 or SR12 would deny, none for 0; no such hint when an I6 violation names the host. | The abilities and capabilities lines describe levels in the tree, which a host has not; the I6 refusal makes the hint moot. |
| D-M2-40-12 | 2026-10-14 | 07 4.1 gains the reason `terminal_no_session_user` (SR12), section 5 the rule SR12; 19 SEC-AUD-5 the I6 form `{code, host_id, credential_holder_id, principal_ids}` of `violations`. | The brief names both as present, but the merged chapter lacked them (spec gaps). |
| D-M2-40-13 | 2026-10-14 | Names that differ from the brief: `kernel/authorize.Principal` gains `StepUpMethodName`, `StepUpConsumedTime` and `ClientComponentName` (only `actor.Actor` implements it); the domain mirrors `authorize.TerminalFacts` as `TerminalSessionFacts` and takes the action as a string, because `authz/domain` imports no kernel port; `domain.HostFacts` gains the stored host facts and stands for the brief's host facts of the guard; `ValidateDeclaration` returns one `kernel/errs` field error like M2-39's checks; `terminal.SetLlmCredentials` carries a `Precondition` instead of `IfMatch int64`; `actor.Actor` stands for `actor.Principal`; `wire.IdentityWrites.Fresh` passes the `FreshStepUpCheck`; `ActiveGrantsOnHosts` of M2-39 serves for the brief's `ActiveGrantsOnHost`; the work is built on `feat/M2-40-terminal-authz` from `int/m1-wave15` and not merged locally (task of the workflow over the brief's local merge). | The merged names and types of M1 and M2-39 win over the brief (Boundaries). |
| D-M2-40-14 | 2026-10-18 | Port onto main from the integration squash of M2-40 on top of M2-39: German chapter names and labels in code, comments and goldens (`requirements_table.go`, `rightschange.go`, `sources.go`) follow the English chapters; the spec additions of M2-40 to 07, 08, 19 and 24 and the terminal README are carried over in English; the ruling table of M2-40 is its own section; the generated files (REST, SDKs, configuration reference, config schema) are regenerated. Tests: the placeholder stubs of `stepup_fresh_test.go` come from `stepup_new_test.go` of main (`stubAudit` for `stubRecorder`), `decide_rules_test.go` no longer expects `terminal_session` without a visibility rule (M2-40 adds it), and German request reasons in tests are English. | The English documents on main win (PR lane task); the integration branch still carries the German chapters. |

## M2-41 Pairing, confirmation and re-pairing of hosts

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-41-1 | 2026-10-15 | A pairing code is stored as HMAC-SHA-256 with the pepper of `auth.token_pepper_ref` over the full code string, the same derivation as `api_tokens.token_hash`; `identity.Tokens.HashHostPairingCode` parses form, checksum and kind `hpc` first and returns `identity.ErrTokenMalformed` before any HMAC. | One derivation for every token of ADR 0061; the prefix `bothy_hpc_` already separates the kinds, so no extra label is hashed. |
| D-M2-41-2 | 2026-10-15 | One issuer for `createHost`, `issueHostPairingCode` and the fallback of `confirmHost`: `app.Service.issueCode` over `PairingCodes.Issue` (bound to `identity.Tokens.NewHostPairingCode`). Case a of the brief: M2-39 already minted `hpc` codes through `domain/apitoken` and the identity port, so its names stay. | The format and the hash derivation exist once. |
| D-M2-41-3 | 2026-10-15 | `pairHost` runs its write as the rights change `host.pair` (with the people of the valid assignments), although 05 gives `host.pair` Permissions `no`. | 03 counts `reported_session_users` as rights state (D-M2-40-9), so the rights version rises first. |
| D-M2-41-4 | 2026-10-15 | A redeemed code leaves `pairing_code_hash` and `pairing_expires_at` NULL. | The index on `pairing_expires_at` serves only `pairing`, and a used code must never match again. |
| D-M2-41-5 | 2026-10-15 | The key and report rules of `pairHost` with their `errors[].code` values: `invalid_key` (Noise key not strict base64url of 32 bytes or a low-order point; JWK `kty`, `crv` or `x` wrong), `unknown_member` (any JWK member besides `kty`, `crv`, `x`), `empty`, `duplicate`, `too_long`, `pattern_mismatch`, `limit_reached`, `invalid_format`, the textcheck rules for labels; every finding is returned, in body order. | The host service is untrusted input, and a host without session users can never give a terminal (HD1). |
| D-M2-41-6 | 2026-10-15 | Only a well-formed prefix (upper-cased, exactly `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` characters of the Crockford alphabet) counts as an attempt; `I`, `L`, `O` and `U` are not mapped. | A typo of the form must not burn an attempt; the client may normalise before sending. |
| D-M2-41-7 | 2026-10-15 | `409 fingerprint_mismatch` of `confirmHost` keeps its effect under an `Idempotency-Key`: the handler calls `idempotency.KeepEffectOnFinal`, and the stage stores the answer with `Store.Complete` in the same unit and commits; only this answer is ever marked, a marked `401` or `5xx` still rolls back. The new problem member `attempts_remaining` is carried by `errs.FingerprintMismatch` through the existing `errs.ProblemExtender`. | Every failed attempt must count also under a key (TM-T19); screen A8 (M4-37) shows the remaining attempts and no other field carries them. |
| D-M2-41-8 | 2026-10-15 | `confirmHost` compares the prefix before anything of the body that needs the database (the people of `session_users`, the credential holder, the assignment rows); it writes one `confirm` audit entry that also carries the declaration, plus one `session_user` entry per assignment, all with the same correlation. A replay with the same key gets the stored answer and never burns a second attempt. | A wrong host must never reach an assignment or a declaration. |
| D-M2-41-9 | 2026-10-15 | The fallback after `terminal.fingerprint_max_attempts` failures: state `pairing`, the three key columns NULL, `key_fingerprint` kept, `pairing_code_hash` the HMAC of a fresh code that is never shown with `pairing_expires_at` now, `fingerprint_failures` 0, the next version, `host.updated` with `key_changed`. | 03 demands a hash exactly in `pairing`, and no code may work until the owner issues one. |
| D-M2-41-10 | 2026-10-15 | Dropping keys keeps `key_fingerprint`; `Host` and `host.updated` omit `key_fingerprint` and `noise_public_key` while the host has no keys; `pairHost` with the retained fingerprint is `409 conflict` `host_key_reused`, with the login key of another host `host_key_in_use`. | Refuses a re-sent key pair (T-TM-05). Honest limit: the server keeps no dropped key, so a host service that keeps one of its two keys is not detected; M2-42 generates both anew. |
| D-M2-41-11 | 2026-10-15 | The visibility of `host` adds: any principal when the requirement of the action is exactly `Public` (only `host.pair`). | The pairing code is the proof, checked by the use case before `authorize`, as for the refresh token. |
| D-M2-41-12 | 2026-10-15 | `key_changed` is set exactly when stored keys were dropped (re-pairing from `confirming`, `active` or `disabled`, and the fallback); `key_generation` rises only at `pairHost`. Audit details: `pairing_code` with `from_state`, `to_state`, `old_fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `sessions_ended`, `reason`; `confirm` with `result`, `fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `attempts_remaining`, `llm_credentials`, `credential_holder_id`, `declared_server_host`, `reason`; `host.pair` with `fingerprint`, `old_fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `platform`. Fingerprints as compact lower-case codes; the host is the resource, not a detail; `from_state` and `to_state` follow M2-39 instead of the brief's `state_before` and `state_after`. | Devices pin keys by generation (24 5.5); one vocabulary for state changes in the audit. |
| D-M2-41-13 | 2026-10-15 | New keys `terminal.report_label_max_chars` (default `100`) and `terminal.report_max_entries` (default `64`) of chapter 24; the start check `fingerprint_keys` requires `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` between 1 and the length of the compact fingerprint and `terminal.fingerprint_max_attempts` at least 1. | Limits only through named keys; M2-43 reuses the report keys for `report`. |
| D-M2-41-14 | 2026-10-15 | The job `terminal.sweep` (and its clause on expired codes) is not built here; an expired code is rejected at redemption, and its hash stays while the host is `pairing`. | No ticket row names the job; follow-up. |
| D-M2-41-15 | 2026-10-15 | Names that differ from the brief: `PairingCodes.Issue` instead of `New`; the port methods take `actor.Actor` and commands carry `terminal.Precondition` (D-M2-39-16); `RepairEffects(state, disabledReason)`; field errors are `errs.FieldError`; `Cache-Control: no-store` comes from the chain for every API answer, not from the handler; `issueHostPairingCode` declares `Idempotency-Key` in the contract and marks its answer as a one-time secret like `createHost` instead of `x-idempotency-exempt`; `TestPairingRoutes_AZP1` is part of `TestAZP1_HostRoutes`; `StorePairing` writes `reported_session_users` with the encoder of the report of M2-40 in the pairing statement instead of calling `ApplySessionUserReport`, which would write a second version and event; the instance id comes from `instance.State.Load`; the test world makes Alex an owner by a fixture row; 08 section 12 already named the kind `hpc`. | The merged code of M1, M2-39 and M2-40 wins over the brief (Boundaries). |
| D-M2-41-16 | 2026-10-15 | The spec chapters on this base are German, so the changes to 02, 06 and 24 are written in German like those of M2-40; the planning rulings D-W-65, D-W-101 and D-W-102 of main change no contract and are not ported. Working directory labels are part of `Host.reported_session_users`, so they appear in the `hosts` row and in `host.updated`; `TestPairingNeverLeaksSecrets` checks the audit, problem bodies and logs for them. | Follow the language of the chapter; the brief's leak list would contradict the Host schema of M2-39. |
| D-M2-41-17 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main from the integration squash of M2-41 on top of M2-40: the spec additions to 02, 06 and 24, the threat model row and ADR 0061 are written into the English documents of main (this supersedes the language part of D-M2-41-16), the terminal README and the requirement label are English, and the generated files (REST, routes, SDKs, configuration reference, config schema) are regenerated. | `main` is English; the integration branch still carries the German chapters. |

## Integration int/m1-wave18

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W18/1 | 2026-10-14 | M1-39 enters as one squash of `82693e5..160c3ce` onto `int/m1-wave17` (`e2f9eb4`). Append-only files are unions: DECISIONS, the server CHANGELOG, `api/operations.yaml` (`loginWithPassword` and `setMyLocalCredential` enabled, neither in `later`), the route table and the stub server of `api/routes/real_test.go`, the vendor groups `v-webauthn` and `v-xcrypto` of identity's adapters (`modules.yaml`, `.go-arch-lint.yml`), the lint exclusions, the privilege table, the identity README and the expected configuration of the wire and CLI tests; `docs/operations/configuration.md` takes M1-39's rows for its keys and the base rows for the passkey keys. sqlc and `go generate` regenerated the models, the REST package, the routes and `config-schema.json`. | Both sides add entries; the generated files are equal to a fresh generation (`make codegen-check`). |
| INT-W18/2 | 2026-10-14 | Semantic conflict: M1-40 (base) and M1-39 each built the conditional step-up of 07 section 4.2 step 8 (`stepUpWaived` with `Input.StepUpWaived` against M1-39's `stepUpCondition` with `Input.NoCredentialYet`). The base mechanism stays; `local_credential.set` joins its table with M1-39's condition `HasEstablishedLocalCredential`, and M1-39's second mechanism is gone. The line of `TestLocalCredentialStepUpOnlyWhenEstablished` that expected `passkey.register` to need step-up "until M1-40" now expects M1-40's rule (no passkey, no step-up). | Two mechanisms for one rule would let them drift.
