## M2-42 Host service: installation, state, doctor and pair

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-42-1 | 2026-10-17 | `pair` and `doctor` run with the identity of the service user: directly when the caller owns the state directory; through the admin endpoint of the running service when an administrator calls them; with the service stopped, on Linux and macOS in a child process with the credentials of the service user, on Windows `doctor` starts the service once with the start argument `--doctor-once` (one full run, its answer on the admin endpoint, exit `0`, no server, no session) and `pair` requires the running service and otherwise exits `2` naming `bothy-host restart`. Every command that needs administrator rights and lacks them exits `2` with `bothy-host <command>: administrator rights required`. | Keys under DPAPI and files owned by the service user can only be written in its context, and places for all must be resolved by the service user (24 2.2 point 6). |
| D-M2-42-2 | 2026-10-17 | Commands `install [--session-user <name>] [--drive-root <drive>] [--drive-root-confirm yes\|no] [--config <path>]`, `session-user add\|remove <name>`, `restart`, `pair --server <url> --code <code> [--ca-file <pem>]`, `doctor`, `run`, `pty-helper --session-user <name>`; exit codes `0`, `1` (a failed check, a runtime failure, a bound name), `2` (usage or environment), `78` (refused start, `EX_CONFIG`). The drive root prompt reads the terminal; the flag answers it, and without both the answer is no. Local output of `doctor`: one line per entry in the order HD1 to HD11 (`HD<n> ok`, `WARN <code>`, `FAIL <code>` with `session_user=`, `user=`, `drive=`, `item=`), then `doctor: <failed> failed, <warnings> warnings` counting checks, then `declaration: <value>`. | 24 2.2 "Output on the host" names the content, not the form; `78` is what no service definition restarts. |
| D-M2-42-3 | 2026-10-17 | One YAML configuration file nested like the key names, decoded strictly with `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3` (unknown and duplicate keys and wrong types name the key); durations in the grammar of the server. Paths: Linux `/etc/bothy-host/config.yaml`, macOS `/Library/Application Support/bothy-host/config.yaml`, Windows `%ProgramFiles%\bothy-host\config.yaml`. New key `host_service.auth_status_timeout` (default `30s`) bounds `claude auth status` and the login shell probe. An invalid file makes `run` log `config_invalid` with the key and exit `78`; the other commands exit `2`. | 24 and 16 named keys but no file; the bound of the probe was missing; a service manager must not loop on a broken file. |
| D-M2-42-4 | 2026-10-17 | The state directory also holds `pairing.json` (server URL, host ID, instance ID, fingerprint) and `declaration.json` (the last `llm_credentials` of `ready`), both without secrets, and on Linux and macOS `admin.sock`; every file is written atomically with mode `0600`, on Windows inheriting the protected DACL. | `run` needs the server and the declaration before a login; neither is a secret or a subscription login. |
| D-M2-42-5 | 2026-10-17 | No cgo on any platform. Every platform behaviour sits behind the interfaces of `internal/hostos`; an ACL entry that cannot be read, parsed or mapped counts as granting every right to every session user. The mode of a symbolic link is ignored; a link is checked by its owner and its folder. macOS ACLs are read from `/bin/ls -led`, because `golang.org/x/sys/unix` has no decoder for `ATTR_CMN_EXTENDED_SECURITY`. | One right too many only blocks, one too few opens a place; Linux and macOS do not use the mode of a link. |
| D-M2-42-6 | 2026-10-17 | The codes of `doctor` are the table "Codes of `doctor`" in 24 2.2. HD3 has no probe on Windows and passes without code. HD9 lists the homes of `root`, the service user, every former session user and every person account (Linux `UID_MIN` to `UID_MAX` of `/etc/login.defs` with the documented defaults, macOS `UniqueID` from 501, Windows every profile under `ProfileList`); all other system accounts are skipped. | Elevation on Windows needs administrator credentials and HD2 covers `Administrators`; system homes such as `/usr/sbin` or `/var/empty` are listable by design and hold no files of a person. |
| D-M2-42-7 | 2026-10-17 | Path resolution walks the path like `realpath`, records every folder of the result, every component as written (`..` kept) and every link with its folder; a missing path, a dangling link, a closed folder or more than 40 links is unresolvable. Identity is `dev:ino`, on Windows the volume serial number and the 128-bit file ID. | 24 2.2 point 6; a component that resolution skips is still a place a person could change. |
| D-M2-42-8 | 2026-10-17 | HD10 adds the code `helper_unreachable` (named with the session user) for a helper whose channel cannot be opened; a setuid or setgid bit on the binary is `helper_untrusted`; a `#!` script checks its interpreter, and for `/usr/bin/env` the first name the search list finds; a missing interpreter makes `host_service.claude_bin` count as empty. | An unreachable helper gives no result, and missing results must not pass (fail closed). |
| D-M2-42-9 | 2026-10-17 | `auth_status_unknown` (a value outside the six methods, no JSON, a timeout or an exit code other than `0` and `1`) counts like a finding; before any `ready` a finding is the warning `declaration_unknown`. | RK-14: an output that cannot be read fails closed; without a declaration no session can start, so a refusal would only hide the names. |
| D-M2-42-10 | 2026-10-17 | The chain files stay `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.claude` and `.git`: the memory page (retrieved 2026-10-17) names CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md, `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, AGENTS.md and `.claude/AGENTS.md` above the working directory, all inside the list; the CLI reference confirms the JSON and the methods of `claude auth status`. | Brief design notes 9 a and 10. |
| D-M2-42-11 | 2026-10-17 | Peer check: the helper passes one end of a `socketpair` over the activated connection (`SCM_RIGHTS`) and speaks only after the first message of the service; the service compares uid and PID of that end with the session user and the main PID of the helper service; on Windows the pipe is created with `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE` and `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`. The list of platforms with a proven peer check is empty; HD10 fails with `peer_check_unavailable` until a recorded platform run adds a platform; only the build of `make platform-test` measures it. | With socket activation the peer of a connection is the service manager; RK-23 allows no terminal without proof. |
| D-M2-42-12 | 2026-10-17 | The entry scan runs per session user in its helper after the full run; until its first scan ends the user fails HD11 with `entry_scan_running`, afterwards the previous finding holds until a new full scan ends; a finished scan sends a new report only after the start was not refused. | 24 2.2 "Check of the entries"; a refused start sends exactly one report. |
| D-M2-42-13 | 2026-10-17 | Installation: program folders `/opt/bothy-host/bin/`, `/Library/Application Support/bothy-host/bin/`, `%ProgramFiles%\bothy-host\`; Linux `useradd --system --user-group` for the service user and `useradd --create-home --user-group` per session user; macOS hidden users through `dscl` (service user from UniqueID 400, session users from 501, own primary group, not in `staff`) and `createhomedir`, the binary signed ad hoc with the hardened runtime unless it already carries the runtime flag; Windows the virtual account `NT SERVICE\bothy-host` and per session user a local account with a random password handed only to the service control manager, `SeServiceLogonRight`, `CreateProfile` and an ACL only for the user and the trusted owners. | 24 2.2 asks the build ticket for the way per platform; the platform suite proves it. |
| D-M2-42-14 | 2026-10-17 | Exit policy: systemd `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartPreventExitStatus=78`, no `NoNewPrivileges=` and no option implying it; launchd `KeepAlive` `{Crashed: true}` with `GOTRACEBACK=crash`; Windows failure actions on crashes only and a refused start stopped with the service-specific code `78`; the DACL of a helper service gives authenticated users only query rights. | Under no_new_privs `sudo -n true` fails and hides a sudo rule from HD3; a refused start must not loop. |
| D-M2-42-15 | 2026-10-17 | `run` reads the stored declaration, runs the full check, logs in through `Control` when paired, stores the declaration of `ready` and evaluates HD6 again; a "start refused" finding sends exactly one report, closes and exits `78`; unpaired or without login the stored declaration decides. `HostReady` gains the required member `llm_credentials`, and the server sends `ready` again on every change of the declaration. This ticket wires `NoControl`, which M2-43 replaces. | 24 2.2 "Message on a refused start"; the host service learns the declaration only from `ready`. |
| D-M2-42-16 | 2026-10-17 | `pair` accepts only `https://`, trusts the system roots plus `--ca-file`, sends only public keys and the lists after HD7, stores keys and pairing record only after a `201` whose fingerprint equals its own computation, prints the fingerprint and the host ID, and as an administrator restarts the service afterwards; problem answers print the code, the reason, each field error and for `401` the hint to ask for a new code. | 24 3.2; a stolen or wrong answer must not replace working keys. |
| D-M2-42-17 | 2026-10-17 | The admin endpoint answers the ops `doctor.run` (full run, report, the local output and the report entries) and `pair.run`; on Linux and macOS it is `admin.sock` in the state directory and answers only uid `0`, on Windows the pipe `bothy-host-admin` with a DACL for `SYSTEM` and `Administrators` answers only elevated clients; a client checks that the server process runs as the service user before it sends anything. | Design notes 1 and 15. |
| D-M2-42-18 | 2026-10-17 | The helper keeps its end of the passed descriptor open until the client closed the connection. | On macOS a descriptor in flight whose last reference the sender closes was lost in repeated runs (reproduced in `TestPeerCheckPasses`). |
| D-M2-42-19 | 2026-10-17 | Steering ruling 2026-10-15 (no cost): the ticket is built with unit and integration tests only; the platform suite never runs on the build machine, a workstation or shared infrastructure. It is the manual or scheduled workflow `host-platform.yml` on free GitHub-hosted runners (`ubuntu-latest` with systemd, `macos-latest`, `windows-latest`), which are throwaway machines; Windows 10 has no hosted runner and stays unmeasured. Until measured, the Windows values of 24 2.2 point 6 apply, the default of `host_service.entry_scan_timeout` stays `10m`, the platform suite is a required gate before M2-48, and the terminal stays off on every unproven platform (RK-23). | The owner's ruling; the brief asked to stop without machines. |
| D-M2-42-20 | 2026-10-17 | Differences from the brief: the merged M2-38 code had no command table entries and German docs; `docs/RISKS.md` does not exist on the integration base, so RK-23, RK-84, RK-88 and RK-90 are not updated here; the server integration test fronts the HTTP harness with a TLS reverse proxy, because the harness listens without TLS; `createHost` already answers with a first pairing code, which `issueHostPairingCode` replaces. | Follow the merged code (brief Boundaries). |
| D-M2-42-21 | 2026-10-17 | The root `Makefile` runs `go vet` (typecheck, lint) and golangci-lint (lint) for `GO_CROSS_MODULES := services/host` with `GOOS` `linux`, `darwin` and `windows`. | Code for the other platforms is checked on every build host. |
| D-M2-42-22 | 2026-10-17 | Windows channels: clients open pipes with `SECURITY_SQOS_PRESENT` and `SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION`; the pipe DACLs add `OWNER RIGHTS` with only `FILE_CREATE_PIPE_INSTANCE`; a pipe server reads the token of its client by impersonating it at identification level, and a client compares the owner of the pipe (the account that created the server end) and `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` with the session user and the main PID, because the token of a helper process gives the service account no access; the process DACL of a helper gives the service account and `OWNER RIGHTS` only `PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION`; `run` and `pty-helper` run under the service control manager from the first step. | Review of M2-42: without these the second connection, the peer check in both directions and the exit code `78` failed on Windows, and the owner of a helper pipe kept `WRITE_DAC`. |
| D-M2-42-23 | 2026-10-17 | HD4 on Windows also accepts `SYSTEM` or `Administrators` as owner of the state directory when its DACL holds only the three principals; an existing local account whose helper service exists keeps its password at a repeated `install` or `session-user add`. | Handing the folder to the virtual account needs a restore privilege; a password reset would lose the data DPAPI protects for the user. |
| D-M2-42-24 | 2026-10-17 | HD6 also reads the folder the login shell exports in `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (inside the helper only): a `.credentials.json` there is a finding, and `claude auth status` also runs with it. A `host_service.claude_bin` that fails HD10 counts as empty and never runs. An entry whose owner the entry scan cannot read counts as `foreign_entry`. The runner starts each program in its own process group, ends the group on the bound and stops waiting for the output shortly after. | Review of M2-42: a login under an exported folder went unseen, an untrusted program ran in every session user, the scan failed open, and a background job of a login shell stretched the bound. |
| D-M2-42-25 | 2026-10-17 | The host module carries `nosemgrep` markers for the rule `bothy-go-egress-only` at the Unix socket dialers of the local channel and at the HTTP client of `pair`, and for `bothy-go-no-model-credentials` in the fake `claude` of `testdata`. | The egress package belongs to the server module and cannot be imported; the dialers reach only Unix sockets on the host; `pair` calls only the https URL the operator gives, with the system roots plus `--ca-file`, no proxy and no redirect; the fake reads only its fixture folder. |
| D-M2-42-26 | 2026-10-17 | Every helper call has a bound: `check.run` four times `host_service.auth_status_timeout` plus a grace of the channel, `scan.run` `host_service.entry_scan_timeout` plus the grace; a call that runs out counts as `helper_unreachable` or `entry_scan_timeout` for that session user. A chain file that is a link counts as its target: the target, its folders and links and, for a folder, everything in it belong to no other session user; a dangling one fails. HD10 never opens a `host_service.claude_bin` that failed the trust rule, and `Head` reads only regular files. | Red team of M2-42: a session user could stop its helper and hold the start of the whole host, a link of root into a file of another person passed HD11 (TM-T29 (j)), and a FIFO at an untrusted `claude_bin` blocked the run. |
| D-M2-42-27 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main from the integration squash of M2-42 on top of M2-41: the chapter additions of 02, 16 and 24, ADR 0068 and AsyncAPI are written into the English documents of main; in 16 section 4.4 main's text stays and the build facts of the ticket follow it as a list "Implementation (M2-42)", because main's section is newer than the base of the ticket; main's usage text of `bothy-host` (tabwriter) stays; the root `Makefile` keeps main's lint call without the contract build tags and adds the per-GOOS loop; the generated REST client of M8-10 is not taken (not on main); `TestHostPairAgainstServer` runs against the HTTP chain of `test/terminalworld` instead of the full-server harness of M1-71, which is not on main (same steps and assertions); the platform workflow names the fixed runner labels `ubuntu-24.04`, `macos-15` and `windows-2025` that main's runner gate requires instead of the `-latest` labels. | `main` is English and newer than the integration base; only the ticket's own changes are ported. |
| D-M2-42-28 | 2026-10-19 | Fixes after the pull request review: the entry scan reads the env snapshot of its run (a data race with `SetDeclaration`, found by the race detector in CI); `install --session-user` applies the checks of `session-user add` (`CheckNewSessionUser`: pattern, letter case, binding); booleans of the configuration decode every YAML spelling of `!!bool`; the platform suite reads `systemctl show` as key and value; on Windows the PID query opens the service control manager with `SC_MANAGER_CONNECT` and the service with `SERVICE_QUERY_STATUS`, a repeated install moves the running image aside before it copies the new one, and the group list of a session user adds the well-known logon SIDs (INTERACTIVE, SERVICE, LOCAL, CONSOLE LOGON, This Organization, Local account). | Each finding reads true on the code; the Windows changes are checked by `go vet` and the linters for Windows only, the platform suite proves them on a Windows host. |
| D-M2-42-29 | 2026-10-19 | Three review findings stay open until the Windows platform run (before M2-48): `Close` of a pipe listener does not end an `Accept` that waits in `ConnectNamedPipe` on a synchronous handle (the pipe DACLs do not let the listener open its own pipe to wake it, so it needs an overlapped handle or `CancelSynchronousIo`), reads on a synchronous pipe handle do not end when `Conn.Call` closes the connection at its deadline (overlapped handles would end them), and the impostor of `TestPlatform_TMT26` on Windows is created by the administrator of the suite, not by the session user. | Both need a Windows host to change and to prove; the platform suite is the gate for Windows and runs only there. |
| D-M2-42-30 | 2026-10-20 | `sonar.coverage.exclusions` leaves out `services/host/**/*_windows.go` and `services/host/**/*_darwin.go`. | The SonarQube scan runs on Linux and reads the coverage of `go test` there; files with a Windows or macOS build constraint never compile in that run, so they counted as uncovered new code, and the first scan failed the coverage condition of the Quality Gate. Their tests belong to the host platform suite (D-M2-42-19), as for the native plugin code of D-M4-10-7. Measured in a Linux run, the Linux files of the change stay above that condition. |

The waits are for things that must happen, so they take the one patience instead of their own seconds. |

## M2-01 Worker consumers of the event log

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-01-1 | 2026-10-12 | No migration seeds `event_consumer_cursors`. In the roles `worker` and `all`, `ConsumerService.Ensure` inserts the row of every registered consumer in one unit of work before the runner starts, with `INSERT ... SELECT $1, COALESCE(max(seq), 0) FROM event_log ON CONFLICT (name) DO NOTHING`; unregistered names have no row and no lag series. The column default `0` of 03 stays. Place: `00007_event_consumer_cursors.sql`, `queries/consumers.sql`, 02 11.5 item 5. | A seeded row for a consumer that arrives in a later release (`push`, M3-19) would make it replay every event retained since the migration; a new consumer starts at the head like the first connection of an agent consumer (04 15.1 item 2). Reading `max(seq)` in one statement is safe because a reader that sees `seq = k` sees every smaller number (04 3.2 item 3); two workers starting together run the same statement and one row wins. |
| D-M2-01-2 | 2026-10-12 | `domain.PlanRound`: numbers contiguous from `last_seq + 1` are processed; when `last_seq + 1` lies below `min(seq)` and the first number read equals `min(seq)`, the events in between were removed by retention, the round processes from `min(seq)`, logs `event consumer lost events to retention` once at error level with `bothy.consumer.name` and `bothy.event.seq` and continues; every other jump, hole or repeat is `ErrConsumerGap`, the round rolls back and the consumer stalls. `min(seq)` is read in the round's transaction only when the first number does not follow the cursor. Place: `domain/consumer.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 6. | The removed events cannot come back and stalling would not help; a webhook subscription detects its own gap through `last_delivered_seq` (11 6), and the alarm fires long before. 04 3.2 items 3 and 4 rule out other holes, so they are bugs; unlike the tail's `ErrGap` the worker does not exit, the cursor just never passes the hole. The round holds `ACCESS SHARE` on `event_log`, so no partition is detached between the reads. |
| D-M2-01-3 | 2026-10-12 | A handler error, a handler panic (recovered into kind internal, the panic value is not kept), a database error and `ErrConsumerGap` roll back the whole round: no job, no cursor move, the next attempt at the next wake or poll; no event is ever skipped. The runner logs the first failure after a success at error level (`event consumer round failed`, `bothy.consumer.name`, `error.type` as SQLSTATE, `consumer_gap`, `timeout`, `canceled` or the `kernel/errs` kind) and the next success at info level (`event consumer recovered`). Each consumer has its own goroutine, so a stalled one never holds back another. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`, 02 11.5 item 7. | One failing event must stall only its consumer, visibly through the gauge and the alarm, rather than lose an event; edge-triggered lines keep a database outage, during which the worker pauses (02 14.4), from flooding the log. |
| D-M2-01-4 | 2026-10-12 | Before the cursor moves, the round asks the appender of `sync/app` (its per-unit `txn.Key` buffer, `bufferedEvents`) whether the unit buffered durable events; if so it fails with kind internal and the unit rolls back. Place: `app/appender.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 8. | The consumer would read its own output, and the round would take the `event_seq` counter lock and lengthen the serialized commit phase (02 11.7); ADR 0051 lets a round insert jobs only. |
| D-M2-01-5 | 2026-10-12 | Two observable gauges, `bothy.event.consumer.lag` (`{event}`, `head_seq - last_seq`, floored at zero) and `bothy.event.consumer.pending_age` (`s`, now from `kernel/clock` minus `occurred_at` of the oldest event after the cursor, zero at the head), with the only attribute `bothy.consumer.name` from the closed set `sync.ConsumerNames()`; one callback calls `Consumers.States` (one read of all rows with the head, then one read per row, bounded by `database.statement_timeout`), and a failed read drops the points. Every role registers them in start step 6b. The registry `internal/platform/otel/metrics` of M1-68 is not merged, so they live in `internal/sync/adapters/otelmetrics` with the meter of the global provider; `sync` already had the `otel` vendor group. `bothy.consumer.name` is in `otel/attr`, the collector allowlist and signals.md 5.3. Place: `adapters/otelmetrics/consumers.go`, signals.md 4.2. | The plan row asks for `head_seq` minus `last_seq`, but `sync.event_retention` is a time window, so the alarm needs the age too. A stopped or missing worker must still show a growing lag, so the api role exports it as well; telemetry never blocks (17 rule 4). |
| D-M2-01-6 | 2026-10-12 | Alarm `BothyEventConsumerLag`, severity `page`, `max by (bothy_consumer_name) (bothy_event_consumer_pending_age_seconds{job="bothy/bothy-server"}) > 900` for `5m`, on the new key `consumer.pending_alert_after` (place Server, duration, default `15m`, at least `1s`). The start check `consumer_alert_below_retention` requires it strictly below `sync.event_retention` and names both keys and no value. The rule file holds the number; `alert-thresholds` keeps it equal to the default. Place: `bothy-alerts.yaml`, alerting.md 3.2 and 4, 02 13 item 4 and 22, `configkeys_consumer.go`, `configchecks.go`. | A normal worker restart takes at most `shutdown.total_timeout` (`60s`) plus a few `consumer.poll_interval` (`1s`), far below `15m`, and the `for` of the neighbouring lag alarm adds five minutes; `15m` is far below the `30d` retention, so the operator has weeks before any event is lost. `page`, because a standing consumer stops pushes and outgoing webhooks, which people notice at once (alerting.md 1). |
| D-M2-01-7 | 2026-10-12 | One `LISTEN event_log` connection per process for all worker consumers, opened with `pg.Pool.DialDedicated(ctx, "bothy-consumer-wake")`, apart from the tail's connection; the listening loop of the M1-16 tail moved into the shared type `listener` in `adapters/postgres`, which both use. A notification wakes every consumer through a channel of capacity one with a non-blocking send; the payload is not read. The connection is reconnected with exponential backoff from `consumer.poll_interval` up to `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap`, and every consumer also polls every `consumer.poll_interval`. Without a registered consumer the runner starts nothing and opens no connection. Place: `adapters/postgres/listen.go`, `waker.go`, 02 11.5 item 9. | In role `all` the ordered loop of the tail must never depend on the consumers; polling covers a lost connection and nothing is lost because the cursor lives in the database (02 14.4). The production list is empty in M2-01, and an idle connection per worker would serve nobody. |
| D-M2-01-8 | 2026-10-12 | After a full batch (`consumer.batch_size` events) the next round of that consumer starts at once (drain); after a short batch, an idle round, a locked row or a failed round the consumer waits for the next wake or poll. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`. | A backlog drains without waiting a poll per batch, while `consumer.batch_size` keeps each round, and with it the `ACCESS SHARE` lock that a detach of the partition maintenance waits for, short. |
| D-M2-01-9 | 2026-10-12 | The stop step `event_consumers` lies in `lifecycle.PhaseConsumers` (before River's phase) with budget `0`, the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout`: the runner starts no new round and waits for the running ones; when the budget runs out the round's context is cancelled and the round rolls back. An unexpected end of the runner (the waker ends early, a panic outside a handler) goes through `Failed()` to `lifecycle.Runner.FailOn`, and the process exits `1` like after an unexpected stop of the River client. Place: `wire/sync.go`, `wire/serve.go`, 02 14.5, 15.2 and 15.3. | 02 15.3 gives this step no own budget; either way no job is lost or doubled, because cursor and jobs commit together. A process whose consumers ended would look healthy while pushes and webhooks stop. |

New text in the German chapters (02, alerting.md, signals.md, ADR 0051, threat model) is written in English. The tests take two hooks of `wire.Deps`: `ConsumerHandlers` (replaces the empty production list in worker and all) and `ConsumerGauges` (replaces the registration on the global meter provider), because `otel.Setup` installs one global provider per process and parallel serve tests would overwrite it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done") and requires English output. The hooks follow the pattern of `Deps.Registrations` and let the serve tests prove that worker and all run a registered consumer and that role api exports the gauges without a runner. |

The same renumbering as the integration (ADR 0007 rule 4). |

## M4-03 Accent variants

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-03-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The tool's ruff configuration selects no `D` rules; the new code carries docstrings anyway, no lint rule is added. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request. |
| D-M4-03-1 | 2026-10-14 | The rules member `accent_variants` of `design/design-rules.json` names the default (`spruce`), the seven accents of 01 section 12 and the eight swapped roles. `tokenkit/accents.py` validates it (default in the accents, every accent a palette scale, every role an opaque colour in both themes) and raises a `RuleError` naming the member. A role is swapped in a theme only where it aliases `palette.spruce.<step>` directly; otherwise it is kept and shown as "not swapped". | One place for the list the server, the apps and the gate share; a role that does not follow the brand scale (dark `color.focus.ring` on `gorse`) must not change with the accent. |
| D-M4-03-2 | 2026-10-14 | Step search: a variant starts on the same step of the chosen scale (nearest step by number if missing, a tie goes darker in light and lighter in dark) and, while one of its pairings fails, moves one step towards more contrast (darker when the role is the darker colour of the failing pair, else lighter) until all pass; a scale end, a flat scale or a revisited step fails the build with `accent <a>, theme <t>: no step of <scale> meets <pairing>`. `moved_from` records the start step and `contrast.md` shows it. With today's palette no variant moves. | A fixed step on a lighter scale cannot always meet AA, while 01 section 12 promises that every listed accent works; the moved steps stay visible. |
| D-M4-03-3 | 2026-10-14 | `DsAccent` is a separate `ThemeExtension` with one constant per theme and accent (`lightSpruce` ... `darkMoss`) and `DsAccent.of(key, brightness)`, an unknown key giving the default; it is not a fourth resolver modifier. `DsColors` keeps its fields. In CSS the light/dark default theme block of an accent also covers an element without `data-theme`, and a `prefers-color-scheme` block covers the other theme, as for the base roles; `data-accent` sits on the same element as `data-theme`. | The tool allows a token to depend on one modifier only, and an accent role always combines with the theme; existing code keeps compiling. |
| D-M4-03-4 | 2026-10-14 | Guard scope: the palette guard "Brand and accent" keeps checking the base roles only; accent variants of an instance are not checked against it. The Discord guard covers every scale. Recorded below the ban list of the design README. | 01 section 12 allows `heather`, `rowan` and `amber` deliberately as the instance owner's choice from the checked palette, while the product brand stays spruce; contrast applies to the variants in full. |
| D-M4-03-5 | 2026-10-14 | An accent without a passing step is reported as a failed gate (exit 1, nothing written, the other gate messages still listed); an invalid `accent_variants` member stays an error of the rules file (exit 2), as for other rule errors. | The brief's test asks for exit 1 when a variant falls below AA; an invalid rules file is a different failure. |

## M7-19 API reference in the docs site

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M7-19/0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. Rulings use the row form of this file (`M7-19/<n>` under a ticket heading) instead of the brief's `D-M7-19-<n>` in section 10, as the other ported tickets do. The two `__init__` methods of the hook carry docstrings because main's ruff configuration requires them (rule `D107`). | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| M7-19/1 | 2026-10-14 | Generator (Design note 1): an in-repository MkDocs hook, `tools/docs/mkdocs_api_reference.py`, renders both documents to static Markdown with the pinned `pyyaml` and `mkdocs`; no new dependency. Compared with the pinned Redocly CLI 2.62.1 `build-docs` (plus the AsyncAPI HTML template for the gateway). Evidence: `redocly build-docs docs/api/openapi.yaml` rendered the OpenAPI 3.1 document without errors, but its default page loads `redoc.standalone.js` from `cdn.redocly.com` and a Google Fonts stylesheet, is one 6.9 MB HTML file outside the site's navigation and search, and renders no AsyncAPI; the gateway would need `@asyncapi/cli` and its template as a further npm tree (not in `tools/node`, not tried). The hook renders the 304 operations, 35 webhook events, 560 schemas and 93 gateway messages offline, with no script, in the site's theme, search and strict link and anchor validation; Apache-2.0 as the repository; maintained here. This replaces the suggestion `redocly build-docs` of ADR 0045. | Requirement 9 and 13 (no CDN, no external asset) and requirement 12 (both references in every build); the static hook meets them without bundling a JavaScript application. |
| M7-19/2 | 2026-10-14 | Generated pages live only in the build: `api/reference/rest.md`, `api/reference/schemas.md` and `api/reference/gateway.md` are added as generated files in `on_files` and listed in the nav under API; nothing is committed. A committed file at one of these paths, a missing document, an unresolved local `$ref` or two equal anchors fail the build. Anchors are `op-<operationid lower case>`, `schema-<Name>`, `response-<Name>`, `header-<Name>`, `security-<name>`, `channel-<key>`, `operation-<key>` and `message-<key>` (characters other than letters, digits, `_` and `-` become `-`). Text of the documents is used as Markdown with `<` escaped outside code spans; a code span wrapped over two lines is joined. `docs/api/README.md` names the site paths in code, not as links, because the pages do not exist for `make linkcheck`. | Requirement 12: a stale generated file cannot be committed; the strict build validates every generated link and anchor. |
| M7-19/3 | 2026-10-14 | External asset rule: `tools/docs/external_assets_check.py site` runs in `make docs` after the build and fails on a `<script src>`, a `<link href>` with rel `stylesheet`, `preload`, `modulepreload` or `prefetch`, or a CSS `url(...)` or `@import` (in `<style>`, `style` attributes and `.css` files) whose URL names a host. Anchors and links that load nothing (`rel="canonical"`) pass, `data:` URLs pass, an empty site fails. Requests a bundled script makes at run time are out of its reach; the known one is Material's Mermaid loader (CDN, accepted in ADR 0045 "Consequences"), which the reference pages do not use. | Requirement 13 in the form the brief gives (a check over the built HTML); the run-time loader is an existing, recorded decision and outside this ticket's scope. |
| M7-19/4 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held: `test_external_assets_check.py` and `test_mkdocs_api_reference.py` were written and run red (module not found) before the check and the hook existed. | Honest record of the order of work. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

| D-M2-24-0 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main: code and tests are taken from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave19` with the integration fixes that belong to this ticket: INT-W19/4 (`CheckAppScope` takes the token scopes, `Scope` and `AdminScope` pass them, `TestScope_AppTokenScopes`), INT-W19/5 (`NewApps` uses the structure source of chat and the host source of terminal, both principal writers stay in `wire.Identity`), INT-W19/7 (`AppsDeps.Grants` is the Admin service of the authz step, the handlers of apps join through `(*Apps).fillHandlers`), the apps part of INT-W19/8 (`test/appsworld` with the identity keys, the instance settings, the full Admin service and the rows of its space and channels; `TestAuthenticateAPIToken_AppInstallation` sets `DevicePendingTTL`) and INT-W19/9 (`RevokeSubjectGrants` appends one `grant.changed` with `change: revoked` per revoked grant). The spec additions are written into the English chapters (02, 07, 08, 11, `configuration.md`, `threat-model.md`) at the same places; code comments, SQL comments and the module READMEs name the English chapter files and headings, so the checksums of both migrations are recorded anew. Every example that shows a manifest uses the new form. | The YAML lacked members that 11 3.1, 3.2 and 03 `apps.manifest` require; the change that finds the contradiction aligns the YAML with the chapter (SPEC 13 point 5), and the rules can only name a refused value they receive. |
| D-M2-24-3 | 2026-10-13 | Validation pipeline: the request validation checks the schema first (`unknown_member`, `invalid_enum`, ... as `400 validation_failed`, also for `validateManifest`); then the handler hands the manifest bytes as received to the pure `domain.ValidateManifest` with an `Env` of catalogue facts and configuration. `validateManifest` answers `200` with `valid` and every rule error relative to the manifest root, stores nothing and writes no audit entry; `installApp` and `upgradeAppManifest` answer `400 validation_failed` with the pointers under `/manifest`, or `400 webhook_host_not_allowed` when every error is a host error. Codes come from the M1-18 vocabulary (`required`, `invalid`, `too_large`, `too_long`, `too_many_items`), plus `webhook_host_not_allowed` and `command_name_reserved` where 06 and 09 name the reason; the detail names the rule, never the value. | SEC-IN-1 checks the shape before any rule; one rule function serves the dry run and the install, so both report the same errors. |
| D-M2-24-4 | 2026-10-13 | Subscribable events: a durable type of the catalogue without audience `self` whose one receive action is in the scope table (with a scope or none), or `interaction.created` and `command.invoked` (receivers `agent_or_app`, scope `events:subscribe`); every other type is refused (`access.changed`, `draft.*`, `agent.invocation`, `grant.changed`, `host.updated`, ...). The scope of the receive action must be requested, and `events:subscribe` whenever `events` is not empty. `delivery` is required with events, commands or components; `events_url` required for `webhook` and forbidden otherwise; `interactions_url` required for commands or components without the gateway; `data_egress` required with events or `interactions_url`. Manifest commands: at most `command.max_per_principal`, names unique without regard to case and not reserved, description at most `command.description_max_chars`, option names unique, no target (`scope_kind` absent or `instance`, no `scope_id`). | 11 3.1 and 3.2 rules 1, 2 and 5; 05 2.6 row "Event subscriptions"; 11 4.1: a manifest never names a target. |
| D-M2-24-5 | 2026-10-13 | Delivery addresses are absolute `https` URLs with a host, without user information and fragment, whose host (lower case, without port) equals an entry of `webhook.allowed_hosts` without regard to case and without wildcards; otherwise the manifest is refused with `webhook_host_not_allowed`. `homepage_url` must be absolute `https`, its host is not compared because the server never loads it. 11 3.2 rule 3 now says that the operator adds a missing host to the process configuration. | `webhook.allowed_hosts` is process configuration that changes only at start (configuration.md 1, 03 9); the consent cannot add a host "in the same confirmed action" (spec gap, sentence changed in 11). |

An app whose installations are all uninstalled is reinstalled: same principal, status back to `active`, display name and manifest columns replaced, a new installation; its revoked tokens and grants stay revoked. A handle taken by another principal and a concurrent install of the same slug answer `409 conflict`. | 11 4.1 and 03 6.3; reusing the principal keeps the authorship of the app's messages (11 4.3) and the unique `lower(handle)`. |
| D-M2-24-7 | 2026-10-13 | `upgradeAppManifest` (`app.install`): same `slug`; changed content needs another `version` (a lower one is allowed, rollback); an identical manifest with identical lists is without effect (`200`, no rights version, audit `no_change`); granted scopes and subscriptions are subsets of the current ones (new ones need `setAppScopes`), so what the new manifest drops acts at once; the upgrade never adds a grant; it is itself the consent for new delivery hosts (owner, step-up), which must be in `webhook.allowed_hosts` and are named in `delivery_hosts_added`. | 11 4.2 (changed: "New scopes and events need a new consent"); `manifest_version` names the content in the console and the audit; a host change is consented by the step-up action that uploads it. |

| D-M2-24-9 | 2026-10-13 | Calls without effect are decided before the rights change: `upgradeAppManifest`, `setAppScopes`, `suspendApp`, `resumeApp` and `uninstallApp` first load in a unit without rights change and, when nothing changes, write the allow entry with `no_change: true` there; otherwise the rights change locks `apps` and the installation and checks again (a concurrent equal change ends as no change). `suspendApp` and `resumeApp` of an uninstalled app answer `409 invalid_state_transition`; `setAppScopes` and `upgradeAppManifest` of an uninstalled app `409 conflict`. | 02 8.1: phase 1 of a rights change always raises `permission_version`, and R-API-27 forbids a rights version for a no-op (the M1-29 pattern). |
| D-M2-24-10 | 2026-10-13 | App tokens and the scope check: `createToken` for an app locks the active installation FOR SHARE (none, suspended or uninstalled: `409 conflict` `app_not_installed`); scopes absent or empty take the granted scopes at this moment, present ones must be granted (`400` at `/scopes/<i>`). The authenticator reads the installation of an app token on every request; an app request needs the action's scope in `GrantedScopes` and in the token scopes (`actor.Scopes`); without a token (webhook delivery) the installation alone decides. A removed scope acts on the next request; a scope granted later needs a new token. | Decides the open points of 08 12; replaces the part of M1-28 D6 that token scopes are not evaluated. The share lock serializes with an uninstall that holds the installation FOR UPDATE, so no live token is created for an uninstalled app. |
| D-M2-24-11 | 2026-10-13 | The installation is part of the credential: the API token path (REST and the gateway `identify`) applies the installation check, `Revalidate` fails with `app_not_installed` when the installation is no longer active or no longer grants a scope the actor carries, and the rights snapshot of an app without an active installation is empty (`PrincipalFacts.AppInstallationActive`), so suspend and uninstall write `access.changed` for the app's targets and resume adds them back. Known gap of the core contract (04 8.2): a change of the granted scopes alone changes no visible target, writes no `access.changed`, and an open gateway connection learns it only at its next `identify`, `resume` or `Revalidate`; REST and webhook deliveries see it at once. `TestAppAccessChanged_SuspendResumeUninstall` asserts the gap so a later fix shows up. | Answers "how long-lived connections recheck the state of the installation" (08 12); the gateway (M1-64, M1-65) is not on the base, so its test waits for those tickets. |

The new start step `apps` (6e) runs after `identity`. | The domain imports only the kernel core (02 5); the app layer turns facts and configuration into `domain.Env`. |
| D-M2-24-13 | 2026-10-13 | `listApps` lists apps whose latest installation is `active` or `suspended`, ordered by `lower(slug)` then principal id, keyset cursor bound to the caller; `getApp` returns any app, uninstalled included, with its latest installation. `listApps` and `validateManifest` authorize `app.read` on the collection of apps (resource kind `app` without id): authz treats that collection as visible like the instance, so a non-owner gets `403 forbidden` with the reason `owner_only`; one app stays invisible to a non-owner (`404`, AZ-P4). | 06 4 point 2 (container check); the catalogue gives `app.read` only the kind `app`, so the collection is that kind without an id (`TestAppCollection_OwnerOnly`). |
| D-M2-24-14 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details (flat, ids, codes, integers, booleans and lists of codes; never names, descriptions, addresses, `data_egress` or the manifest): install `app_id`, `installation_id`, `slug`, `manifest_sha256` (lower-case hex), `granted_scopes`, `event_subscriptions`, `delivery_hosts`, `reinstall`, `reason`; upgrade adds `previous_manifest_sha256` and the added and removed manifest scopes, events and commands, `delivery_hosts_added`, `granted_scopes_removed`, `event_subscriptions_removed`; scope change the added and removed scopes and subscriptions; suspend and resume `app_id`, `installation_id`; uninstall also `tokens_revoked`, `grants_revoked`, `reason`; `no_change: true` for a call without effect. `manifest_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the manifest bytes as received, `apps.manifest` keeps the parsed content as jsonb.
