The trigger `apps_principal_kind_check` (SQLSTATE `23514`) refuses an `apps` row whose principal is not of kind `app`. | Rows are never deleted (03 1 point 7, M1-12 D4); same pattern as `identity_users_kind_check`. |
| D-M2-24-17 | 2026-10-13 | `platform/httpserver` keeps the JSON body that `StageBody` checked in the request context (`httpserver.RawJSONBody`); the app handlers hash and store the manifest member exactly as received from it. | The generated strict server hands a handler only the decoded value, and a re-encoding would change the bytes the hash must cover (11 3.1). |
| D-M2-24-18 | 2026-10-13 | The base lacks `routes.SpecVersion`; this branch carries the M1-46 generator commits that add it (`feat(api): spec version, gateway protocol versions and problem catalogue from the contract`, `style(api): gofmt of the generator meta test`) unchanged, so `min_api_version` is compared with the real spec version and the later merge with M1-46 brings identical content. | The brief asks to extend the generator "exactly as M1-46 Scope item 10 describes"; taking M1-46's own commits is the exact form and avoids a second, diverging implementation. |
| D-M2-24-19 | 2026-10-13 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-24-apps-install` from `int/m1-wave12` (`da08f58`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it. Rulings stand in this section in the table format of the base. M1 tickets outside the closure on the base: M1-31 (yes); M1-19 (yes); M1-46, M1-48, M1-51, M1-53 to M1-57, M1-64, M1-65 (no). The AZ cases and the `access.changed` assertions run in tier 1 (real installation, token, authenticator, Authorizer and rights hook, grants from `authz.Admin.CreateGrant`, structure from the fake source); tier 2 and the gateway test wait for M1-56, M1-57, M1-64 and M1-65. | The launcher's task takes precedence over the brief's merge step; the brief allows only merged tickets (design note 17). |
| D-M2-24-20 | 2026-10-13 | Names that differ from the brief: the token scopes of the actor are the existing field `actor.Actor.Scopes` (no new `TokenScopes`), read through the new `authorize.Principal.AppTokenScopes`; the principal port methods are `identity.PrincipalWrites` (`CreatePrincipal`, `SetPrincipalStatus`, `UpdateDisplayName`); `audit.reason_max_chars` is registered in `configkeys_audit.go` (its area), the other keys in `configkeys_apps.go`; the scope table port also lists the scopes (`AppScopes.Scopes`). | The brief says to add `TokenScopes` only if missing and to follow the merged names; M1-51 may reuse `PrincipalWrites` for agents. |
| D-M2-24-21 | 2026-10-13 | A manifest whose JSON holds the escape of NUL (`\u0000`) or of a surrogate that is not part of a pair is invalid at the root pointer (`invalid`); values taken from a manifest (delivery hosts, command names, the slug) enter the audit through one helper that writes a value that is no audit code as `sha256:` and 32 hex digits. | PostgreSQL refuses both escapes in `jsonb`, and the audit refuses codes longer than 64 bytes or address literals; without the rule and the helper a manifest that passes every check made install or upgrade answer 500 (red team M2-24, SEC-IN-4, SEC-T06). One rule and one helper fix the class instead of each value. |
| D-M2-24-22 | 2026-10-13 | A keyed call (`Idempotency-Key`) of `suspendApp`, `resumeApp`, `uninstallApp`, `setAppScopes` or `upgradeAppManifest` that loses a race decides the call without effect inside its rights change: the plan runs again under the locks and writes the allow entry with `no_change`, and the rights version rises once. | The idempotency stage runs the handler in one unit, so the rights change is a late one whose error fails the whole unit even when swallowed; the losing caller got 500. Design note 9 g accepts the one extra version step for this rare case; unkeyed calls still roll back and plan again without it. |
| D-M2-24-23 | 2026-10-13 | The integration world of the module apps lives in `services/server/test/appsworld`; its tests are split over `internal/apps/app`, `internal/apps/adapters/httpapi` and `internal/apps/adapters/postgres`. | `make coverage` measures each package only by its own tests, so the use cases and the repository counted as uncovered while the HTTP tests ran through them, and the server total fell below the threshold. Code under `test/` is test code (02 section 6, Code layout) and outside the measurement. |

## M8-01 Discord read client

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commit; the English docs of main win. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. The brief relies on no core rule and no contract change; D-W-51, D-W-100, D-W-120 and D-W-124 are planning rulings in this file. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request (precedent D-M3-17-8 and M1-23/3). |
| D-M8-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The tool code lives under `services/server/cmd/bothy/importtool/` (D-W-124 (1)); the client is `cmd/bothy/importtool/discord`. `TestDiscordClientImportsNoServerModule` holds that `go list -deps` of the package contains no package under `internal/` except `internal/platform/egress` and `internal/kernel/...`. | `bothy import` is a subcommand of the one binary (component `cmd`), which may use every package; the test keeps the tool on the public API (22 2, R-IM-01). |
| D-M8-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | Rate-limit guard: the route key is the path template plus the guild or channel ID; the bucket is the `X-RateLimit-Bucket` Discord last named for the route, else the path template, again plus that ID. A missing or unparsable wait (`retry_after`, `Retry-After`, and also `X-RateLimit-Reset-After` with `Remaining: 0`) counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`; any wait above it ends the call before sleeping with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`, also the wait for an exhausted bucket. After a waited-out `429` the bucket's refill time is cleared, so the retry does not wait twice. The retries after the last allowed `429` end with the new sentinel `ErrRateLimited` (status `429`). `5xx` (`ErrServer`) and transport errors are never retried in the client. | 22 4.3 items 1 to 4: fail towards waiting, never towards hammering (RK-76); the mirror's backoff is M8-05, an import run ends with exit `1` (22 10.3). |
| D-M8-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TestClientHasOnlyGet` type-checks the non-test files of the package from source against export data (the approach of `internal/wire/counters_static_test.go`): exactly one call of `http.NewRequest` or `http.NewRequestWithContext`, whose method argument is the object `net/http.MethodGet`; no `http.Request` literal and no use of the field `Method` of a request; no exported name, method or field that matches `post`, `put`, `patch` or `delete` (case-insensitive); no exported function with a parameter named like a method or verb. `TestFakeSeesOnlyGet` drives every exported method against the fake, which answers every other method `405`; `TestNoTestPackageInBinary` lists the dependencies of `cmd/bothy`. | 22 16 "Discord client without a write method", ADR 0043 ruling 1; the literal and field checks close the other ways to send another verb with the one construction. |
| D-M8-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `MessagesAfter`, `MessagesBefore` and `MessagesAround` return messages in ascending snowflake order, sorted by the numeric value, whatever order Discord used. | Discord returns newest first; the import appends strictly in Discord order (22 6.2 `thread_seq`), so no caller may see another order. |
| D-M8-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `Config.Egress` carries the `egress.*` values of the tool (allowed hosts, timeout, response size limit), because only an operator rule with `allow_private` may open the loopback fake and a consumer policy never can; the host policy of the client names exactly the host and port of `import.discord.api_base` over `https`. The `User-Agent` URL is the constant `https://github.com/OWNER/bothy` (the placeholder of `repo_url` in `mkdocs.yml`), the version comes from `Config.UserAgentVersion`. Further sentinels beside the brief's: `ErrRateLimited`, `ErrUnexpectedStatus` (any other status, a redirect included), `ErrDecode` (a body that is not the expected JSON, without its content), `ErrInvalidID`, `ErrTokenHostMismatch`; `CallError` has a `Hint` (the missing `MANAGE_THREADS`). `Attachment.URL` and `Embed.URL` are of type `SignedURL`, whose print forms show scheme, host and path only and whose `Raw` returns the full link (M8-02). `Deps.Sleep` is a `Sleeper` function. The fixture holds archive pages explicitly; a request beyond the last page gets the last page again. Fixture and test IDs are invented 8-digit strings, because the white-label rule of `tools/denylist/generic.rules` flags 17 to 20 digits as real Discord snowflakes; the client accepts 1 to 20 digits that fit an unsigned 64-bit number. | The brief allows following the code where names differ; each addition keeps a rule of the brief (no proxy, token only to the API base, no secret in output) provable. |
| D-M8-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | `TestTokenOnlyToAPIBase` checks the unexported header setter directly with another host, plain `http` and another port (refused, no header), and a redirect of the fake to a second host name, which the client does not follow (`ErrUnexpectedStatus`, one request, all on the API host). | Every method builds its URL from the API base, so only the setter itself and a redirect can aim a request elsewhere. |

## M7-01 Image bothy-postgres with pgBackRest

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The image lives in `services/server/images/bothy-postgres/` (Dockerfile, `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, `entrypoint.sh`, `check-pgbackrest-version.sh`, README), not in `images/bothy-postgres` of the plan. | The image is an artifact of the component `server` (18 2); there the component path filter of `tools/ci/components.py` sees it. |
| D-M7-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | pgBackRest comes as the package `pgbackrest=2.59.3-1.pgdg13+1` of `apt.postgresql.org` (suite `trixie-pgdg`, the Debian release of the pinned `postgres:18.6-trixie` digest), checked through the repository key of the base image, whose fingerprint is pinned in the Dockerfile. The package stage runs on the build platform, downloads `pgbackrest` and `libssh2-1t64` for the target architecture and unpacks them; the final stage only copies, plus package records under `/var/lib/dpkg/status.d/` for image scanners. `libssh2-1t64` takes the version of the signed Debian archive of that release. The version guard checks the pinned package version in the build and the installed binary on every start. | 18 "Server images" rule 2 forbids emulation; with no step on the target platform one host builds both platforms natively, so `make postgres-image` writes both into one archive. A package exists for both architectures, so no source build is needed (brief design note 1). An exact pin of the Debian library would break the build at every Debian point release, while its integrity is already guaranteed by the signed archive. |
| D-M7-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `bothy-backupd` is a second `main` package `services/server/cmd/bothy-backupd` with the server version from `internal/platform/buildinfo`; in M7-01 it supervises `pgbackrest server`, creates the stanza `bothy` (retried every `--retry` until it succeeds), stops the child on `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT` (the base image's stop signal), and offers `healthcheck` and `version`. | Brief design note 2: the server binary with its API role has no business in the database image; one commit versions both images. |
| D-M7-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `pgbackrest_tls/` holds `ca.crt` and per host `postgres` and `backup` a certificate and an ECDSA P-256 PKCS#8 key. The CA is self-signed with path length zero; its key is never written, so a rotation regenerates the directory. Host certificates carry the host name as common name and DNS name and both server and client use, and all three are valid for `stack.PGBackRestTLSValidity`. The whole directory goes to `postgres` and `backup`, as the catalogue row names it. | Both hosts are TLS servers and clients (D-M7-01-5); `tls-server-auth` matches the common name; a stored CA key would let any service holding a copy mint certificates. |
| D-M7-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Besides PostgreSQL, the container `postgres` runs `pgbackrest server`; the repository host reaches it as `pg1-host` over TLS (`pg1-host-type=tls`) for `stanza-create`, `backup` and `check`. | A repository host can only back up a database whose host it reaches through pgBackRest; without it the full backup "from inside backup" of design note 3 is impossible. It is an inbound connection on the internal network, so R-OP-08 holds. |
| D-M7-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | Both roles run as the user `postgres` of the base image (`USER 999:999`); the entry point never needs root. `secrets-init` gets `--owner postgres=999:999 --owner backup=999:999` (16 5.3 item 4 updated), and the repository and configuration directories belong to that user. The new variable `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_PG_HOST` names the database host for `backup`; `BOTHY_PG_ARCHIVE_TIMEOUT` and both host variables are required. The cipher pass goes only into the configuration file of `backup` (mode `0600`), because pgBackRest reads it from no file of its own. | pgBackRest refuses keys that another user can read (`0444` copies of root failed in the first run); a root image fails the Trivy rule of a non-root user. Host names and timeouts come from named variables (rule 9). |
| D-M7-01-7 | 2026-10-14 | Two CI jobs, `postgres-image (amd64)` on `ubuntu-24.04` and `postgres-image (arm64)` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, each with a docker-container buildx builder and no QEMU; they run when the plan lists `server` or `all`, and `ci-ok` (`tools/ci/ci_ok.py`) requires each to end with success or skipped. Written out as two jobs instead of a matrix, because `ci_ok.py` requires the jobs of `ci.yml` by name and a skipped matrix job has no expanded name; the runner rules of `tools/ci/check_runners.py` accept both labels as they are. | B12 and 18 rule 2 need a native arm64 run; the arm64 runner is GitHub-hosted and free for public repositories (D-W-125 rule of no cost). |
| D-M7-01-8 | 2026-10-14 | The two-container test fills the secret volumes of `postgres` and `backup` with a helper container that copies the output of `stack.Init` and sets owner `999:999`, directories `0500` and files `0400`, exactly as `secrets-init` with `--owner` leaves them. The point-in-time target lies at least a second after the end of the full backup. | A test running without root cannot chown files to `999`; pgBackRest selects the backup set by its stop time in whole seconds. |
| D-M7-01-9 | 2026-10-15 | `/etc/pgbackrest` is a link owned by root to `/tmp/bothy-pgbackrest`, which the entry point creates with mode `0700` before it writes `pgbackrest.conf` (and refuses when it is not a directory of the running user); the image copies no configuration directory owned by the user any more. The repository and log directories stay owned by `999:999` (D-M7-01-6). | SonarQube Cloud rates a user-writable copied path below `/etc` as a vulnerability (rule docker:S6504); the default path `/etc/pgbackrest/pgbackrest.conf` stays, so pgBackRest, `docker compose exec` and the docs need no option. |

## M8-02 CDN fetch with host allow list

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-02-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. Brief, spec and REUSE were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule and no contract change, and chapter 22 already describes the behaviour (6.5, 14, 16), so no spec text changes. Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `CDNConfig` also carries `MaxRetries`, `MaxRateWait` (the `429` limits of requirement 16), `UserAgentVersion` and `Egress` (as in `Config` of M8-01, so that only a test rule with `allow_private` opens the loopback fake); `AttachmentRef.URL` is a `SignedURL`, so that a printed reference never shows the signature; the one request construction of the package moved into `newGet`, which the API client and the fetcher share, because `TestClientHasOnlyGet` allows exactly one; the fetcher also returns `*CallError` (op `GET <scheme://host/path>`) and the `429` sentinels of the client. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases and main takes each ticket as one pull request (precedent D-M8-01-0); the brief allows following the code where names differ, and each addition keeps a rule of the brief provable. |
| D-M8-02-1 | 2026-10-15 | One `platform/egress` client per fetcher with a host policy of exactly the entries of `import.discord.cdn_hosts` over `https`, `RedirectNever`, `Timeout` = `import.http_timeout` and `MaxResponseBytes` = `import.attachment_max_bytes` plus one, instead of one transport per allowed host (REUSE 5.2). The fetcher checks every link against the same list before the request, so `egress.allowed_hosts` adds no CDN host. | The per-request host policy gives the same guarantee: no connection is shared across a host the policy did not allow, because the pool keys connections by scheme, host and port. |
| D-M8-02-2 | 2026-10-15 | Re-read rule: on `403` or `404` the body is closed unread, `MessagesAround(channel, message, 1)` reads the message once, the attachment with the same ID (also in a forwarded message snapshot) gives the fresh link, which passes the URL check and the declared size check again, and is fetched once. A missing message or attachment, or a second `403` or `404`, is `ErrCDNGone`; `5xx` and other statuses are a `*CallError` without retry. A `429` waits like the read client (`retry_after`, else `Retry-After`; a missing value counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`, a longer wait ends with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`); its body is read only for that value. | 22 6.5 item 3 asks for exactly one re-read and one more try; the caller writes the jump-link line for a failed attachment (22 6.5 item 6, M8-04). |
| D-M8-02-3 | 2026-10-15 | Port rule: a link matches an entry when its lower-cased host without one trailing dot equals the entry and its port equals the entry's port, `443` when the entry names none; an explicit `:443` matches an entry without a port. Entries are DNS names with an optional `:port`, never IP literals or wildcards. | Production entries name no port; tests write the fake's port into the entry as `host:port` (Design note 1). |

## M6-10 Color vision deficiency check for charts

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commit; the chart roles `color.viz.*`, their contrast pairing and the preview `viz` of plan ruling D-W-13 are on main already, so the base's port of them is left out. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | Simulation model: the matrices of Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes 2009 (IEEE TVCG 15(6), doi:10.1109/TVCG.2009.113) for severity 1.0, for protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia, in `tokenkit/cvd.py` with the citation. They are applied in linear sRGB: decode, multiply, clip to [0, 1], encode. Tests pin every column against the published numbers, white and greys unchanged, and two samples worked by hand from the table. | One model for all three deficiencies, in linear RGB, as browser developer tools use it; the gate needs a reproducible transformation, not a perceptual study (brief, design note 1). |
| D-M6-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | Distance: OKLab Euclidean distance of the two simulated colours (`delta_e_ok`), computed on the unrounded simulated values. `cvd_min_delta_e_ok` is 0.05 in `design/design-rules.json`, with source and reason there: 2.5 times the just noticeable difference 0.02 of deltaE OK (CSS Color 4), because chart marks are small and the noticeable difference grows as marks shrink (Szafir 2018). A row passes when the distance rounded to three places reaches the minimum, as contrast rows round to two. Measured minimum before and after the adjustment (series; traffic light): light protanopia 0.056 to 0.071; 0.069 to 0.123, deuteranopia 0.012 to 0.094; 0.008 to 0.083, tritanopia 0.076 to 0.076; 0.095 to 0.079; dark protanopia 0.054 to 0.086; 0.071 to 0.075, deuteranopia 0.012 to 0.067; 0.007 to 0.059, tritanopia 0.116 to 0.116; 0.011 to 0.099. | The measure the palette guards already use; the factor is a stated design choice, not a measured value (brief, design note 2). |
| D-M6-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | Changed values (alias steps of the same scale, so hue and series order stay): light `color.viz.series.3` `spruce.600` (`#1b8a83`) to `spruce.700` (`#136f69`), `color.viz.series.6` `moss.600` (`#1b9046`) to `moss.900` (`#063e1a`), `color.warning.fg` `amber.700` (`#8c4f10`) to `amber.800` (`#6b3b09`), `color.danger.fg` `rowan.700` (`#b21622`) to `rowan.900` (`#63080e`); dark `color.viz.series.3` `spruce.400` (`#49c0b7`) to `spruce.300` (`#7cd5cd`), `color.viz.series.7` `rowan.400` (`#fb827a`) to `rowan.500` (`#f15653`), `color.danger.fg` `rowan.300` (`#fcaaa3`) to `rowan.400` (`#fb827a`). Two series per theme is the fewest that passes (exhaustive search over the steps of each series' own scale); no palette step changes, so no other pairing moves. The traffic light failed under deuteranopia in both themes and under tritanopia in dark; the state roles changed because every contrast pairing of banners, badges and live box stays green in both themes and with every accent (`make tokens`). All previews are regenerated, because the state roles appear in the components, layout, mobile and palette sheets too. | Brief, design note 4: fewest series, lightness before hue, guards and contrast green; the state roles only with the banner and badge pairings green. |
| D-M6-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | Pairs (`cvd_pairs`): a `sequence` entry with the eight series and `wrap: true` gives 1 with 2 up to 7 with 8 and 8 with 1; a `pairs` entry gives `success.fg` with `warning.fg`, `warning.fg` with `danger.fg` and `success.fg` with `danger.fg`; all under the three deficiencies, per theme (`light`, `dark`). The accents of M4-03 swap none of these roles, so there are no per-accent CVD rows; a test fails if an accent role joins the checked roles. Rows go to `contrast.md` (section "Color vision deficiency") and to the member `cvd` of `tokens.resolved.json`. | Brief, design notes 3 and 5. |

## M4-01 Dart workspace and architecture test

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win (chapters `15-client-architecture.md` and `23-quality.md`), text this ticket adds is English, and its first commit (R-QA-14 into chapter 23) is left out because main has R-QA-14 since its baseline. `ci-ok` on main has no `needs` and requires the jobs of `ci.yml` by name through `RULES` in `tools/ci/ci_ok.py`, so the two app jobs are added there. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The Flutter pin lives in `environment.flutter` of the root `pubspec.yaml` (`3.47.7`, Dart `^3.13.5`), CI reads it there (`flutter-version-file: pubspec.yaml` in `gates`, `sonar`, `apps-macos`, `apps-windows` and the Pages build), and every member repeats the same exact `environment` lines; `test_flutter_pins_agree` holds the fifteen manifests equal. Replaces D-M2-38-12. | One known Flutter for every job and member; the workspace root is the one manifest every member shares. |
| D-M4-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | `packages/bothy_noise` joins the workspace (`resolution: workspace`), its `pubspec.lock` is deleted and its code and tests stay unchanged; its OSV-Scanner overrides move with the lock file to the root `osv-scanner.toml`, with a new checked entry for every package or version the one resolution adds. The dev dependency `test` is `^1.31.1` in every pure Dart member, because `flutter_test` of Flutter 3.47.7 pins `test_api` 0.7.12 and `test` 1.33 needs 0.7.15; one resolution cannot hold both. Replaces D-M2-38-13; D-M2-38-20 applies to the root file. | Brief note 1; OSV-Scanner reads the override file next to the lock file it scans. |
| D-M4-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | The reach of the diagram of 15 3.1 is an allow list: `tools/arch-check/rules.yaml` holds the arrows, and a member may depend on and import exactly the members they reach (arrows and transitive closure), checked in `make arch`. A new arrow changes 15 3.1 and `rules.yaml` in the same change; `test_rules_edges_equal_the_spec_diagram` holds both equal. 15 3.1 gains rule 8 (the reach) and rule 9 (every manifest a listed member), and the check details of rules 1, 4 and 7. Findings on a manifest name its line 1. | A deny list would let every new edge in silently (brief note 2). |
| D-M4-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | Generated Dart code (`*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`, `*.drift.dart`, `lib/src/generated/`, `build/`) is out of the doc lint and the style lint (analyzer `exclude` in the root `analysis_options.yaml`), the format check (`DART_GENERATED` in the root `Makefile`) and coverage (`tools/ci/lcov_filter.py`). It stays in the compile check: the members that import it are analyzed, and `flutter test` compiles it. An excluded file is not analyzed itself, so an analyzer error inside it shows only through its users and the compiler. Each member includes its base set and then the root file through a list under `include` (Dart 3.6 and later). | Generators format and document their own output (M4-02, M4-12); the same split as M2-23 note 8 for the other languages. |
| D-M4-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | `make lint` runs `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile` and `flutter analyze --fatal-infos` once at the workspace root, which analyzes every member, and `dart run custom_lint` in each member that declares it; `make test` resolves once at the root and runs `flutter test --coverage` per member, after checking that every member has `test/`. | One resolution and one analysis for the workspace (brief note 4); a stale lock file still fails instead of being rewritten (D-M2-38-21). |
| D-M4-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | The Dart API pages are generated for every build and never tracked: `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` runs `dart doc` per package into the ignored folder `docs/api/dart/`, and a warning or an error fails it. `make docs` runs it whenever a Dart member is tracked, for every component selection, not only with `apps`, because `docs/reference/dart-api.md` links the pages and `mkdocs build --strict` fails on a missing target; `make linkcheck` builds them first when the folder is missing, for the same reason (lychee checks the links offline). No validation setting changes. | Generated pages cannot drift from the code (brief note 5); the strict site build and the link check stay as they are. |
| D-M4-01-7 | 2026-10-14 | Platform tests are outside `gates`: `make apps-platform` (`tools/ci/apps_platform.py`) runs the argument lists of each member's `platform-tests.json` for the host system, and the CI jobs `apps (macOS)` (`macos-15`) and `apps (Windows)` (`windows-2025`) run it when the `apps` component is selected; `ci-ok` requires each to end with `success` or `skipped`. | They need a desktop session (brief note 6, ADR 0039); a skipped job (component not selected) must not block. |
| D-M4-01-8 | 2026-10-14 | The root files `pubspec.yaml`, `pubspec.lock` and `analysis_options.yaml` and the prefix `tools/arch-check/` select the gate component `apps` (`tools/ci/components.py`). | A change there must run the Dart gates (brief note 7); before, the root files selected `all`. |
| D-M4-01-9 | 2026-10-14 | The packages of the Flutter SDK in the root lock file (`flutter`, `flutter_test`, `sky_engine`; source `sdk`, version 0.0.0) get license overrides in `osv-scanner.toml`: BSD-3-Clause, the license of the Flutter repository. The LICENSE file of `sky_engine` also collects the notices of the third-party code compiled into the engine (Apache-2.0, MIT, BSD, Zlib, Unicode, IJG and FreeType texts among them); its review is the ScanCode step before a release (docs/quality/licenses.md item 4). | deps.dev does not know SDK packages, so the license gate reports them as UNKNOWN; every Flutter app ships the engine (ADR 0027). |
| D-M4-01-10 | 2026-10-14 | Skeletons: each package library is a `library;` directive with its doc comment and no other public symbol, so each first test imports it with `// ignore: unused_import` (the import is what it checks). `device_key` is created from `flutter create --template=plugin --platforms=macos,windows` reduced to a Flutter package without native files, without the example app and without a `flutter: plugin:` section, because no platform class exists until M4-10. The apps show an empty `MaterialApp` without text and have no platform folders yet. | Brief note 8 and rule 4: no native code before its ticket. |
| D-M4-01-11 | 2026-10-14 | `packages/api_client` gets a `Makefile` whose `generate-check` passes while no generated code is tracked and fails as soon as `lib/src/generated/` or a `*.g.dart` file is tracked; M4-02 replaces the target with the generation, the compile and the comparison. `make api-clients` stays fail-closed for a manifest without the target. `tools/ci/apps_platform.py` resolves the program of each command with `shutil.which` before it runs it. | Code review M4-01: the tracked skeleton manifest made `make api-clients` (and so `make gates` and CI) fail, and without a shell Windows does not find `flutter.bat` for the job `apps-windows`. |
| D-M4-01-12 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: `dart doc` links Google Fonts (a preconnect and two stylesheets) in every page, which the external asset check of M7-19 refuses, so `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` removes every `<link>` to another origin from the generated pages, which then use the fonts of the system. The generated folder `api/dart/` is listed in `not_in_nav` of `mkdocs.yml`, because main's strict build fails on a page missing from the navigation and `docs/reference/dart-api.md` is the entry to those pages. | The site loads nothing from another origin (M7-19/3); the generated pages are reached through one reference page, like the single ADRs through the ADR index. |

## M7-10 Server release chain with a trial release

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win. The runbook keeps main's layout: the server steps stand in `release.md` 2.1, the trial release in 2.3, the key pair of the private phase in 2.4, the verification commands in 5. On main the release workflow follows the stricter rules of `tools/ci/check_runners.py` (release jobs resolve the tag again, outputs of jobs without a release environment only in `if:`, artifacts only by id), so `postgres-build-amd64`, `postgres-build-arm64` and `scancode` run in the environment `release` and resolve the tag themselves (D-M7-10-8). | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged rules win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M7-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | Trial release (Design note 1): `make release-trial COMPONENT=server` runs `tools/ci/release_trial.py`, which starts `registry:3.1.2` pinned by digest on `127.0.0.1` with a random port, creates a `docker-container` buildx builder in the host network (so BuildKit reaches the registry on `127.0.0.1`), runs `make -C services/server release-build`, pushes `bothy-server` (both platforms, one build) and `bothy-postgres` (one build per platform) by digest, joins the postgres digests, generates a cosign key pair in the temporary directory, and then calls the same `tools/ci/release_chain.py` functions as `release.yml` (`sbom`, `sign --mode private`, `verify --mode private`, `tag`). It checks every image `infra/compose/compose.yaml` names with `${BOTHY_VERSION}` against the signed digest and runs three probes that must fail (changed file against `SHA256SUMS`, unsigned child digest against `cosign verify`, a release without one SBOM). cosign and syft get an empty `DOCKER_CONFIG`, so no credential of the host enters. `TRIAL_PLATFORMS` defaults to both platforms for both images, unlike the brief's default of the host platform for `bothy-postgres`: the merged Dockerfile of M7-01 builds the foreign platform of `bothy-postgres` without emulation (D-M7-01-2). Vulnerability scan and ScanCode are not part of the trial; they are the separate targets `make trivy-image` and `make scancode`. The trial and the target refuse to run when `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set. | D-W-127; the same code in trial and release is what makes the trial a proof; the merged code wins over the brief's default (D-W-100); a scan result depends on the vulnerability database of the day, not on the chain. |
| D-M7-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | Signing commands (Design note 2, cosign 3.1.3 pinned): private phase `cosign signing-config create --no-default-rekor --no-default-fulcio --no-default-tsa --no-default-oidc --out <file>` (checked to name no service), `cosign sign --yes --key env://COSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY --signing-config <file> --bundle <image>.sigstore.json <ref@digest>` per image index and `cosign sign-blob --yes --key env://COSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY --signing-config <file> --bundle <file>.sigstore.json <file>` per binary and for `SHA256SUMS`; every bundle is checked to carry no transparency log entry and no timestamp. Verification `cosign verify --key <pub> --insecure-ignore-tlog <ref@digest>`, `cosign verify-blob --key <pub> --insecure-ignore-tlog --bundle <bundle> <file>`, `sha256sum --check --strict SHA256SUMS`. Public phase: the same commands without `--key` and signing configuration (keyless with the OIDC token of the job), verified with `--certificate-identity-regexp '^https://github.com/<owner>/bothy/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/server/v' --certificate-oidc-issuer https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`, plus `actions/attest` per image digest; the mode follows `github.event.repository.visibility`. `SHA256SUMS` covers every file of the release except the signature bundles. Signing steps stand only in the job `supply-chain` with `environment: release`; `check_runners.py` rule `signing-outside-release` holds that for every workflow (environment name compared without case). | ADR 0062 items 2 and 3 and its confirmation; the deprecated `--tlog-upload=false` is not used; the trial ran these commands against the pinned version. |
| D-M7-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | ScanCode gate (Design note 3): ScanCode publishes no container image, so `tools/scancode/Dockerfile` builds one from `python:3.13-slim-trixie` pinned by digest and the release archive `scancode-toolkit-v32.5.0_py3.13-linux.tar.gz` pinned by sha256 (`ADD --checksum`); the archive's native wheels are linux/amd64 only, so the release job `scancode` runs on `ubuntu-24.04` and an arm64 host runs it emulated. `make scancode` scans `git archive HEAD` with `--license --copyright` and `tools/ci/scancode_policy.py` fails on any file whose detected SPDX expression `quality/licenses/allowed.txt` does not satisfy (`OR` one side, `AND` both, `WITH` the license, `LicenseRef-*` never) and on scan errors, except paths in `quality/licenses/scancode-exceptions.txt`, each with a reason (only text that names or quotes a license). `supply-chain` needs `scancode`, so a finding stops the release before any tag; the report is a release artifact. Calibration: a run of ScanCode 32.5.0 over the tracked tree at this branch found 38 files outside the list, all text that names licenses or reviewed data (documentation, tool manifests and lock files, the OFL-1.1 fonts of docs/design/typography.md rule 5 in `design/` and `tools/design-tokens/`, the Unlicense Noise vectors of D-M2-38-15, the OSV-Scanner overrides, the SPDX header templates of the server's code generators, DCO and CLA in `CONTRIBUTING.md`); `scancode-exceptions.txt` lists these paths with their reasons, and the same report then has no finding. That run used the PyPI package `scancode-toolkit-mini` 32.5.0 natively, because the container was killed twice for memory in the shared Docker VM of the build host; the release job runs the container on an amd64 runner. | licenses.md section 4; a digest-pinned base plus a checksum-pinned archive fixes the image as rule 9 of the brief asks; a gate needs a decision rule, and documentation that lists forbidden licenses must not stop a release. |
| D-M7-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | Tag form (Design note 4): `server/vX.Y.Z` stays. 18 section 2 rule 2 now quotes the Go modules reference as written: the subdirectory prefix of tags and the `/vN` suffix from major version 2 on are stated without a condition, and the reference gives their purpose ("If a module might be depended on by other modules, these rules must be followed so that the go command can find and download the module"); the server module is a main module that no module requires (P-05), so the go command never maps its versions to tags, and its version comes from `VERSION`. | The old text attached the condition "only if loaded as a dependency" to the rule itself, which the source does not say (the plan's risk RK-57). |
| D-M7-10-5 | 2026-10-15 | The public key of the private phase lives at `quality/release/cosign-private-phase.pub` (with a README there). This ticket does not create it; until the owner commits it and stores `COSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY` and `COSIGN_PASSWORD` in the environment `release`, a server release stops at its verification step, before any tag (`release_chain.py verify` names the missing file). | Brief Design note 2 and Out of scope; a missing key must fail closed, not sign with nothing. |
| D-M7-10-6 | 2026-10-15 | `make release-prep` (Design note 5): git-cliff 2.14.2 selects and parses the commits (`--unreleased --context`, `--tag-pattern` per prefix, `--include-path <dir>/**`, for `server` also `docs/api/**`), and `tools/ci/release_prep.py` applies the rule of 18 section 3: a break gives a major from 1.0.0 on and a minor before, `feat` a minor, `fix` or `perf` a patch, other types alone nothing to release; the first release is 0.1.0. `--bumped-version` is not used: in a fixture repository it gave 1.0.0 for a `BREAKING CHANGE` footer on 0.1.0 and a patch for a `docs` commit, and with `no_increment_regex` it ignored a break marked on a `chore!` commit. `CHANNEL=beta` appends `-beta.N` (N one above the highest existing beta tag of that version); stable after betas keeps the version and gets a section that points to the beta sections. The section `Unreleased` becomes `[X.Y.Z] - <date>` under a new empty `Unreleased`; nothing is written on any error; the script never commits or tags. | ADR 0060 item 2 names the rule, and the tool did not follow it in three reproduced cases; git-cliff still does the path and tag selection and the Conventional Commits parsing ADR 0060 chose it for. |
| D-M7-10-7 | 2026-10-15 | `tools/ci/components.py release` also rejects a tag whose component `CHANGELOG.md` has no section `[X.Y.Z]`, so the job `resolve` stops before the gates and the build; `publish` keeps its own check. | Requirement 15 of the brief: a missing changelog section aborts before anything is published; before, `publish` found it after the image was tagged. |
| D-M7-10-8 | 2026-10-15 | `bothy-postgres` is built by two jobs, `postgres-build-amd64` on `ubuntu-24.04` and `postgres-build-arm64` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (GitHub-hosted, environment `release`), each pushing its platform by digest, uploading the digest as the artifact `postgres-digest-<arch>` and passing its id as the output `digest_id`; `supply-chain` downloads both by id and joins them with `release_chain.py join`, which pushes the index by its own digest (computed from `imagetools create --dry-run`) and compares what the registry stores. `scancode` also runs in the environment `release`, so that its `report_id` may reach `supply-chain`. Images are pushed by digest only until the version tags. | Brief requirement 13 and 18 section 7 rule 2; `imagetools create` needs a target and a tag would publish a name before the scan; main's rules release-artifact-by-id and release-untrusted-output refuse artifacts by pattern and outputs of jobs without a release environment, and a matrix has no output per leg. |
| D-M7-10-9 | 2026-10-15 | `services/server/Makefile` `release-build VERSION=<v> OUT=<dir>` writes `bothy_<v>_linux_amd64` and `bothy_<v>_linux_arm64` with the flags of the Dockerfile (`CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath -buildvcs=false -ldflags "-s -w"`); the image builds stay with the root targets and `release.yml`. | Component contract of 23 section 3; the binaries cover the platforms of the images (18 section 7 rule 1). |

## M4-02 Dart API client

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-02-0 | 2026-10-16 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. No contract change of `main` is needed: `docs/api/openapi.yaml` has every schema the ticket uses. The generated client is generated again from main's `openapi.yaml`. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M4-02-1 | 2026-10-15 | The generator runs from `openapitools/openapi-generator-cli:v7.26.0@sha256:a304ddf1e2e5f24f68fa3153568d6174cea4959d09aa8e3db6d526fd0782326d` (the digest of the multi-architecture index, `docker manifest inspect` on 2026-10-15; `docker buildx` is not installed on the build host), named in `packages/api_client/Makefile` (`GENERATOR_IMAGE`) and `docs/quality/toolchain.md`, run with `--rm --network none` (`GENERATOR_DOCKER_FLAGS`), `--user` of the caller, the repository mounted read-only and only a fresh `mktemp -d` folder writable. Temporary folders come from `$TMPDIR` (default `/tmp`), which Docker must be able to mount. | The generator needs a Java runtime the repository does not pin otherwise; the digest makes the run reproducible (brief Design note 2). |
| D-M4-02-2 | 2026-10-15 | Layout and copy step: the generator option `sourceFolder=src/generated` puts every generated file below `lib/src/generated/`, so the patterns of M4-01 cover them and the root `analysis_options.yaml`, `tools/ci/lcov_filter.py` and `sonar-project.properties` stay unchanged. The generator's library entry `lib/api_client.dart` becomes `lib/src/generated/generated_api.dart` (the brief named `lib/src/generated_api.dart`, which would sit outside the generated patterns; `pubLibrary` names no file and the generator writes no `library` directive); `lib/api_client.dart` is hand-written and exports it and the hand layer. `.openapi-generator-ignore` keeps the generator away from the hand-written paths and skips its docs and test stubs; the copy step takes only `lib/src/generated/` and the entry, and fails on any other file under `lib/`. `generate-check` (`tool/generate.sh check`) builds a workspace with this package as its only member and the root lock file in a temporary folder, generates, runs `build_runner` and `dart format` there, compares `lib/src/generated/` byte for byte (missing, extra and changed files by path, with the hint `make -C packages/api_client generate`) and analyses the copy with `package:lints/recommended.yaml` only: an error in generated code or any finding in hand-written code fails. The comparison lives in the script, not in `tools/ci/codegen_check.py`, because that tool runs the generator in place and the gate must never write into the working tree. The skeleton test of D-M4-01-11 gives way to `tools/ci/tests/test_api_client_generate.py`. | Brief Design notes 1 and 2; the root rules exclude generated code from analysis, so a separate analysis run is the only place where an error inside it shows. |
| D-M4-02-3 | 2026-10-15 | Tolerant events live in the hand layer: `EventsPageDecoder.decode` and `decodeEnvelope` (the `d` member of a gateway `dispatch` frame) keep an element whose `type` is not in `Event.discriminatorMapping` as `UnknownEvent` with its raw JSON, and decode every other element with the generated serializers. `fetchEvents` reads `GET /api/v1/events` through the client and this decoder, with the security marker of the generated operation, because the generated `listEvents` throws on the first unknown type. A known type that violates its schema still fails. | The generated union cannot skip an unknown member (brief Design note 6). |
| D-M4-02-4 | 2026-10-15 | No `null` stripping: the generated serializers leave absent optional members out, and `test/null_free_test.dart` holds it for the request models of M4 with only their required fields set. A required member typed nullable (`UserSettingPut.value`, a JSON value) is sent as `null` only when the caller leaves it unset, which the server answers with `400 validation_failed`. | Brief Design note 7 asks for the plugin only if the generator emits `null` for absent fields; it does not. |
| D-M4-02-5 | 2026-10-15 | Read retry only for `GET` and `HEAD`, after a network error, a timeout, `429` or `503` except `503 setup_required`; the wait is `Retry-After` (seconds or an HTTP date) or a full-jitter backoff `uniform(0, min(cap, base * 2^n))`. `ReadRetryPolicy.fromBackoff(base, cap)` sets `maxAttempts` to one plus the number of doublings of `base` until the bound reaches `cap` (base 2 s and cap 5 min: nine attempts), so the apps pass only `client.outbox_retry_base` and `client.outbox_retry_cap` and no new key exists. `createApiClient` therefore takes a required `readRetry` parameter, which the brief's signature did not list. A retry runs through the whole interceptor chain again (a new span ID, a new proof from the auth slot). | Brief Design note 4; 15 8: the outbox retries writes. |
| D-M4-02-6 | 2026-10-15 | Every response error reaches the caller as a `DioException` whose `error` is an `ApiProblem` (`ApiProblem.of(e)`), with the problem text as its message, so no request body reaches a log through it; the auth slot sees the raw error before the mapping. The dependency `uuid` the brief listed is not added: span IDs come from `Random.secure`, and the hand layer creates no other ID. | Interceptors of `dio` can only pass a `DioException` on; an unused dependency would only grow the lock file. |
| D-M4-02-7 | 2026-10-15 | `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` leaves out a `dart doc` warning whose location line points into generated code (`lib/src/generated/`, `*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`, `*.drift.dart`, the patterns of D-M4-01-4); every error, every warning in hand-written code and every warning without a location still fails. The pages of the generated API stay in the site (about 13,000 pages for `api_client`). | The generated doc comments name model members in brackets that `dart doc` cannot resolve (323 warnings, all in `lib/src/generated/`), so `make docs` failed although the doc lint already excludes generated code; changing generated comments would break the comparison of G4. |

## M6-11 bothy_viz: primitives, models, fixtures and harness

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-11-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M6-11-viz-package` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The base already holds every schema, operation and ruling the brief uses, so no contract change enters first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the spec chapters are `25-visualisierung.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `03-datenmodell.md` on this base; the brief's `harness_test.dart` lives in `packages/bothy_viz/harness/test/`, because `bothy_viz` cannot import the harness that depends on it; `StatsQuerySource` throws the `ApiProblem` of M4-02 instead of returning it, as every call of the generated client does. Text this ticket adds is English, also inside the German chapters. | Instructions of the launcher; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-11-1 | 2026-10-15 | View models are immutable classes built only from the generated types and app configuration values; they never call the network. Lanes group runs by space and channel in frame order; `runs_truncated` becomes the "More runs than shown" marker; a run card shows agent, state, step chip, badge, "Approval waiting" and the cost only in mode `organization` with `cost_micros` and `currency` present (`cost_is_estimate` gives "Estimate"); mode `personal` shows costs only in cost tiles with "information only"; a missing member, a missing cost response and a missing SLI are `null` ("not shown"), never 0. Executive tiles sum only the additive counters of the query responses (`messages`, `threads_created`, `runs`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `invocation_denials`); a level (percentile, rate, distinct count such as `active_people`) shows the latest bucket of a single group and no tile with several groups; a metric name of an earlier query keeps its first tile. A series of a grouped query carries the display name of its group (`VizNames.any`), and the area mark names it in the legend and the semantics label, so two groups of one metric never differ by color alone. The traffic light only maps `StatsSloState` to role, icon and text; an unknown state reads "not enough data". | Brief, design note 2; 25 4 rule 5 and 6.1. |
| D-M6-11-2 | 2026-10-15 | The stale rule is part of the port: `LiveFrameSourceBase` waits `viz.stale_after` on an injected `VizClock` after `start` and after every frame and emits one `stale` until the next frame; `start(spaceId)` closes the previous stream; error events carry the wire code and its kind (`forbidden`, `not_found`, `feature_disabled`, `rate_limited`, other). The apps of M6-16 extend the base, so every implementation behaves the same. | Brief, design note 3. |
| D-M6-11-3 | 2026-10-15 | Every mark is one semantics container with a label built from its model and excludes its parts, so a screen reader reads it once; the run card is a button that reports its thread ID and never navigates. The traffic light announces politely (`SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement`) only when the state of the same SLO changes between two builds, never on an unchanged frame; it uses no `liveRegion`, which would announce every label change. Every run bar of the bar mark starts with the icon of its run state, so the state is never color alone. | Brief, design note 4; 25 7.4. |
| D-M6-11-4 | 2026-10-15 | Fixtures are `<schema>.<state>.json`, or `<schema>.<query>.<state>.json` when one schema serves several queries (`StatsSeries.usage.normal.json`); the table "Fixtures" of `fixtures/README.md` lists each with its schema and readers. The check `viz-fixtures` of `make api-checks` validates them with the `jsonschema` package already pinned through `check-jsonschema` (no new dependency), and fails on an unknown schema, an invalid file, an unlisted file and a listed schema without a file. The fixtures are also assets of the package, so the harness reads the same files on desktop and web. | Brief, design note 5; 25 7.2. |
| D-M6-11-5 | 2026-10-15 | `tools/observability/sli_bounds.py` writes `slibounds.gen.go` (sorted map metric to SLO, objective, threshold, period) and `slo_catalog.g.dart` (sorted list without thresholds), both `DO NOT EDIT`; modes `--write` and `--check`. Label rules (exactly one `bothy_sli_metric` per SLO, a value of the `sli.*` list of `stats_minutes.metric`, one SLO per metric) are enforced by the generator and by `sli-labels`; `sli-bounds` compares the files with a fresh run. Labels per SLO: `bothy-api/requests-availability` `sli.api_availability`, `bothy-sync/delivery-latency` `sli.delivery_latency`, `bothy-sync/reconnect` `sli.reconnect`, `bothy-push/provider-acceptance` `sli.push_acceptance`, `bothy-agents/run-start` `sli.run_start`, `bothy-agents/turn-duration` `sli.turn_duration`. | Brief, design note 6; R-VZ-03. |
| D-M6-11-6 | 2026-10-15 | The harness is the workspace member `bothy_viz_harness` under `packages/bothy_viz/harness` with the members of `bothy_viz` plus `bothy_viz` itself (`rules.yaml`, kind `app`, rule 6). Its platform folders are not checked in, like `apps/client` and `apps/console`: `flutter create --platforms=macos,windows,web .` makes them locally (README). Its time-lapse moves the fake clock by one frame interval per step; the tests pass reduced motion so the spinning run icon lets the tree settle. The repository tooling learns the nested member in the same change: the node `bothy_viz_harness` with its edges in the diagram of 15 3.1, the lcov path in `sonar-project.properties`, and the member discovery of the arch-check and CI toolchain tests (one level plus `packages/*/harness`, counts derived from the workspace list). | Brief, design note 7; no generated native code in a package without native rule. |
| D-M6-11-7 | 2026-10-15 | Golden matrix: every mark in light and dark, the three densities, text scale 1 and 2, de and en (24 cells per file), CI goldens on every host and platform goldens on macOS, both for the full matrix. Every file stays under `repo.max_file_kb`; the area mark shows the daily usage series, because the minute series of the timeline made the anti-aliased file exceed the limit. | Brief, design note 8. |
| D-M6-11-8 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with its own commits and the integration fixes INT-W27/3 (dartdoc of `ReplayStep`) and INT-W27/4 (term check exception for `vizLaneLabel`); the English docs of main win (spec 25 7.1 and 7.2, 15 3.1 diagram, `docs/quality/gates.md`, `docs/observability/slos.md` and the tool READMEs), code comments name the English chapter files, and the `sli-labels` test reads the English column "Metric of the own measurement". The run card and traffic light goldens are rerendered on main. | Main carries its own value of `color.warning.subtle` (`palette.amber.100`, light `#FEE6D4` instead of `#FEF3EA` on the integration base), which these marks show. |

## M6-12 bothy_viz: six views

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-12-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave27` at `3d4f0f3`, branch `feat/M6-12-viz-views` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The schemas, operations and rulings the brief uses (T7, T8, D-W-100, D-W-120) are on the base; the statistics schemas differ from `origin/main` only in the chapter names of their descriptions, so no contract change enters first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the spec chapter is `25-visualisierung.md` on this base; the golden test lives in `test/goldens/views_golden_test.dart` next to the mark goldens (the golden path resolver of M6-11); the switch "As table" is a toggle button with toggled semantics, because the Material switch animates in the reduced motion context; the performance test runs through `harness/tool/perf_macos.sh`, listed in the harness `platform-tests.json`, because the platform folders of the harness are not checked in (D-M6-11-6). New fixture states: `StatsBackendReport.rate_limits.json` (every `rate_limit` state, I2 and I3 denials, a mismatch) and `StatsTimeline.day_boundary.json`; `StatsTimeline.normal.json` gains buckets of the delivery metrics for the history of 6.3. | Instructions of the launcher; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-12-1 | 2026-10-16 | Every view is `XxxView({live, queries, options, onOpenThread})`. `VizHostOptions` carries what only the host knows: form factor, scope (`VizScope.instance()` or `VizScope.space(id)`), the period of the queries, `allowTvMode`, `showCostsInTv` (default `viz.tv_show_costs`), `onOpenGrafana` (only with `telemetry.dashboard_url`), display names, the time zone of the person, the clock and the app configuration. `vizViewRegistry` lists per `VizViewKind` the title key, the icon, whether the view is instance only and whether it appears on mobile; `vizViewsFor` never offers TV mode on mobile or without `allowTvMode`. An error of a source (`forbidden`, `not_found`, `feature_disabled`, `rate_limited`) shows its `problemText` and stops the live source and every further query until the host builds the view again. Without a live source a live view reads `getStatsLive` once. | Brief, design note 1; 15 principle 1 (no permission logic in the client). |
| D-M6-12-2 | 2026-10-16 | In a space scope delivery and sync live shows "Only for the owner" and calls neither source; the other views pass the space to their sources and show what the server delivers. | Brief, design note 1; 25 4 rule 1. |
| D-M6-12-3 | 2026-10-16 | Data per view as 25 6: the live topology and delivery read the frames (delivery adds the history of its six metrics from `getStatsTimeline`), the backend view reads `getStatsBackends` once and takes presence, reported mode and rate limit from the `agents` of every frame, the executive page reads the five queries once, the timeline reads `getStatsTimeline` once. `forbidden` from `getStatsSlo` shows "Only for the owner" in the traffic light tile; `forbidden` from `getCostStats` leaves the cost tiles out (no `cost.read.aggregate`), never zero. The `offline` state shows the last frame with the stale marker. Delivery shows the count of the SLI window with its good share and the deliveries per minute as history, because the frame does not carry the length of `stats.live_sli_window` (build note in 25 6.3); catch-ups show their count and summed duration of the last minute. | Brief, design note 2; 25 4 rules 2 to 5. |
| D-M6-12-4 | 2026-10-16 | Each view has a toggle "As table" that swaps the chart for `VizTable`s with the same texts and column headers from `l10n`; a header tap sorts by its column (numbers by value), a second tap reverses. The render table gives the table, row and cell roles; header cells add the column header role and a "Sort by" label, every cell the label "column: value". A column that would only show "none" (cost without any cost) is left out. | Brief, design note 3; 25 7.4. |
| D-M6-12-5 | 2026-10-16 | TV mode is the route-less overlay `TvMode(views, options, live, queries, onExit)`: dark theme, density `comfortable`, the text scale times the step from `type.body-sm` to `type.body`, no controls (the views read `VizTvScope`), rotation every `viz.tv_rotation_interval` on the clock of the options with an `AnimatedSwitcher` of `motion.duration-move-slow` (zero in the reduced context), costs only with `showCostsInTv`, TV mode takes the keyboard focus after its first frame (autofocus would not take it from a focused host control behind an overlay), and the first key press or pointer down calls `onExit` once; the timeline keeps its playhead at the end there, so a screen shows the whole period. A view that leaves the rotation (or a tab) does not stop the live source when a newer view already started it: the closed stream tells the old view that the source is no longer its own. Constructing it without `allowTvMode` or on mobile fails an assertion; `buildVizView` refuses `tvMode`. | Brief, design note 4; 25 6.6; components.md 11. |
| D-M6-12-6 | 2026-10-16 | The performance test is `harness/integration_test/topology_perf_test.dart`: the `load` frame and a variant with every third run in another state, alternated every `stats.live_interval` of the fixture clock for 200 frames; it fails when the p95 of `FrameTiming.buildDuration` is above `viz.perf.frame_build_p95`. It runs with `flutter drive --profile -d macos` through `tool/perf_macos.sh` (which makes `macos/` for the run and removes everything the run added, because a native folder under `packages/` fails rule 4 of `make arch`) from `make apps-platform` (the discovery of `platform-tests.json` now includes `packages/*/harness`) and from `make perf`, whose new Dart step runs every member's `tool/perf_macos.sh` on macOS (`PERF_HOST`) and prints `SKIP perf-dart` elsewhere. The live topology keeps the widget of every unchanged card and lane between frames and paints each lane in its own layer, so a frame rebuilds only changed cards. | Brief, design note 6; 25 7.3; PLAN P-07. |
| D-M6-12-7 | 2026-10-16 | Goldens: every view with every fixture state that applies (the `load` frame belongs to the performance test), the delivery view in a space scope, the executive page of a space manager and TV mode, each in the full matrix as CI goldens; the timeline is split into one file per theme because its minute areas pass `repo.max_file_kb` in one file. Platform goldens on macOS keep one representative per view (the `normal` state and TV mode), as the size rule of D-M6-11-7 allows. | Brief, Tests; M6-11 design note 8. |
| D-M6-12-8 | 2026-10-16 | The timeline playhead moves in whole minutes, one minute every `60 / speed` seconds of the clock, and starts at the end of the period so the whole day is visible first; play at the end starts again at the beginning. In the full motion context the playhead glides with `motion.duration-move`, in the reduced context it jumps. Controls are buttons with text, because the icon set has no play or pause glyph. The rates leave out the `cost` buckets: they count bookings, and a count is no amount with currency and label (25 4 rule 2). | Brief, design note 5; 25 6.5 (minute precision); components.md 11. |
