Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the sign-out notice is `SignedOutNotice` (`SignedOut` is an event of `auth`), the REST reads are the port `Fetcher` with `ApiFetcher` over the generated API, `Dispatcher.apply` takes a `DecodedEnvelope` (the raw envelope with the `DecodedEvent` of `api_client`, null when malformed) plus the stored cursor and an `AfterCommit`, `Fetcher.myReadStates` takes a channel because the generated `listMyReadStates` requires `channel_id`, the write path of drafts calls `CatchUpListener.applyDraftPutResult`, and a failed gap fill is retried by `CatchUpListener.onFocus`. The wire mappers (`wire.dart`) and the identity port (`LocalIdentity`, `AuthIdentity`) are files beyond the brief's list. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-14-1 | 2026-10-16 | A gateway page is the run of `dispatch` frames up to the next `cursor` frame or `catchup.complete`; it is applied in one `commitPage` with the `seq` of that frame (never below the stored cursor) and the epoch of the session. In the live phase every durable `dispatch` is its own transaction with `cursor = seq`; ephemeral events apply at once without a transaction. A disconnect drops an unfinished page; its events come again from the stored cursor. | Design note 1; 04 7.1 (the server sends `cursor` at the end of every page) and 04 9 item 2. |
| D-M4-14-2 | 2026-10-16 | `ready` without a stored cursor empties the mirror and applies the whole snapshot with `cursor = head_seq` and the epoch in one transaction and goes `live` at once, because an `identify` without a cursor gets no catch-up and no `catchup.complete` (04 6.3 item 4; red team of M4-14); with a cursor only the parts that are not events apply (active run summaries by version, the counters, the agent presence, the feature flags into a stored instance row). After a whole snapshot the drafts are loaded with `listMyDrafts` when a draft reference is newer than or missing from the local drafts, the read states with `listMyReadStates` for the channels with unread threads, and the instance with `getInstance` (its flags replaced by the snapshot's). | Design note 1; without a cursor the mirror belongs to no known time line; the snapshot names drafts only by version and carries counters without a read state version. |
| D-M4-14-3 | 2026-10-16 | The outbox, the run store and the outbox start are hooks (`onEcho`, `onRunUpdated`, `onLive`), constructor parameters with no-op defaults, not dependencies. `onEcho` runs after the commit for a `message.created` with `client_msg_id` whose author is the person (or when the person is not known yet, after a `resume` in a new process); a truncated `message.created` echoes after its reload. | Design note 2; M4-17 wires them; outbox IDs are UUIDv7 of this device, so an echo of another author never matches an entry. |
| D-M4-14-4 | 2026-10-16 | `access.changed` in the middle of a page applies in its `seq` position (removal inside the page transaction, file bytes and `TargetsRemoved` after the commit) and the rest of the page applies after it. Only `4012` and `session.revoked` of this session stop a page: the page rolls back, the database is wiped (`LocalDatabase.wipe`), `SignedOutNotice(deleteLocalData: true)` is emitted and nothing is applied after that. `4012` reaches the listener as the connection state `signedOut`/`revoked` (`watchConnection`). | Design note 3; 04 8.1 and 8.3 item 2: the gateway withholds only events of removed targets, so dropping the rest would lose events that are never sent again. |
| D-M4-14-5 | 2026-10-16 | `session.revoked` wipes only when its `session_id` is this device's session (`LocalIdentity.sessionId`, the `sid` of `auth`) or the own session is unknown; a revocation of another session of the person (a logout on the phone) changes nothing here. | `session.revoked` has the audience `self` and reaches every device of the person (05 6); the revoked device itself is also closed with `4012` (04 8.5). |
| D-M4-14-6 | 2026-10-16 | Gaps: a `message.created` that continues a loaded range, or arrives in a thread with no loaded range, or lies below every loaded range joins the loaded ranges; otherwise it is stored without joining them, the gap from the end of the highest range below it up to it is recorded, and `Fetcher.range` loads it after the commit (`listThreadMessages` with `after_seq`, page by page); the fetched messages and the whole range up to the new message join the loaded ranges in one transaction. `MessagesDao.watchContiguous` therefore shows only the contiguous range ending at the newest loaded message. A failed fill keeps the gap recorded; `onFocus` retries it. | Design note 4; 04 4: a real gap is missing data, and a thread never opened here shows from its newest message without loading its history. |
| D-M4-14-7 | 2026-10-16 | Personal data: the echo of this device (`updated_by_device_id`) is ignored for drafts, settings and the sidebar; the read state is merged also for the own echo, because `mergeReadState` is monotonic and versioned and the counters come only from the server. A `draft.updated` or `draft.deleted` for a draft the person is typing on this device (`DraftEditing`) is not applied; the next local write wins. | Design note 5 and 04 14. |
| D-M4-14-8 | 2026-10-16 | A page whose transaction fails (a payload that breaks its mapping, a database error) leaves rows and cursor as they were; the listener then applies nothing until the next `ready` or `resumed`, which catches up from the stored cursor. An envelope that does not fit the schema of its type is skipped like an unknown type and the cursor passes it. | A later page must not move the cursor past events that were never applied; `SessionControl` has no close, and a reconnect repeats the page. |
| D-M4-14-9 | 2026-10-16 | When `getSnapshot` fails during a resync, the listener keeps mirror and cursor and retries the snapshot after the reconnect backoff (`sync.reconnect_backoff_base`, `sync.reconnect_backoff_cap`, full jitter) on the same connection; frames that arrive meanwhile wait in memory and apply after the snapshot with the duplicate rule. A disconnect ends the retries; the next session catches up from the stored cursor and gets `resync_required` again. `onLive` runs once per session, also when a `catchup.complete` follows a resync. | Code review of M4-14: dropping the mirror and halting left the app empty on an open connection until the next reconnect; after a restore every client asks for the snapshot at once. |
| D-M4-14-10 | 2026-10-16 | `packages/local_store` gets three methods beyond the brief's list, each with its test: `PersonalDao.deleteSettingIfNewer` and `deleteNotificationPreferenceIfNewer` (for `deleted: true`, 04 14) and `MessagesDao.reactionsOf` (reading the `me` flag of a reaction inside the page transaction, where a stream would wait for the transaction's end). | Fixed requirement 17 and Design note 5 need the deletions; no existing method removes those rows. |
| D-M4-14-11 | 2026-10-16 | `decideApply` decides per event between the version rule and the `seq` rule and drops a versioned type that arrives without `resource.version`; the comparison with the stored version is the conditional upsert of the DAOs (D-M4-12-3), one statement per row. Event payloads, the snapshot and REST answers go through one set of wire mappers from JSON to the domain types; the generated models of REST answers are serialized back to JSON first. The page size for the isolate decision is an estimate over the decoded frames (string lengths plus a few bytes per value). | One rule in one place; the gateway frames are already JSON-decoded by the connection, so their raw size is not at hand. |
| D-M4-14-12 | 2026-10-16 | REST reads that run after a commit (a truncated object, a gap fill, the newest page of a focused thread, the structure of `added` or `changed` targets, drafts and read states after a snapshot) store their result only when, inside the transaction of the write, the target is still held: content needs its channel row and neither the channel, its space nor the thread may have been removed in this process (`SyncContext.removedTargets`); a view of `changed` needs its row. An `access.changed` with `added` (for its spaces and channels and for every thread its structure load lists), a `thread.moved` and a snapshot that lists a target take it out of the removed set (follow-up review of M4-14: a moved-out thread stayed blocked after its new channel became visible). | Code review of M4-14, reproduced: a `getMessage` in flight during `access.changed` with `removed` stored the message and its search row again (04 8.3 item 3, T-DV-01). |
| D-M4-14-13 | 2026-10-18 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with its own commits and the integration fix INT-W33/3 (the doc comments of `fetcher.dart` and `sync_events.dart` name `ApiClient` and `AuthSession`, so `make docs` resolves them); the English chapter 15 of main takes the page boundary of 7.3. The event catalogue of main has two durable types more than the base, `device.pending` and `gdpr.request_due`; neither has a local table, so they join the skipped set of `catalogue_test.dart` and the README. | Tickets are built in parallel on integration bases, main takes each as one pull request with the integration fixes it needs; the other fixes of that wave belong to M6-01. |
| D-M4-14-14 | 2026-10-19 | The snapshot retry of a resync (D-M4-14-9) checks its session after the snapshot fetch and after the follow-up steps of its commit (`markSnapshot`, `_afterCommit`, which always run once the snapshot is committed, because only their reads restore the instance row and the read states) and ends when a new session started; only the retry of the current session sets `lastProcessedSeq`, clears `_resyncing`, sets `live`, reports `Resynced` and drains the held frames. The drain flag names its session (`_drainingSession`): a drain of an ended session no longer makes the next session hold its frames. | CodeRabbit on the pull request of M4-14, reproduced with `resync_session_test.dart`: a disconnect and a new `ready` while the retry awaited its commit set the new session live during its catch-up and reported `Resynced` for it, and frames of a new session that arrived while the old drain was inside a frame stayed in the hold list after that drain ended, so the next `cursor` or `catchup.complete` moved the cursor past them (04 9, D-M4-14-1). |

## M4-17 Sync engine: fake server and composition root

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-17-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave35` at `4773519`, branch `feat/M4-17-fake-server` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100 and D-W-114 are on the base), so nothing is ported; the API documents of the base hold every operation of Design note 3. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` and `04-sync-protocol.md` are `15-client-architektur.md` and `04-sync-protokoll.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds there are English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the local store is `LocalDatabase`; the REST client is `AuthSession.api`, which the session already builds with its interceptors, so `SyncEngine.create` does not build a second one; `create` also needs `client` (`GatewayClientInfo` for `identify`) and takes `ids`, `draftEditing`, `keepConnectedInBackground`, `allowPlainHttpForTests` and `logHook`; `focus` is an `EngineFocus` over the `FocusManager`; the engine exposes `state` and `states`. Fault names: `delay(duration, operationId:)`, `revoke(deviceId:)`, plus `holdDispatches` and `releaseDispatches`; a lost network is `FakeServer.disconnect`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-17-1 | 2026-10-17 | One composition root: `SyncEngine.create` builds the parts of M4-13 to M4-16 and connects every hook (echo to `Outbox.confirmByEcho` with `message_id` and `version`; the catch-up's live signal to `Outbox.start`; every disconnect to `Outbox.pause`; `run.updated` to `RunStore.onRunUpdated`; `run.state_changed` to an end state to `RunStore.onRunEnded`; focused runs into the gateway focus through `EngineFocus.focusRun`). The run end is read by a listener that wraps the catch-up listener after it applied the frame, so the dispatcher of M4-14 stays unchanged. | Brief Design note 2: the desktop and the mobile app cannot wire the hooks differently. |
| D-M4-17-2 | 2026-10-17 | The fake's REST side routes by the paths of `openapi.yaml` (a literal segment before a parameter) and keeps a handler registry: `FakeServer.handle(operationId, handler)`; this ticket's handlers register from one file per group under `lib/src/testing/rest/`; an operation without a handler answers `501` and is recorded. | Later tickets add operations without editing this file (Design note 3). |
| D-M4-17-3 | 2026-10-17 | Faults are deterministic: `FaultPlan` is a queue of REST rules matched in order and used up per request (applied after the token and proof checks, so a nonce retry does not eat a fault), and gateway faults act at once or after a fixed delay; IDs of the world are counters. | A failing case must repeat on every run (Design note 4, 15 20 rationale). |
| D-M4-17-4 | 2026-10-17 | Contract checks in the fake with an own validator (`ContractSchemas`) for the subset the documents use, plus `allOf` and the bounds `minimum`, `maximum`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `minItems`, `maxItems` and `pattern`; no JSON Schema package is in the lock file, so no dependency was added. Invalid request bodies answer `400 validation_failed` with the pointers and are recorded; a response the fake would send against the contract becomes a recorded `500`; a frame of the fake against the contract throws; an invalid client frame closes with `4002`. Every scenario fails on a recorded violation. | The fake can never drift from the contract silently (Design note 5). |
| D-M4-17-5 | 2026-10-17 | The scenarios of 04 16 are functions over the interface `ScenarioClient` (send, offline and online, local messages, outbox, channels, notices, cursor, focus, run document, run state) and a running `FakeServer`; they throw `ScenarioFailure` instead of using `package:test`, so the library has no test framework as a runtime dependency. `test/scenarios/` implements the interface over `SyncEngine`; M4-18 implements it over the app. | One set of flows for the headless and the app tests (Design note 7, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-17-6 | 2026-10-17 | `SyncEngine` accepts only an `https` base URL (gateway `wss`); `allowPlainHttpForTests: true` admits `http` (gateway `ws`) for the fake and the app's test entry point. `tools/arch-check` gains rule 10 as a general rule: a library named `testing.dart` of any member and its `lib/src/testing/` folder are imported or exported only from `test/` and `integration_test/` folders, also relatively inside the own member; 15 3.1 lists it. | DPoP and the device binding assume a protected transport (15 8); test support never reaches a production build (Design note 8). |
| D-M4-17-7 | 2026-10-17 | The gateway's `hello` carries the same nonce as the REST `DPoP-Nonce` and the gateway accepts the current and the previous one, as REST does. | 08 7.3 makes nonces stateless for every process; the client keeps one nonce store, so a gateway-only nonce made the next REST call fail with `use_dpop_nonce` (found by the first scenario run). |
| D-M4-17-8 | 2026-10-17 | `crypto` and `pointycastle` move from `dev_dependencies` to `dependencies` of `packages/sync_engine`, and `yaml` (already in the lock file) is added there: `lib/testing.dart` lives under `lib/` and needs them; the production library does not import them. | Brief Scope (`pubspec.yaml`: the dependencies the fake needs); their licenses (BSD-3-Clause, MIT) are on the allowed list. |
| D-M4-17-9 | 2026-10-17 | `FakeWorld.append` takes `resource.version` from the event catalogue of `domain`: a type the catalogue marks without it never carries one (reactions do not raise the message version, `read_state.updated` and `settings.updated` carry the version only in the payload), and a type that needs it fails the write; the contract test checks every logged event against the catalogue. | 04 9.3; found by the code review: a foreign version made the fake answer `412` to an edit after a reaction. |
| D-M4-17-10 | 2026-10-17 | Fixes after the red team: the outbox's `onConfirmed` stores the `Message` a confirmed `createMessage` or `updateMessage` returned through the new `CatchUpListener.storeConfirmedMessage` (version rule, removed targets skipped), so a sent message never disappears between its response and its echo; after `Resynced` the engine ends the documents of focused runs that the local run summaries list as ended or no longer hold; the fake computes `unread_threads` and the read state's `mention_count` (messages of others after the read position, not deleted). | 04 13.2 item 6, 04 7.3 with 15 7.4, 04 14; red team of M4-17. |

## M4-18 Desktop app shell

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-18-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave36` at `8493d3f`, branch `feat/M4-18-desktop-shell` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-116 and `auth.device_pending_ttl` in `AppConfig` are on the base), so nothing is ported. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` is `15-client-architektur.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds there are English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the local store is `LocalDatabase` and its wipe `LocalDatabase.wipe`; `openLocalStore` takes `file` and `keys`; the device key client is `DeviceKeyClient` with `ensure`, `sign` and `delete(scope)`, whose `StepUpPrompt` (texts) is a different type from the `StepUpPrompt` port of `auth` (a question); `LoginService` has no discovery call, so E1 calls `getDiscovery` through the generated client; the fake server has no handlers for `getSpace`, `getChannel` and `getThread`, which no flow of this ticket needs against it (the target lookup is tested with a stub client). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-18-1 | 2026-10-17 | `bootstrap.dart` composes per server address (`InstanceServices.open`): the device keys with the `instance_id` of discovery as scope, `InstanceSecrets` with every key prefixed `instance/<instance_id>/`, the database key (32 bytes of `Random.secure()`, stored as `database_key`), the encrypted database `<application support>/instances/<instance_id>/local.db`, the `AuthSession` with browser, loopback receiver and (where present) passkey port, and the `SyncEngine` (`keepConnectedInBackground`). `AppPorts` holds every port and `appPortsProvider` hands it out, so tests replace all of them. On macOS only a release build uses the Data Protection keychain with the keychain access group; a debug or profile build is not signed by a team, has no access group and keeps the items in the login keychain. | One instance never sees the secrets or keys of another, and a wipe can find everything by the instance ID; an access group without a team signature makes the app fail to launch. |
| D-M4-18-2 | 2026-10-17 | One `AccountWiper` per instance for every end of an account: logout, E4 "Cancel", a rejected or expired waiting device, `4012`, `session.revoked`, `401 session_revoked`, and also a refresh token the server no longer accepts (E7 "session expired"). It stops the engine, wipes the database, deletes the instance folder (database files and file cache), both device keys and every instance secret, runs every step even when one fails and keeps only the server address. The controller reacts to the `SignedOut` events of the session and to the engine's `SignedOutNotice` through one guarded path. | 01 5.6 shows "The data of this device has been deleted" on every E7; one path cannot forget a step for one cause. |
| D-M4-18-3 | 2026-10-17 | E1 classifies discovery: a `HandshakeException`, a `TlsException` or Dio's `badCertificate` is "Certificate invalid" with no way to continue and no setting to trust a certificate; `setup_required` (flag or problem) is "Instance not set up yet"; a `min_client_versions` entry of the platform above the build's version (numeric `major.minor.patch`) is "App too old"; everything else is "No instance at this address". Only the integration test entry accepts `http`. The address is remembered after a found instance and asked again at the next start. | R-PR-14: an exception button would let an attacker in the middle defeat DPoP and the device binding. |
| D-M4-18-4 | 2026-10-17 | The router has the paths of 15 11 plus `/onboarding/<step>` and `/settings/...`; `parseLocation` is a pure function that checks IDs with the ID types of `domain`. The redirect follows the onboarding controller: signed out, a content path goes to the current screen and is kept, and after the login it opens. A target that is not in the local database and that REST answers with `404` or `403` shows "Not found or no access"; without an answer it waits. Deep links: macOS registers the URL type in `Info.plist` and the app delegate forwards links; the Windows runner registers the scheme under `HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Classes` at every start (no administrator rights; MSIX of M7 replaces it), a second start with a link sends it to the running window by `WM_COPYDATA` and exits; both forward over the method channel `bothy/deep_links` (`takePending` for a link that started the app, `open` for later ones). | 15 11; a per-user registration works for a local build without an installer. |
| D-M4-18-5 | 2026-10-17 | Column names `sidebar`, `post_list`, `history`, `session`; the sized columns default to the layout tokens `DsLayout.sidebarWidth`, `listWidth`, `sessionWidth`, never below `DsLayout.columnMin`, and the history keeps at least `columnMin`. One drag is one `putMySetting` of `layout.column_widths` through the outbox (only the widths that differ from the defaults); the dragged widths stay until the stored setting comes back from `user_settings`. "Reset" is `deleteMySetting` (the setting's default is empty). Below `ui.desktop_min_width_four_columns` the session column is not shown, below `ui.desktop_min_width_three_columns` the post list and the history share one place. Until M4-25 the sidebar frame carries the "Log out" button. | 01 5.1 and 11; a column that jumps back while the write travels would look broken. |
| D-M4-18-6 | 2026-10-17 | `CodeText` shows a code in groups (four by default; the verification code of E4 in groups of three as the wireframe of 01 5.6 shows it) in the mono font of the tokens, offers copy without spaces, and its semantics label spells the code with commas between the characters while the visible groups are excluded from semantics. Remaining times are text ("Valid for 23 more minutes") from `expires_at` (E5) or `created_at` plus `auth.device_pending_ttl` (E4) and an injected clock, announced through the `LiveAnnouncer` of M4-07 at most once per `a11y.live_announce_min_interval`. | 01 5.6 and 8: a screen reader must not read "481" as a number. |
| D-M4-18-7 | 2026-10-17 | Build branding before M7: `apps/client/branding.example.yaml` (`app_name` `ExampleChat`, `url_scheme` `chat-example`, `bundle_id` `com.example.chat`, `default_server` `https://chat.example.com`) and `tool/branding_defines.dart`, which writes `build/branding/dart_defines.json` for `--dart-define-from-file` and the checked-in `macos/Runner/Configs/Branding.xcconfig` and `windows/runner/branding.h`; `--check` (and a test) holds the checked-in files equal to the example. The interface shows `branding.product_name` of the instance after discovery; the build's app name only names the window before it. | Design note 7; one source for the Dart side and both runners. |
| D-M4-18-8 | 2026-10-17 | Network status without `connectivity_plus`: `LifecycleNetworkStatus` reports online and emits "network may be back" on every return to the foreground; a lost network shows through the gateway's failed connects and heartbeat (04 6.5). | No new dependency in the license and OSV gates; the engine already retries with backoff. |
| D-M4-18-9 | 2026-10-17 | The desktop composition passes no `PasskeyAuthenticator`, because no package of the workspace bridges WebAuthn on macOS or Windows yet; E2 and E3 offer passkeys only when the ports have one (the tests and goldens inject one). E3 offers "Use this device" (`redeemEnrollmentCode`), "Create passkey" and "Link with" each login service; it offers no "Set up password and one-time code", because the API has no operation that sets a password and a TOTP secret while a code is redeemed (spec gap, reported). | Fixed requirement 13 ("where offered"); P-11: no deviation from the contract. |
| D-M4-18-10 | 2026-10-17 | When the poll of a waiting device ends because the server ended its session first (`401 session_revoked` before `getDevice` could report the revoke reason), E7 shows "not confirmed in time" once the app's own deadline (`created_at` plus `auth.device_pending_ttl`) has passed and "the confirmation was rejected" before it; a revoke reason that arrives is shown as it is. | The device cannot read its revoke reason without a session; the deadline is the only fact it has. |
| D-M4-18-11 | 2026-10-17 | E6 is built as a screen with its states and goldens and has its route `/onboarding/locked`; the onboarding controller never enters it, and "Unlock" does nothing until M5-08 builds the lock. | Brief Out of scope. |
| D-M4-18-12 | 2026-10-17 | The new pub packages of `apps/client` and their transitive packages get one `PackageOverrides` entry each in the root `osv-scanner.toml` (name and exact version), checked against the LICENSE file of that version: MIT (Riverpod, `state_notifier`, `uuid`, `freezed_annotation`, `analyzer_buffer`), Apache-2.0 (`mockito`) and BSD-3-Clause (the flutter.dev and Dart team packages, `flutter_secure_storage`, `ffi_leak_tracker`, `win32`). `quality/licenses/allowed.txt` is unchanged. | deps.dev has no license data for these pub packages, so OSV-Scanner reports them as UNKNOWN (docs/quality/licenses.md). |
| D-M4-18-13 | 2026-10-17 | The app remembers the public description of the last instance discovery found (`app/instance`: address, `instance_id`, product name, accent, login methods; no logo, no secret). A start or "Connect" whose discovery gets no answer at all (`DiscoveryUnreachable`: no network, host down) opens the remembered instance from its local data when its address matches and a refresh token is stored, and shows the main view (or E4 for a waiting device); otherwise E1 shows "No instance at this address". A found instance with a stored session resumes on every connect, not only at the start. | 01 3.6 (offline reading) and 6.6 (the offline banner): a signed-in person must reach the local data without network. |
| D-M4-18-14 | 2026-10-17 | Fixes after the red team: E1 leaves the "connecting" state when discovery found the instance and on E2 "Change"; stored column widths are fitted to the window (`fitColumns`: the sized columns shrink in proportion to their width above `DsLayout.columnMin`, so the history keeps its minimum); the onboarding card runs its main action on Enter only from the card itself or a text field, a focused button or radio keeps Enter for itself, and the text fields no longer submit on their own, so one Enter is one action. | Red team round 1 (four reproduced Majors), each with a test. |
| D-M4-18-15 | 2026-10-17 | One pure function `routeStart` (`apps/client/lib/onboarding/start_route.dart`) decides the screen of every start and "Connect" from what is stored for the instance (no session, a session of an active device, a session of a waiting device), the discovery result and what `getDevice` says about the device. A certificate error, `setup_required` and a too old app show E1 with their text; a stored session of the remembered instance at that address otherwise opens its local data whatever else the network answered (no answer, a timeout, a `4xx` or `5xx` page of a reverse proxy, a captive portal): the main view, or E4 for a waiting device, where the server's answer about the device wins over the stored state; without a session a found instance shows E2 and everything else "No instance at this address". E4 stores its verification code and `created_at` as the instance secret `onboarding/pending_device` (deleted at the login and by the wiper), so a waiting device started offline shows E4 with its code and polls until the network returns. This refines D-M4-18-13, which opened the local data only without any answer. A table test runs every stored state times every network result times "comes online" or "stays offline" through the controller against the fake server. | Delta red team: a proxy's `502`, `503`, `504` sent a signed-in person to E1, and a waiting device started offline landed in the empty main view; both were instances of one routing rule spread over two places. |
| D-M4-18-16 | 2026-10-18 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with only its own commits, stacked on the port of M4-17; the English docs of main win: the two paragraphs of 15 9 and 15 11 sit in the English chapter, and code comments point to `01-product.md` and `15-client-architecture.md`. The lock file is resolved again on main (`flutter pub get`, only `dart_style` differs from the base, as on main), the two goldens of E1 with the "app too old" banner are rerendered, and the goldens compare with `DsGoldenFileComparator` (PL/5). `sonar.coverage.exclusions` leaves `apps/*/macos/**`, `apps/*/windows/**` and `apps/*/tool/**` out of the coverage measure. The skip line of the Riverpod generation step is `codegen-riverpod-dart`, like the drift and Pigeon steps. `osv-scanner.toml` gets the license overrides of `flutter_driver`, `integration_test`, `fuchsia_remote_debug_protocol`, `flutter_web_plugins`, `sync_http` and `webdriver`, which the base had from M6-12 and M6-02. The fake server of M4-17 lists the local login driver in `auth_providers` of discovery, as the server does (`loginoptions` lists every enabled provider); without it E2 showed no login name and every integration test of the app failed on the desktop runners. | Main carries its own value of `color.warning.subtle` (`palette.amber.100`), which the banner shows (as D-M4-07-9 found); without the comparator the CI goldens recorded on macOS fail on the Linux runner at anti-aliased edges, as those of design_system and bothy_viz did. The runners run only on the platform runners and write no report the Linux scan reads (as D-M4-10-7 for the plugin members), and `flutter test --coverage` records only `lib/`, so the tested branding tool counted as uncovered (as D-M4-06-8 for the members' tools). |
| D-M4-18-17 | 2026-10-19 | The fake server of `sync_engine` carries a gzip and base64 copy of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and `docs/api/asyncapi.yaml` in `lib/src/testing/contract_documents.g.dart` (`ContractSchemas.embedded`); `ContractSchemas.load` still walks up to `docs/api/` and uses the copy only when there is none. `tools/catalog/contract_documents_dart.py` writes it (`make contract-documents`), and `make codegen-check` decodes it and fails when its text differs from `docs/api/`, so a change to the API documents needs a regeneration. The macOS app sandbox stays on in every build configuration; no asset is added to the app. | Steering ruling 2026-10-19: the app's integration tests ran on a device for the first time, and inside the macOS app sandbox the fake could not read the contract files (working directory `/`, no access to the repository), so no route and no contract check existed. A Flutter asset would ship 1 MB of YAML in every release build, a sandbox exception would weaken debug builds; the copy is test code in `package:sync_engine/testing.dart`, which no release build imports (tools/arch-check rule 10). The copy is random-looking text, so `.gitleaks.toml` allows its path; `docs/api/` itself stays scanned. |
| D-M4-18-18 | 2026-10-19 | The Windows failure "file in use" when a test deleted its data folder is a test teardown issue, not a product one: the product wipe (`AccountWiper`, every sign-out cause) awaits the engine stop and `LocalDatabase.wipe`, which closes the database before it deletes the files, and only then deletes the instance folder; `logout_wipes_test.dart` checks on the Windows job that the folder is gone. Only a test that removes the app leaves the close to the controller's dispose, which does not wait (the app quits). `FakeInstance.stop` retries the delete, within a bounded time, until that close is done. | Steering ruling 2026-10-19: check the product path first and fix it if it has the same bug; reading the code and the device run of the wipe test show it does not. |
| D-M4-18-19 | 2026-10-19 | `apps/client/platform-tests.json` runs each integration test file in its own `flutter test <file> -d macos` or `-d windows` call; a new file needs its line there. | On both desktop runners every file after the first failed with "Unable to start the app on the device" (the log reader of the next app start stopped) so each file gets its own call and a fresh app process, and a failure names its file. |
| D-M4-18-20 | 2026-10-19 | Discovery accepts an `instance_id` only as a UUID (`canonicalUuid` of `domain`, stored in lowercase); any other value is "no instance" in E1, and a remembered instance whose ID is no UUID is not read back. | Review finding on PR #138: the ID names the data folder, the device key scope and the secret prefix, and the wipe deletes that folder recursively, so a server answering `..` could make a sign-out delete data outside its instance. |
| D-M4-18-21 | 2026-10-20 | A test that removes the app waits for the close of its services before the fake stops and the data folder goes: `InstanceServices.close` returns the one close begun first (the app's dispose begins it without waiting), and `TestApp.stop` and `SecondApp.stop` await it. The bounded retry of `FakeInstance` (D-M4-18-18, D-M4-25-9) stays only as the last resort. | The same failure came back on the Windows runner after the retry: `pairing_test` and `login_pending_confirm_test` failed with `PathAccessException` (errno 32) on `client-flow*` once the bounded retry (`closeTimeout`) ran out, so the close the dispose began had not ended while the fake stopped under it. Awaited before the fake stops, the close ended at once in every repeated run of the three affected files on the Windows runner (a diagnostic build on 2026-10-20), and no delete needed a retry. Test: `services_close_test.dart`. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

The `/settings/<section>` routes are registered in `app.dart` next to the other `GoRoute`s, because `router.dart` only parses locations (D-M4-18-4). | The task of the build lane overrides the merge step of the brief; the router file has no route table. Sources: brief M4-25 Boundaries. |
| D-M4-25-1 | 2026-10-17 | Settings defaults live on the client (`PersonalSettings.resolve`): an absent key or a value outside the list of 01 11 reads as the default; `composer.enter_sends` defaults to `true` on desktop, `privacy.crash_reports` to `true` only with a crash target, `privacy.client_metrics` to `true`. Choosing a device-independent default deletes the key (`deleteMySetting`), every other change is one `putMySetting` through the outbox; the local row changes at once. "Column widths: Reset" deletes `layout.column_widths`. | 04 14 stores no defaults, because some depend on the device or the instance. Sources: 01 11, 04 14, brief M4-25 Design note 1. |

Sources: 15 3, 3.1. |
| D-M4-25-3 | 2026-10-17 | `StepUpDialog` and `StepUpDialogPrompt` (the `StepUpPrompt` of M4-09) live in `admin_ui`; `AppPorts` installs the prompt over the router's navigator key, replacing the `ConfirmingStepUpPrompt` of M4-18. The dialog appears only when the server answers `401 step_up_required` (`device.approve`, `device.revoke`); ending a session asks one confirmation and never for step-up. On a pairing `404` the server ends the step-up of the session (the fake server does the same), so the next attempt asks again (08 5.3). | Step-up handling stays in the `StepUpService` of M4-09; the screen adds no step-up of its own (05). Sources: 08 5.2, 5.3, 6; brief M4-25 Design note 3. |

Sources: brief M4-25 Design note 4. |
| D-M4-25-5 | 2026-10-17 | The device list shows no hardware binding label: the `Device` schema of `docs/api/` has no hardware or attestation field. Revoking this device or ending the current session calls `OnboardingController.endLocally`, which runs the wiper of M4-18 without a server logout and shows E7. Time zones come from the IANA database of the `timezone` package. | 01 A1 names the label, but the contract does not carry it; the gap is reported, not built (P-11). Sources: 01 5.1 D8, 08 10, R-SY-14. |
| D-M4-25-6 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave40` on top of M4-18: the ticket's own commits only, with the doc comment fix of INT-W40/4 (`ApiSelfRepository` names its constructor argument as `` `api` ``); the rulings stand in their own section; the move of the early rulings to `DECISIONS-EARLY.md` (INT-W40/3) is not ported, because main's log is below `repo.max_file_kb`. | The PR lane ports only the ticket and the integration fixes it needs. |
| D-M4-25-7 | 2026-10-19 | The integration tests `devices_test.dart` and `settings_sync_test.dart` of this ticket get their lines in `apps/client/platform-tests.json`, so the platform jobs run them on macOS and Windows; the source references of D-M4-25-0 to D-M4-25-5 move into the reason cell, as the table has four columns. | D-M4-18-19 asks a line for each new file; the register check refuses a row with more cells than its header. |
| D-M4-25-8 | 2026-10-19 | Ending the own session from the settings ends in E7 at once: `GatewayConnection.dispose` no longer waits for the close of its state stream, and `OnboardingController` sets no state after its provider was disposed; the platform test of the theme reset taps the `system` choice inside `settings.theme`. | The first platform runs of `devices_test.dart` hung 20 seconds in the wipe: the status line provider listens to the state stream and Riverpod pauses it while the settings cover the shell, and the close of a broadcast stream waits for paused listeners; the late `state` write then threw `UnmountedRefException`. Theme and motion both offer `system`, so the old finder matched two chips. Tests: `session_test.dart` (a paused listener does not hold dispose) and `controller_test.dart` (an end that outlives its container). |
| D-M4-25-9 | 2026-10-20 | `FakeInstance.stopSibling` deletes the data folder of the second app with the retry of `stop` (D-M4-18-18), until its database is closed or `closeTimeout` ends. | On the Windows runner `settings_sync_test.dart` failed in its teardown with `PathAccessException` (errno 32): the disposed container closes the database without waiting, and Windows refuses to delete an open file. Both deletes now share one helper. |

The new goldens of `admin_ui` had never run on Linux and failed there without the comparator of PL/5. Both were reproduced in a Linux amd64 container with Flutter 3.47.7, where all goldens of `apps/client`, `admin_ui` and `bothy_viz` pass after the change. Test: `formatting_test.dart`. |

## M4-19 Sidebar column, space tree and forum post list

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-19-0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave38` at `297ab0c`, branch `feat/M4-19-sidebar-post-list` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks, so nothing is ported; the base names the spec chapter `01-produkt.md`, and the sentence this ticket adds there is English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the engine's counters are `catchUp.countsOfChannel` (no separate `UnreadCounter` instance in the app); `postListProvider` takes the channel and a `PostListFilter` that carries the sort; the executors are registered through the `registerExecutors` hook of `SyncEngine.create` in `bootstrap.dart`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-19-1 | 2026-10-18 | Data path: every provider of the sidebar and the post list reads `local_store` only (R-CL-03): spaces with `user_space_states`, channels per space, read states, the person's notification preferences, runs that have not ended, threads with their tags, forum tags, principals. The counters of a channel are the engine's values per thread (snapshot, `read_state.updated`, its own counting of `message.*`, 04 14), else the stored read state; muted is only the channel's own row with level `mute` and `muted_until` absent or in the future. The mapping to the row data of M4-07 is in small pure functions (`sidebar_model.dart`, `forum_model.dart`) with unit tests. | Brief Design note 1; the app never derives a counter or a control from grants (15 1). |
| D-M4-19-2 | 2026-10-18 | The order key lives in `domain` as `FractionalIndex` (`between`, `isValid`), the same rule as the server's `fracindex` of M1-52 with its normative vectors; the fake server validates `putMySidebarSpace` with it. A drag or "Move up/down" writes one key between the new neighbours; when the neighbours leave no room (equal keys, or a global key outside the index), every space gets a fresh key in the new order and each changed one is written. The local row changes at once; while a later write of the same space waits in the outbox, an earlier answer is not stored. | One shared key rule, so a key the client sends never fails with `400`. |
| D-M4-19-3 | 2026-10-18 | "Waiting for me" shows the open and done posts with a run that the reader triggered in `waiting_input`, or with an unread mention of the reader; the tag filter keeps posts with at least one selected tag; filters last while the app runs. | Brief Design note 3. |
| D-M4-19-4 | 2026-10-18 | The collapse state of the Done section is kept per forum on this device in the local-only table `forum_view_states` (`channel_id`, `done_collapsed`), schema version 2 of `local_store` with its migration and survival tests; collapsed by default; not synchronized. 01 6.1 says so. | 01 11 has no key for it, and no new personal setting key may be added. |
| D-M4-19-5 | 2026-10-18 | Paging: the post list loads the first page of `listChannelThreads` (sort of the list) when it opens and the next ones when scrolled within 400 logical pixels of the end; pages go into `threads`. The Done label counts the loaded done posts that match the filters and reads "Done (n+)" while a further page may exist, because the API returns no total. A failed page keeps the cursor for the next try. | Brief Design note 3. |
| D-M4-19-6 | 2026-10-18 | The fake server lists a local identity provider in its discovery: E2 offers the login name only with a provider of kind `local`, so without it no desktop integration test could sign in (they also run on `flutter-tester`). The failure reason of `DsThreadRow` and the name and "New post" of the post list header wrap instead of overflowing a narrow column at text scale 2, which the goldens of this ticket showed. | A test path that cannot sign in proves nothing; 01 8 text scaling. |
| D-M4-19-7 | 2026-10-18 | The time of a post row is shown in the device's time zone: the local store does not hold `users.time_zone`, so the wall clock of this device decides "today", and the clock time or the day is formatted from it. | No new local column for one label; the person's zone arrives with the settings of M4-25. |
| D-M4-19-8 | 2026-10-18 | After the code review: (a) "New post" offers every tag of the forum, restricted ones too, and the server decides; (b) a `createThread` the server refused stays in the outbox and the post list shows it at the top with its reason, "Edit" (the New post dialog filled with it, then `Outbox.edit`), "Try again" (`Outbox.retry`) and "Discard" (`Outbox.discard`); (c) the sync engine keeps the `head_seq` of the last snapshot (`SyncContext.countedUpToSeq`) and does not count a message event at or below it, which the catch-up replays after `ready` and which the snapshot's counters already hold (D-M4-19-9); (d) after a failed page the post list loads again only when the person scrolls or presses "Try again" in the row it shows at the end of the open posts; (e) the created thread goes through `CatchUpListener.storeConfirmedThread` (version rule, title index, held channel); (f) the post list keeps its scroll offset per forum while the app runs, and the shell keeps the last route selection while a new one resolves. | 15 1 principle 1; 04 13.2 item 5 (nothing is dropped silently); 04 14; no request loop; 14 6; opening a post must not lose the place in a long forum. |
| D-M4-19-9 | 2026-10-18 | After the red team: the counter guard of D-M4-19-8(c) judges replayed events by their global `seq` against the snapshot's `head_seq`, not by `thread_seq`, so a replayed deletion or edit no longer cancels a mention the snapshot already holds; the route selection of `/c/<id>` watches the stored channel, so a forum opened by a link before the first snapshot shows its post list once the channel arrives; the generated domain tables are again byte for byte what their generators write. | 04 14 (the snapshot holds the state at its head); 15 11; `make codegen-check`. |
| D-M4-19-10 | 2026-10-18 | Class fix after the delta red team, replacing the guard of D-M4-19-8(c) and D-M4-19-9: the order of server values and own counting lives in one place, `UnreadCounter` (domain). Every server value holds the events up to a global `seq` (the snapshot its `head_seq`, a `read_state.updated` its own `seq`); every counted message keeps the `seq` of the event that counted it. An event or a server value at or below the `seq` a thread's values hold changes nothing (the snapshot seeds a new counter at its head). A newer server value drops the mentions counted from events at or below its `seq`, and the unread messages too where it delivers an unread count; a computed unread count (`read_state.updated` carries none) covers only the messages up to the stored `last_message_seq` and the read position, so messages counted above both stay counted. The sync engine only passes each event's `seq`; `SyncContext.countedUpToSeq` is gone. A seeded model test runs random histories of created, edited, deleted and read events with a random cursor, head and live tail through the engine against the server's exact values. | The three findings (a replayed deletion, a replayed edit, a replayed read state changing a counter the snapshot already set) were instances of one class: a counter change judged without the position of the server value; a live read on another device also kept mentions counted before it. The class-fix red team found that a partial live read dropped unread messages its computed value does not hold (fixed with a regression test; the model reads partially and sends no unread count). 04 14. |
| D-M4-19-11 | 2026-10-19 | Port onto main from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave40` on top of M4-25: the ticket's own commits with INT-W40/2 (the sidebar column is the `SidebarColumn` of M4-19 with the settings entry and "Log out" in its footer, the column widths go through the settings repository of M4-25) and INT-W40/5 (`postListLoadFailed` is an exception of the term table check); the rulings stand in their own section. `UnreadCounter.resetFrom` joins the ordering by global `seq` of D-M4-19-10 with main's later fix of M4-08, which drops buffered mentions and unread messages at or below the new read boundary (`GREATEST(last_read_seq, unread_floor_seq)`): an entry is dropped when either rule drops it. The local identity provider of D-M4-19-6 is already in main's fake server (`FakeWorld.localProviderId`, from the port of M4-18), so the fake keeps main's field and its discovery entry and adds only the logo. The spec sentence of D-M4-19-4 is written into the English chapter 01, and comments name the English chapter files. | The PR lane ports only the ticket and the integration fixes it needs; both counter fixes answer reproduced defects, and neither may be lost. |
| D-M4-19-12 | 2026-10-19 | The integration tests `forum_test.dart` and `sidebar_sync_test.dart` of this ticket get their lines in `apps/client/platform-tests.json`, so the platform jobs run them on macOS and Windows. | D-M4-18-19 asks a line for each new integration test file. |
| D-M4-19-13 | 2026-10-20 | The goldens of the sidebar and the post list put their column at the top left of a cell of `cellWidth` (460), wider than the longest scenario label of alchemist, as D-M4-07-10 does for the component sheets; the goldens are recorded again. | The cells took the width of their label, a fraction of a pixel wider than a whole number, so the last pixel column of the light goldens stayed transparent on the Linux runner and the CI comparison failed there (reproduced in a Linux amd64 container with Flutter 3.47.7, where the new goldens pass). |
| D-M4-19-14 | 2026-10-20 | `CatchUpListener.dispose`, `EphemeralStore.dispose` and `Outbox.close` close their broadcast streams without waiting for the done event, as `GatewayConnection.dispose` does since D-M4-25-8. | The counter provider of the sidebar listens to `countChanges` with `await for`, and a paused listener holds the done event of a broadcast stream: the engine never finished its dispose, the database stayed open, and the Windows runner could not delete the data folder after `login_pending_confirm_test.dart` (`PathAccessException`, errno 32). Same class as D-M4-25-8, so every broadcast close of the engine follows the rule. Test: `test/catch_up/dispose_test.dart`. |
| D-M4-19-15 | 2026-10-20 | `CatchUpListener.dispose` no longer waits for the background reads: it marks itself disposed, queued reads do not start, and a running read that ends later fails on the closed database and is logged like every failed read. | On the Windows runner `pairing_test.dart` still could not delete the data folder: after pairing, the read of `getInstance` behind the snapshot had no answer when the app was removed, and `dispose` waited for it without a bound, so the database stayed open (traced in a headless run). A read is best effort and runs again with the next snapshot. Test: `test/catch_up/dispose_test.dart`. |
| D-M4-19-16 | 2026-10-20 | `ForumPager` watches `syncEngineProvider`: a new engine (a logout, a new login) starts the paging of every post list again. | The pager kept its cursor and `hasMore: false` across a logout and a new login, so the post list of the new, empty database never loaded its first pages until a restart (CodeRabbit on PR #159, reproduced by `test/forum/pager_test.dart`). |

## M4-20 Thread view, input and message actions

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-20-0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave40` at `35d6a9c`, branch `feat/M4-20-thread-view` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-115, SEC-OUT-1 to SEC-OUT-3, SEC-T13 and the actions of 05 are on the base), so nothing is ported; the base names the chapters in German (`01-produkt.md`, `04-sync-protokoll.md`, `15-client-architektur.md`). Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the outbox view is `OutboxEntryView` with `OutboxStateLabel`; the executors register through the `registerExecutors` hook of `SyncEngine.create`; the app's executors are `AppMessageOps` in `sync/message_ops_app.dart` and the thread from a message is a payload form of `ThreadOps.createThread`; the composer widget is `composer/thread_composer.dart` next to the brief's files, the REST reads are `thread/thread_reads.dart`, the rows are built by `thread/history_model.dart`, the D2 layout by `thread/history_area.dart`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-20-1 | 2026-10-18 | Read state: while the newest confirmed message of a thread without a gap after it is in view and the window has the focus (`AppLifecycle` in the foreground and Flutter's state `resumed`, not `inactive`), the history writes `putReadState` with its `thread_seq` through the outbox, at most once per newest message; Esc in the history writes it regardless of the scroll position. After "Mark as unread" the view does not mark read again on its own until a newer message arrives; Esc compares with the stored position only. The app's `putReadState` executor stores the answer, and `markThreadUnread` takes the stored version when it is sent, so an own `putReadState` ahead of it in the lane does not make its `If-Match` stale; a second edit of a message joins the first that is not confirmed (`Outbox.edit`). The focus of the gateway is the set of threads whose history shows (`VisibleThreads`), `idle` while the window is in the background. | Brief Design note 2; 04 14 (the server keeps the maximum, so a repeated write is harmless but not needed). |
| D-M4-20-2 | 2026-10-18 | Drafts: every change of the field updates the one `putThreadDraft` entry that has not been attempted (04 13.3: in place) or enqueues one, so one write is in flight and one waits with the newest text, without a timer; sending drops the waiting write (`clear_draft` deletes the draft on the server). The composer marks its draft as edited (`DraftEditingRegistry`, the `DraftEditing` port of M4-14) while its field has the focus and the person changed the text, and takes a stored draft of another device only otherwise, and only while no own draft write is unconfirmed (its answer after a send must not bring the sent text back); the base version is taken over only with the text. A composer that opens again takes the text of an unconfirmed draft write from the outbox. The app registers its own `putThreadDraft` executor, which hands the `DraftPutResult` to `CatchUpListener.applyDraftPutResult`, so `overwrote_newer` shows the notice. | Brief Design note 3; 04 14. |
| D-M4-20-3 | 2026-10-18 | Edit conflict (`412 version_conflict`): the failed entry shows "Compare", which opens both versions; "Keep mine" edits the failed entry (`Outbox.edit`: a new entry on the version of the kept `current`), "Take theirs" discards it. An edit of a deleted message (`410`) keeps its text with "Copy" and "Discard". No automatic merge. | Brief Design note 4; 04 13.3. |
| D-M4-20-4 | 2026-10-18 | Actions follow the server's answer, never grants: the level and capabilities of the stored `ChannelView` and the channel's `read_only` map to the actions by the table of 05 (`message.create`, `reaction.add`, `thread.create`, `thread.close`, `thread.reopen`: level `write` or `trigger`; `message.update`: write and author; `message.delete`: write and author, or `manage`; `pin.*` and `thread.move`: `manage`; `thread.title.update`: write and creator or `manage`; `message.revisions.read`: read, offered for edited messages). The server checks every write again. | Brief Design note 4; 15 1 principle 1. |
| D-M4-20-5 | 2026-10-18 | The header button "Pin" shows the session column with its section "Pinned"; it writes nothing, because no operation pins a thread (03 has pins of messages). The session column shows the pinned messages of the open thread from the local `pinned` flag of the messages (`MessagesDao.watchPinned`); opening the section reads `listPins` once and stores the pinned messages that are not stored yet (`getMessage`), so pins outside the loaded history show too. | Brief Design note 5; no contract change (P-11). |
| D-M4-20-6 | 2026-10-18 | D2: a thread of a text channel shows to the right of the channel's main history in the history column, and the session column is collapsed until the person shows it ("Column", "Pin"); the area keeps the main history it showed when a removal deletes the rows, so both columns close with "No access anymore"; a channel route of a text channel shows its main history; the main history shows "Thread: title, n replies" under a message that started a thread. | 01 5.1 D2. |
| D-M4-20-7 | 2026-10-18 | Fixes outside the app that the thread view needs: drift shares query streams of equal SQL, and the trigger statements of `watchThread`, `watchForChannel` and `watchContiguous` were all `SELECT 1`, so with a post list open the history did not show new messages or loaded ranges; each now has its own statement text. `CatchUpListener` gets `loadOlderMessages` and `loadMessageRange` (paging up and the jump), `DsDelivery.delayed` shows "Sending delayed", and `DsMarkdown` styles a mention token only for the handles of `mention_ids` (03 content format item 4). | Reproduced in a test of `local_store`; the brief's history needs paging and the delayed state. |
| D-M4-20-8 | 2026-10-18 | The context menu of a message opens on the raw right click (`Listener`), not through a gesture recognizer, because a recognizer of the content won the arena after the first menu and the menu no longer opened. | Reproduced in the integration test. |
| D-M4-20-9 | 2026-10-20 | Port to `main`: the ticket commits from `35d6a9c` to `9ba1a62` on top of M4-19, M4-25, M4-26, M4-27, M4-29, M4-37 and M3-13; the fake server registers the thread view handlers next to the admin, access, audit and host handlers, `world.dart` keeps the host import next to the thread view part file, the l10n catalogues keep the keys of `main` plus the keys of this ticket with regenerated localizations, the contract test declares `final world = h.server.world;` once (the admin part further down uses it), and the chapter reference of `thread/providers.dart` names `15-client-architecture.md`. | The admin part of M4-26 and the thread view part of this ticket each declared the same variable in one test body, which no longer compiled (`duplicate_definition`); the integration branch fixed it the same way. |

## M4-29 Administration: audit view

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-29-0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave42` at `c5a86a6`, branch `feat/M4-29-admin-audit` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The audit operations of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and `audit.read`, `audit.verify` of `actions.yaml` equal those of `origin/main`, so no contract change was brought in. The base keeps the German chapter files, so the A6 line changes in `docs/spec/01-produkt.md` (the brief names `01-product.md` of `origin/main`). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-29-1 | 2026-10-18 | The action filter offers the action IDs of the loaded entries as suggestions of a text field that also takes any typed ID; Enter applies the typed text, a picked suggestion applies at once. Every filter maps one to one to a query parameter of `listAuditEvents`; days are typed as `YYYY-MM-DD` in the person's time zone, "to" is the start of the day after. An action shows its translated label where `l10n` has one (the administration actions), else its ID; a resource shows its kind and ID, because `AuditEvent` carries no name. | Brief Design note 1: the catalogue is not shipped to the client as a list of its own. |
| D-M4-29-2 | 2026-10-18 | "Check chain" sends `verifyAuditChain` without a range while no filter is set (the server checks from the last checkpoint); with a filter it sends the `seq` span of the listed entries as `from_seq` and `to_seq`, because the request takes numbers, not times. | Brief Design note 2; `AuditVerifyRequest` has only `from_seq` and `to_seq`. |
| D-M4-29-3 | 2026-10-18 | "Export" writes the bytes of `exportAuditEvents` unchanged; "Export as CSV" converts the same stream line by line into the columns `occurred_at`, `actor_principal_id`, `action`, `decision`, `resource_kind`, `resource_id`, `deny_reason`, `correlation_id`, `seq`, `hash`, `prev_hash` (header with these field names, RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF), and a cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` gets a leading apostrophe. Both need the filter's time range. The save dialog is a port of the host (`AuditSaveTarget`, a `StreamConsumer` of the chosen file), because `admin_ui` also serves the console on the web and the sandboxed desktop app may write only to a file the person picked in the platform's panel; a host without it shows no export buttons. The desktop app wires no panel in this ticket (its files are outside the brief's scope), so the export is reachable once a host passes one. A stream that breaks off says that the file is incomplete. | Brief Design note 3; ADR 0021 (offline verification needs the hash links of the NDJSON). |
| D-M4-29-4 | 2026-10-18 | A streamed response hides its problem body from the `StepUpInterceptor` (Dio passes the raw body), so `exportAudit` first reads one entry of the same range through `listAuditEvents` (same action `audit.read`, same purpose text): the step-up and a `403` arrive on a JSON answer, and the stream follows within `auth.step_up_max_age`. A refused stream itself is read into an `ApiProblem` too. | Found by the fake-server test: the bytes request answered a bare `401` without the step-up. |
| D-M4-29-5 | 2026-10-18 | `AdminRepository` includes `AuditRepository` (`ApiAdminRepository` delegates to `ApiAuditRepository`), so the shell hands one repository to every screen and the app needs no change; the audit screens use only the audit calls and `allPeople` for the person picker. The audit section of the shell gets the body's height instead of the scroll view, so its list is virtualized. | Brief Scope "Registration of the Audit entry in the admin shell"; fixed rule 2 (the audit code stays in `lib/src/audit/`). |
| D-M4-29-6 | 2026-10-18 | Fixes after the code review: a failed or cancelled load keeps the filter bar (only a `403` or `404` shows the server's reason alone), so a filter the server rejects (`400` for a resource ID that is no UUID) can be changed; a failed "Load more" keeps the loaded entries and retries the same page; a new filter clears the result of the last chain check. | Code review finding: the view lost its filters while the filter still applied to the list, the chain check and the export. |
| D-M4-29-7 | 2026-10-20 | Port onto main from the integration squash of `int/m1-wave45`: the seven own commits of the ticket on top of the M4-27 port (the ticket branch starts at the wave 42 head, which holds M4-26). ## M7-11 API breaking change check

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-11-0 | 2026-10-16 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The rows stand in a section per ticket, as for the other ported tickets. No contract change of `main` is needed: the brief relies on 06 13 and 15. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-11-1 | 2026-10-15 | Tool (Design note 1): G6 is the own comparison `tools/api-checks/breaking.py` over the parsed YAML (PyYAML, already locked), not oasdiff; nothing new is pinned. Evidence: oasdiff 1.33.0 (`darwin_all` release asset) parsed `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and reported "No changes detected" against itself, but on mutated copies it reported no breaking change for a removed property of `TypingStartedPayload` (an event schema no path references) and for a changed `x-authz-action` of `GET /instance/discovery`, and it reported a removed query parameter `cursor` only as a warning (`request-parameter-removed`); it did report a removed `201` as an error. A wrapper would have needed its own comparison for the events, the action and the pagination kind anyway. | 06 13 item 7 leaves the tool open; Design note 1 asks for the own comparison when oasdiff misses a row; the rows are structural and few. |
| D-M7-11-2 | 2026-10-15 | Base (requirement 12): the newest stable tag `server/vX.Y.Z` (SemVer precedence) among the tags merged into `HEAD`, read with `git show <tag>:docs/api/openapi.yaml` (and `asyncapi.yaml` for directions when present); any pre-release tag, not only `-beta.N`, is the base only when no stable tag is merged, the newest by SemVer precedence; no tag gives the notice `no published version` and exit 0; a tag without `docs/api/openapi.yaml` is an error. Only merged tags count, like `last_stable_tag` of `tools/ci/release_prep.py`, so a maintenance branch compares with its own line; CI checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, so the tags are there. The gate runs when `server` is selected; a change under `docs/api/` selects every gate component. | Brief requirement 12 and Design note 3; the API document ships with the server (`RELEASE_EXTRA_PATHS`). |
| D-M7-11-3 | 2026-10-15 | Major version exception (requirement 14): the gate accepts the findings, printed as `accepted`, only when the major of `info.version` of the working tree is higher than that of the base, also from `0.x` to `1.0.0`; a minor step under `0.x` does not accept a break, and an `info.version` that is not SemVer is an error. Rules of the comparison that the 06 table leaves open: a schema's direction comes from paths, `webhooks` (reversed) and `asyncapi.yaml`, and a schema without one counts in both directions; a response property that is no longer required counts as row 1, a changed `default` as row 3 and, for a parameter named `sort`, also as row 6; a changed `operationId` is a rename (row 1); the pagination kind comes from the query parameters `cursor`, `before_seq`, `after_seq`, `around_seq` and an integer `after` (a time filter `after` is none); the schema of a response header is compared like a field; a removed response status is row 5, a new status passes; a changed status of a problem code or `x-status` of a response component is row 5, a removed problem code row 1. `asyncapi.yaml` changes of channels, operations and messages are not covered (brief Out of scope). | 06 13 items 4 and 5; requirement 14 says nothing else lowers the gate; an unknown direction must not hide a new required request field. |

## M4-05 Icons in the design system

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-05-0 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The lock keeps `vector_graphics_compiler` 1.3.1, the version the ticket measured and generated the assets with; `http`, which `vector_graphics` brings, already has its license override from M4-06, whose reason now names both users. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M4-05-1 | 2026-10-15 | The icons ship as `vector_graphics_compiler` assets (`assets/icons/<weight>/<glyph>.vec`), not as an icon font. Measured on 128 glyphs at 16, 20 and 24 px and ratios 1 and 2 against the `flutter_svg` render: vectors 0 % mean alpha difference everywhere, the font 2.97 % mean and up to 47.1 % in one pixel at 16 px x1 (table in docs/design/icons.md "Integration (M4-05)"); 74,608 bytes for the vectors against 34,564 for the font. | Design note 1: the clearly sharper candidate at 16 px wins, the size only decides a tie. `vector_graphics` is maintained by the Flutter team (BSD-3-Clause). |
| D-M4-05-2 | 2026-10-15 | The icon assets, their lookup file, LICENSE and MANIFEST.json are generated by the token tool (`tokens.py icons`, part of `build`); `check` rebuilds them in memory and compares byte for byte, an extra file under `assets/icons/` is stale too. The compiler runs from the workspace lock with the mask, clip and overdraw optimizers off. | docs/design/icons.md names `icon.tokens.json` as the single source; the optimizers need the native path-ops library of the host, and Phosphor glyphs have no masks or clips, so turning them off keeps the bytes the same on every host. |
| D-M4-05-3 | 2026-10-15 | The assets hold every mapped glyph in its mapped weight and in `fill`, and every space icon in `regular`; `tokens.py vendor` copies exactly these SVGs and no longer requires `--sans` and `--mono` (without them `vendor/fonts/` stays as it is). | Design note 3: the golden and `DsIcon(filled: true)` need the active form of every icon; a new icon should need only the Phosphor package. |
| D-M4-05-4 | 2026-10-15 | A spinning glyph rotates once per `motion.duration.pulse`, and only while the ambient `DsMotion` has a positive pulse, which only the full context has (the reduced one is zero); otherwise no `AnimationController` exists. | No dedicated token exists, and adding one would change the token files of M0-01's gate (design note 4). The pulse test also holds during a theme animation, when `AnimatedTheme` lerps a new `DsMotion` instance that is not identical to `DsMotion.full`. |
| D-M4-05-5 | 2026-10-15 | `DsIcon` throws an `ArgumentError` without a non-empty `label` and without `decorative: true` in debug and in release (no separate assert); an empty label counts as none. `DsIconSizeRole` is a new enum of this ticket (the brief names it, the generated code has only `DsIconSize`). `testing.dart` gains `dsPumpIcons`, so `flutter_test` (SDK) becomes a regular dependency like `alchemist`, and `DsIconPlaceholder` is public so the helper can wait for it. The lookup class is `DsIconAssets` with `lookup(glyph, {filled})` and `space(name)`, returning the asset path. | One rule in every build mode; an empty label gives no screen reader text. Vector assets load asynchronously, and every member with icons in a golden needs the same wait. |
| D-M4-05-6 | 2026-10-17 | The pre-push hook runs `make check` as `env -u GIT_DIR -u GIT_WORK_TREE -u GIT_INDEX_FILE -u GIT_COMMON_DIR -u GIT_PREFIX make check`; `tools/hooks/tests/test_pre_push_env.py` runs the hook's prefix with the five variables set and holds that none reaches the command. The per-tool removals (D-M2-38-22, `codegen_check.py`, `dart_api_docs.py`) stay. | Fourth tool with the same failure: `tokens.py` runs `dart run vector_graphics_compiler`, and under the hook the Dart SDK read its version from this repository's GIT_DIR and refused the workspace ("requires the Flutter SDK"), so the token gate failed in every pre-push run while it passed in CI. One rule at the hook covers every gate instead of each tool. |

Enum values carry the codes of 03 and an `unknown` member (`DsCategory` falls back to `granite`); `fromCode` never throws. Callbacks are plain functions; no component performs I/O or imports a provider. | Brief note 1: testable with literal data, usable by the console and the golden harness; a newer server may send a new code (06 13). |
| D-M4-07-2 | 2026-10-15 | `DsMarkdown` parses with package `markdown` (GitHub flavored without the inline HTML syntaxes) and builds its own widgets for the subset of the brief. HTML renders as literal text; an image renders as the text "Image: <alt> (<domain>)" and never loads; links are active only for `https`, `http`, `mailto` and the internal paths `/t/` and `/c/`, show their domain (or the internal target) on hover and focus and reach `onOpenLink` only on activation; every other scheme renders as plain text; headings are at most `type.heading`; code blocks scroll horizontally and copy exactly the code. `markdown` 7.3.1 gets a checked `PackageOverrides` entry (BSD-3-Clause, from its LICENSE file) in `osv-scanner.toml`, because deps.dev knows no license for it, as D-M4-04-5 does for alchemist. Block quotes and lists nest as structure up to `DsMarkdown.maxNesting` (8) levels; a deeper element renders as its plain text, because the server accepts any nesting and thousands of levels froze the interface (red team of M4-07). | Brief note 2: a widget package would decide link and image handling and could load resources (SEC-OUT-1, SEC-OUT-2). |
| D-M4-07-3 | 2026-10-15 | `DsMessageComponents` draws a framed header with `security.step-up` built only from `DsComponentHeader` (agent, run, required capability, approval note), then a divider labeled "Text from <agent>", then the agent text as Markdown, then the components. `DsMessage` therefore does not draw the content of a `kind = card` message that has components. After the first activation the widget ignores every further one until the caller reports a new state of the card (another `state`, `pendingCustomId`, `errorText` or message); an equal view model rebuilt by the parent keeps the lock, and `pendingCustomId` from the caller locks the same way. For screen readers the group is labeled by the server header and the header is one node with the plain names; the agent's card title is read after the divider, and the message label of a card names only author and time. `onActivate` gets null for a button, the list of chosen values for a select and the field map for a form. Only Enter or Space on the focused button activates (the default `ActivateIntent`); no other key or shortcut does. | Brief note 5, SEC-OUT-4 and SEC-T14 at component level; 01 6.5 (a double click creates nothing twice); R-PR-04. |
| D-M4-07-4 | 2026-10-15 | `LiveAnnouncer` ignores the first observation, announces a change of the state text or a new question ID, never a patch of steps or numbers, and merges announcements closer than `minInterval` (`a11y.live_announce_min_interval`) into the latest candidate, which `flush` sends once the interval has passed; a state candidate equal to the last sent text is dropped, a new question never is (all questions of an agent read the same). The live box arms the flush timer again when it fires early. The live box sends through `SemanticsService.announce` with polite assertiveness, in all three forms: the collapsed and the mobile line also take `announceMinInterval`, so the end of a run and questions on a phone are announced. | Brief note 4, 01 8 and components.md 2.4. |

| D-M4-07-7 | 2026-10-15 | The message label stays "<Name>, <Time>, <Text>" with the text joined by spaces between blocks, list items and table cells, and the content keeps its own semantics below it, so links (own nodes with a tap action) and the copy button stay reachable; a screen reader may read the text twice. A continuation message shows the trigger state at the end of its row. Trigger and run reasons cover the acceptance reasons of 09 3.5 and `user_cancelled` without a name. | Code review of M4-07: links and the copy button were unreachable; 01 6.3 wants the state at every triggering message. |
| D-M4-07-8 | 2026-10-15 | The composer leaves Enter, Tab, the arrows and Escape to the input method while a composition is active. The generated token files stay byte for byte as `tokens.py build` writes them; `dart format` runs only on hand-written folders. | Red team of M4-07: Enter during an IME composition sent half-typed text; a formatted copy made `make tokens` red. |

Two opacity values without a token are named constants with a reason: the icon tile of a space (0.16) and the lowest point of the run dot pulse (0.4). | components.md 7 asks for a tree; the semantics that Flutter offers are the expanded state. The token set has no tint or pulse opacity role. |
| D-M4-07-9 | 2026-10-17 | The ticket was ported from its integration base onto main with only its own commits (the integration fixes of the wave concern other tickets); the English docs of main win, so the new paragraphs in `docs/design/components.md` sit under the English headings of main. The nine light goldens of badges, composer and active live box are rerendered on main, macOS and CI variant. | Main carries its own value of `color.warning.subtle` (`palette.amber.100`), light `#FEE6D4` instead of `#FEF3EA` on the integration base; the warning chips of these sheets show it, nothing else changed in the images. |

The contrast guideline measures the most frequent colors of the rendered glyphs: on Linux a blended edge shade of the link outnumbered `color.text.link` and measured 4.18 although the pair has 5.9 and passes the token gate, which checks every contrast pair on every host. |
